# Tests

Generated by `python -m tools.testspage` from the tests' docstrings and the designs' headings; do not edit it by hand.
Each test file begins with a docstring naming the design sections it verifies (design 0001 §8.5).

**243 of 418 design sections have tests.** A section with subsections is covered through them.

## Test files

### `tests/cli/test_autostart.py`

Design 0021 §11: Clank's service, and Docker Desktop through it, start when the owner signs in to Windows, so the PC is ready for remote work after a restart. `clank autostart on` writes the entry Windows runs at sign-in (the current user's Run key, `Clank`): Python's windowless program running `clank autostart run`, with CLANK_HOME when it is set; `off` removes it; `status` says whether it is there and what it runs. `run` starts the service in the background when it does not run, then asks it to start Docker, writing what it did to logs/autostart.log. On another system, `on` says it is not supported there yet.

- on writes the sign in entry off removes it and status says which
- on another system it says it is not supported yet
- run starts the service when it does not run then asks it to start docker

### `tests/cli/test_cloud_commands.py`

Design 0016 §9, §10, §11 and §16: the cloud's other commands, each a client of the service printing its words. `clank workspace allow FOLDER --cloud | --local` asks first (the service's words for what changes) unless `--yes`; `clank mcp local NAME [--off]` marks a server local only or not; `clank mcp add` asks whether a new server is local only once a provider is agreed, unless `--local` or `--cloud-ok` says; `clank cost` prints today and this month against the limits, a pause, each session's and each flow's cost; `clank cost limit` sends the limits as amounts, and one that is not an amount is refused, saying what to type.

- allow cloud asks first in the services words
- allow local with yes asks nothing
- mcp local marks a server and off unmarks it
- mcp add asks once a provider is agreed unless told
- cost prints today and this month against the limits and a pause
- cost by session and by flow
- cost limit sends the limits as typed and a refusal says why

### `tests/cli/test_commands.py`

Design 0002 §2.15: every action is also a command, a client of the API: `clank events`, `clank questions` (and answering one), `clank operations`, `clank records`, each as readable text or, with --json, JSON; with no service running, a command says how to start it.

- events prints the newest events oldest first and json lines for scripts
- events follow ends saying so when the service stops
- questions lists what waits and an answer by key records you through the cli
- operations lists the newest operations
- records lists the kinds and shows any record
- a command without the service says how to start it
- arguments reach the service as typed with no home folder or wildcard expanded

### `tests/cli/test_dev_command.py`

Design 0016 §16 (decision 0149): `clank dev` says whether dev mode is on in the service's words, and `clank dev on` or `off` turns it so, recorded as the command's; `--json` prints the service's answer.

- dev says whether it is on and on or off turns it so

### `tests/cli/test_docker_commands.py`

Design 0002 §2.15 and §3.2: `clank docker status` prints Docker's state, its versions and each running container with its owner, as readable text and, with --json, as JSON; `clank docker start` and `clank docker stop` follow their operation step by step; a stop that needs an answer asks at the terminal (no leaves Docker running), `--yes` answers it as you (cli), and an answer given elsewhere while the prompt waits is reported with who gave it.

- docker status prints the state and each running container with its owner
- docker status of a stopped docker is its state alone with no empty line
- docker start prints each step and the end
- docker stop asks at the terminal and no leaves docker running
- docker stop yes answers the question as you through the cli
- an answer given elsewhere while the prompt waits is reported with who gave it

### `tests/cli/test_docker_group.py`

Design 0028 §5 (decision 0150): on Linux, a `clank` command whose account the system lists in the docker group, but whose process lacks it (joined since the last login), runs itself again through `sg docker` with the same arguments, once (`CLANK_DOCKER_GROUP`), so it and the service it starts may use Docker at once; with the group already, without `sg`, or on Windows, it runs as it is. The command's own entry takes the group first; a test's call of the command never does.

- a command joined since the last login runs again through sg with its arguments
- otherwise it runs as it is
- the commands entry takes the group before the command

### `tests/cli/test_endpoint_commands.py`

Design 0016 §4 and §16: `clank endpoint`, the cloud provider's commands, each a client of the service saying what the service says. `add` asks the agreement (the guide's words), or takes `--agree` from a script; `key` reads the key without echo, or one line of standard input with `--stdin` (refused at a terminal, which would echo it), sends it once and prints only its last four, and no option of any command takes a key; a refused key says why and exits 1; `list`, `show` and `models` print the service's views; `preset` writes a cloud preset and tests it; `check` prints each item; `remove` in use names what uses it.

- add asks the agreement in the services words and n adds nothing
- add with agree asks nothing
- key with stdin sends the line and prints only its last four
- key asked without echo never prints it
- no option of any command takes a key
- a refused key says why and exits one
- list show and models print the services views
- preset writes a cloud preset and tests it
- check prints each item
- remove in use names what uses it
- stdin at a terminal is refused since the terminal would echo the key
- a cloud preset says its provider where an engine would be

### `tests/cli/test_flow_commands.py`

Design 0013 §3, §5, §6, §7, §8, §9, §11 and §16: the `clank flow` commands (list, show, check, prepare, run, pause, stop, diff, proposals, accept, reject, next and orchestrator), clients of the API, for scripts and as the Flow view's backup: `orchestrator` says a flow's orchestrator's settings and turns it on or off in the flow's file; `list` shows each flow with its workspace, title and boxes, and where flows are kept and Clank's template when there are none; `show` names each box with its type, title, files and what comes after it, the workspace being the current folder's, and the switches of the main slot a run makes (each preset with how long its last whole switch took, else its engine's last start), `check` how many; `check` says a good flow keeps every rule, and prints each problem of a broken one with its file, line and field, ending with a failure; `run`, `pause` and `stop` say what they did and where the run is, each box with its state; `diff` compares two tries of a box, saying how many lines changed, then the diff; `proposals` lists the run's proposals with their states (`--full`: each whole), `accept` (with `--prompt`, an edit first) and `reject` decide them by their ids or all, saying what they did; `prepare` lists each box's first message size against its model's window, saying how each was counted, or shows one box's message whole; `next` shows the flow made after this one, or why there is none, and makes (`--make`) or runs (`--run`) it; `--json` prints what the API answers.

- orchestrator says its settings and turns it on and off in the flows file
- list shows each flow with its workspace title and boxes
- list says where flows are kept and where the template is when there are none
- show names each box with its type title files and what comes after it
- show and check say the switches a run makes before it runs
- show says how long a presets last whole switch took when one is kept
- check of a good flow says it keeps every rule
- check warns of a file a box sees that nothing gives it and does not fail
- check of a broken file prints each problem with its file line and field and fails
- json prints what the api answers
- a flow named outside a workspaces folder needs its workspace
- run pause and stop say what they did and where the run is
- diff compares two tries of a box saying how many lines changed then the diff
- proposals lists each proposal with its state and full shows each whole
- accept with a prompt edits it first and reject and accept say what they did
- prepare lists each boxs first message size against its window or shows one whole
- next shows the next flow or why there is none and makes or runs it
- flow new opens opencode here and says the flow was built and how to look and run it
- flow new says when the flow was not built and why its session failed
- flow new in vs code opens clanks vs code window and in vs codes terminal unasked
- over ssh flow new opens in this terminal whatever asked and says why
- flow attach runs opencode here on the open planning and says what the one window rule closed
- flow attach runs the command of the services own answer
- flow attach with no planning open says how to open one
- flow attach home names clanks home as a pane of windows terminal gives it

### `tests/cli/test_latex_commands.py`

Design 0020 §3: `clank latex setup [FOLDER]` gives a folder's workspace the TeX layer: the workspace is opened (made the first time), the layer added to its record, its image built on the sandbox image (asked first unless `--yes`). A container already running the image before keeps running it, and the command says how to move it; with `--restart` it is stopped and opened again on the new image. `clank latex build FILE` builds the document a `.tex` file belongs to in its workspace's container (LaTeX Workshop's recipe in Clank's window), says where its PDF is and what its log warns of, and fails, saying the error, when it did not build.

- setup opens the workspace adds the tex layer and builds its image
- a running container keeps its image until restart moves it
- build builds the document in the container and says what its log says

### `tests/cli/test_mcp_commands.py`

Design 0002 §2.15 and the M8.0 plan, task 2: `clank mcp` adds MCP servers the way other harnesses do (`claude mcp add`, `codex mcp add`): the server's own command after `--`, its options never taken for Clank's; its first word after the name without `--`; a URL with `--transport http` (or alone); `-e` and `-H` given as secrets, never printed; `--scope project` writing the current folder's `.mcp.json`; `add-json` taking an entry as that file holds one. A command that starts with a dash (the owner's own first try, `clank mcp add myproject --myproject-mcp`) is refused, showing the form with `--`. `list`, `get` (secrets by name and last four characters), `remove` and `check` (following its operation) do the rest.

- a server clank starts is added with its command after the double dash
- without the double dash the first word is the command and unknown options go with it
- a command starting with a dash is refused showing the double dash
- a server at a url is added with its headers as secrets
- a projects server goes into the folders mcp json
- add json list get and remove
- check follows its operation and lists the tools

### `tests/cli/test_model_add.py`

Design 0016 §14 and §16: `clank model add` is the console's Add a model in the terminal, in its order and the service's words: the whole pass with its answers on standard input writes the provider's record once agreed, keeps the key (read without echo, never printed), writes the preset from the model picked with the settings as the service fills them, and loads it into the empty main slot, saying its answer and cost; a refused key is said and asked again, and so is a mistyped limit, the preset already written then taken as it is, as a second pass takes it; Local lists the main slot's presets and says what to do when a step cannot be done from here. Design 0028 §4: `--cloud` and `--local` skip the choice, for `clank setup`'s model step, which asks it once.

- the whole pass on standard input agrees keeps the key writes the preset and loads it
- cloud and local skip the choice
- a refused key is said and asked again
- with a local model running it tests then asks and no keeps it
- local lists the presets and says what to do when a step cannot be done
- a mistyped limit is asked again and the preset already written is taken
- a rerun after a preset was written takes it and goes on

### `tests/cli/test_output.py`

Design 0002 §2.15: the `clank` command writes UTF-8 on every platform, so what it prints reads the same in a terminal, a pipe or a file (on Windows, Python would otherwise write a pipe in the system's code page).

- the command writes utf8 to a pipe

### `tests/cli/test_paint.py`

Design 0028 §4: color in the terminal, for the commands that print for people. Each kind of word has its color and the words never depend on it: a checklist in color is, its color taken away, the checklist without; commands are bold, even across a line's end; `NO_COLOR` drops every color.

- a checklist in color is the checklist without its color taken away
- commands are bold even across a lines end and no color drops every color

### `tests/cli/test_preset_change_command.py`

Design 0016 §16 and §7 (revised after approval 2026-10-29, the owner's request): `clank preset change PRESET [--output N] [--context N] [--reasoning R] [--json]` changes a cloud preset's settings through the service, by its id or its title, and prints the service's words: what changed, where it applies, and, while the main slot runs it, that OpenCode windows open now keep the limits they started with until they start again; `--json` prints the service's answer. A refusal is the service's own words and exits 1, a local preset's saying where its options change; with no setting to change, it says what to give.

- change raises the most output and prints the services words
- change with json prints the services answer
- a refusal is the services words and exits 1
- with nothing to change it says what to give

### `tests/cli/test_service_commands.py`

Design 0002 §2.15: `clank status` says whether the service runs and what waits for the user, and how to start it when it does not; `clank stop` stops it; `clank serve --background` says when a service already runs and starts none, and a start in the background that fails says only what its own service said (the owner's report, 2026-10-18: the error quoted another service's log lines), and it runs from Clank's home, never the current folder.

- status says how to start the service when it does not run
- status shows the running service and stop stops it
- serve in the background says when a service already runs and starts no second one
- a start in the background runs from clanks home never the current folder
- a start in the background that fails says only what its own service said
- status names the clients connected in words
- an api time is said in this computers time with the day when it is not today

### `tests/cli/test_setup_command.py`

Design 0028 §4 and design 0002 §2.15: `clank setup --check` prints the checklist, each item with its state and what to do about it, and changes nothing; it exits 0 when everything required is done, else 1. It works with or without the service: with it, the checklist is the service's (`GET /setup`); without it, Docker is read here through the platform, as the doctor reads it, and the model and the folder are said to be told once the service runs. The folder it was started in is checked as a workspace's would be. `--json` prints the checklist as the API gives it.

- without the service it reads docker itself and says what only the service can tell
- check changes nothing
- with the service the checklist is the services
- it exits 0 when everything required is done
- json prints the checklist as the api gives it
- the folder it was started in is checked as a workspaces would be
- at eighty columns what an item is now goes under its title and every line fits

### `tests/cli/test_setup_fixes.py`

Design 0028 §4 and §5: `clank setup`, without `--check`, asks about each item that is not done, in order, showing its fix and its exact command first, and does it on a yes; nothing runs without its yes, and without a terminal to answer in the answer is no. A command that needs administrator rights (`sudo systemctl start docker`, `sudo systemctl enable docker`, `sudo usermod -aG docker <account>`, the installer's Docker step) runs attached to the terminal, so sudo asks for the password itself and nothing it says is captured; after joining the docker group it runs itself again with the group where `sg` is installed (decision 0150), else says to log out and in once and stops there; a service that started before the account joined is stopped and started again, with the group. Docker Desktop is started through its own start, by the service when it runs, else here. Clank's service is started on a yes, the folder `clank setup` was started in is opened as a workspace on a yes, and the model's step asks once: in the cloud, on this computer's GPU, or both, the terminal handed to Add a model's command for each, the cloud first. The sandbox image is built by the service in the background on a yes while the setup goes on, and the folder opens once the build ended (S4). `--yes` does every fix that needs no administrator rights without asking and lists the others with their commands. A command the checklist does not build is never run, whoever gave it: it is shown, for the person to run if they trust it.

- nothing runs without its yes
- without a terminal to answer in the answer is no
- an administrator command runs attached to the terminal on a yes
- the terminal runner inherits the terminal so sudo asks itself and nothing is captured
- after joining the docker group it says to log out and in once and stops there
- with sg after joining the docker group the setup runs again with it
- with sg a service started before the account joined is stopped and started again
- what is the persons to do is said and listed again at the end
- a command that fails says so and the setup goes on
- docker desktop stopped is started through its own start without the service
- with the service docker desktop is started by the services operation
- clanks service is started on a yes
- the folder it was started in is opened as a workspace on a yes
- the models step asks once cloud local or both and hands the terminal to add a model
- yes does every fix without administrator rights and lists the others
- yes starts docker desktop through its own start
- the sandbox image is built in the background and the folder waits for it
- yes builds the sandbox image too
- a command the checklist does not build is never run and is shown for the person

### `tests/cli/test_update.py`

Design 0026 §1 and §2 (decision 0145): what is installed, read from uv's record of the tool (a release from GitHub at a tag, a clone installed editable, another way); what is newer (the latest release, or the commits a clone's branch is behind, with whether its tree has changes of its own); and what updating does: a clone with changes of its own is refused, a clone whose pull touches no dependency pulls and starts the service again, a release (or a clone whose dependencies changed) is installed by a helper that runs after `clank update` ended, written where the owner can read it; a dry run says each step and changes nothing; no answer from GitHub is said, never taken for "up to date".

- how clank was installed is read from uvs record of the tool
- a releases newer version comes from githubs latest release and no answer is said
- a clone says how far its branch is behind and whether its tree has changes of its own
- updating a clone with changes of its own is refused and one without pulls and restarts
- a release is installed by a helper after clank update ends and a dry run changes nothing
- check says what is installed what is newer and what it contacted

### `tests/cli/test_work_cloud.py`

Design 0016 §9, §14 and §16, decision 0112: `clank work` with no usable model says so and how to add one; with a cloud model in the main slot and the workspace not answered, it asks the workspace's question in the terminal, in the service's words, before OpenCode starts, answered through the question's own route: `n` keeps the workspace on this machine and stops before OpenCode, saying how to allow it; `y` allows it and goes on.

- with no usable model it says so and how to add one
- n keeps the workspace local and stops before opencode
- y allows the workspace and goes on

### `tests/components/agents/test_agent_files.py`

Design 0018 §7.5: each time Clank starts an agent in a workspace (`prepare`, and `configure` for one started without a terminal), it writes its current MCP server (`phone_mcp.py` and `find_figures.py`) and its instructions into the agent's home in the container (`/home/agent/.clank/`), and OpenCode's configuration runs that server and reads those instructions: the agent's tools are this Clank's, whatever image its container was made from. The instructions say how a file is opened for the user (`show_files`; there is no `code` command in the container).

- clanks files for the agent are its mcp server and its instructions as this clank has them
- the agent asks one question with options from the folder when a request could mean different things
- starting an agent writes them and its configuration runs and reads them

### `tests/components/agents/test_agent_jobs.py`

Design 0021 §6: the agent the owner talks to starts a long computation as a job. A job is numbered in its workspace, its folder `.clank/jobs/agent/<n>/` in the folder, its title, command, minutes and the session that started it kept in its `job.json` beside the runner's files; it runs in the container through the runner. A job is running once its process id is written, and ended once its exit is; one is listed, or read whole with its log's last lines and the files it wrote, and stopped. The watcher ends a job past its minutes, and when one ends records `agents.job_ended` once and tells the session that started it, in a message saying how it ended, its log's end and the files it wrote. The agent reaches them through the gateway: start, status and stop. The readout carries the jobs running in the folder's workspace, and `clank jobs` lists, shows and stops them.

- a job is numbered its files kept in the folder and started in the container
- a job is running once its process is written and ended once its exit is
- an ended job is recorded once and the session that started it told
- a job past its minutes is ended and one is stopped when asked
- the routes start follow and stop a job and list every workspaces
- the readout carries the jobs running in the folders workspace
- clank jobs lists shows and stops them

### `tests/components/agents/test_agent_servers.py`

Design 0004 §10: the MCP servers a workspace's agent gets, and the threads it starts. Your own servers are given to every workspace's agent unless held back from one; a folder's own (its `.mcp.json`) are offered, never trusted: given once allowed for that workspace, and not given again after their entry changes until allowed again. A server reached by address is not given (the container reaches nothing but the model). The agent's configuration carries the servers it gets, each a local command run in the container with its environment (secrets included); the agent runs with as many threads as its container's CPUs. A workspace's server is checked where it runs: in the workspace's container, through `docker exec` without a terminal. Design 0009 §3: the planner gets the tools a server's last check marks read-only. Design 0013 §15: it gets `flow_draft` too, since the planning session that drafts a flow is the planner's; and design 0013 §16: `flow_decide`, since an orchestrator's checkpoint is a Plan-mode session.

- your servers are given and the folders only once allowed
- an agent started without a terminal gets its configuration written first
- the planner gets the tools a servers last check marks read only and none before a check
- a folders server changed since it was allowed is not given until allowed again
- the agent starts as many threads as its container has cpus
- a server is checked in the workspaces container through docker exec without a terminal
- clank mcp allow and hold give this folders agent a server or hold it back
- a workspace terminal free command has no terminal
- clanks own server is shown as opencode runs it

### `tests/components/agents/test_annotations.py`

Design 0008 §5: a figure the owner marked up goes to the workspace's agent, run against a fake OpenCode server: each mark in the figure's own terms (from the geometry Matplotlib's save left in the folder), the owner's words, and the marked-up copy as an image when the agent's model reads images, else what the vision model read in it (design 0008 §7); it is kept in the folder's `.clank/annotations/` (out of git) and recorded. It goes to the session most recently active; nothing is sent while no agent runs, for a figure outside the folder, or with neither a mark nor a word.

- a marked up figure reaches the agent with its marks in words and the copy attached
- an annotation is refused with the reason when it cannot be sent

### `tests/components/agents/test_artifact_tool.py`

Design 0021 §5: Clank's own MCP server gives the agent `make_artifact(name, title, kind, files, note)`: a request to the gateway's `POST /agents/artifacts` naming the workspace the server serves, the files as the model gave them (one given as a string is a list of one). Its description says when to make one, that its page uses only its own files and Clank's libraries (named `clank-lib/...`), and how a new version is made; the answer is the service's words, a refusal the tool's error in its words.

- make artifact is listed and says what a page may use
- make artifact asks the gateway naming its workspace and answers the services words

### `tests/components/agents/test_artifacts.py`

Design 0021 §5: artifacts, pages the agent makes for the owner. Making one copies the files the agent named into the artifact's next version (`.clank/artifacts/<name>/v<n>/`), keeping their layout from their common folder, its entry page first; `artifact.json` keeps its title, kind and every version (when, its entry, its files, its note, the session that made it); `agents.artifact_made` is recorded. A Markdown artifact gets Clank's page that renders it with the kept libraries, math included, and so does an SVG one. A name, a kind, a file not the folder's, too many or too large files, an entry page that names the network, or an HTML page that would show broken (a file it reads missing from where it looks, math in dollar signs auto-render would leave as text), are refused, saying why. The owner's comment on a version goes to the session that made it, in one message naming the artifact and its version (through a server that runs in the workspace when the service has not heard from that session since it started), and `agents.artifact_commented` is recorded.

- making one copies its files as a version keeping their layout and records it
- a markdown or svg artifact gets clanks page that renders it
- what cannot be an artifact is refused saying why
- a page that names the network is refused
- a page that reads a file missing from where it looks is refused naming the path that works
- math in dollar signs that auto render would leave as text is refused with the call that draws it
- the owners comment goes to the session that made that version
- a comment after a restart goes to that session through a server that runs in the workspace
- the routes make list and read artifacts and take the owners comment
- an export is one file with its libraries and data inside that needs nothing else
- clank artifact lists a folders artifacts and exports the newest version

### `tests/components/agents/test_board_export.py`

Design 0022 §7: a frame, or a selection, exports as one figure. What is exported is what a paper's figure shows: the figures and the LaTeX in the frame (or chosen), and the arrows between them; the working layer (notes, slots, pins, pen strokes, highlights, the frame itself) is left out. Each figure's SVG is nested in the export with ids of its own, so it stays vector and two figures never share a clip path; a picture is embedded as it is; the export is cropped to what it shows, with a small margin. A caption's words are written for TeX, its math kept.

- a frame exports its figures latex and arrows and leaves the working layer out
- a figures svg is nested with ids of its own and nothing that runs
- a picture is embedded as it is
- the export is cropped to what it shows each part placed in its box
- a captions words are written for tex and its math kept

### `tests/components/agents/test_board_export_service.py`

Design 0022 §7: a frame exports as a file of the folder, beside its board (named after the frame) or where the owner or the agent says, as PDF or PNG (made with librsvg in the folder's container, from the export's SVG) or SVG. The LaTeX and the captions are typeset by TeX there, so the figure's words are the paper's. A frame can be kept exported: the board then remembers the file. A container without the converter says how to add it; a part that does not build is left out and said.

- a frame exports as a pdf beside its board with its latex typeset by tex
- an export can be png or svg and kept and without the converter says how to add it
- a kept export is made again when the agent changes what its frame shows

### `tests/components/agents/test_board_latex.py`

Design 0022 §6: a LaTeX item in compiled mode (TikZ, pgfplots, a table) is built in the folder's container with the TeX layer: a `standalone` document with the board's preamble, pdfLaTeX, then `pdftocairo -svg`; the SVG's glyphs are flattened into paths (no ids left to collide), and it is kept by its source's and preamble's hash in `.clank/boards/<board>/latex/`, so the same source is built once. A build that fails says LaTeX's error line; a container without TeX says how to add it; a math item, or an item that is not on the board, is refused.

- a snippet is a standalone document with the boards preamble
- cairos svg is flattened glyphs into paths with ids of its own
- a compiled item is built once in the container and kept by its source
- a failed build says latexs error and no tex says how to add it
- the owners window asks the service for a drawing
- the agent hears at once whether its compiled latex builds and a drawing is placed at its size

### `tests/components/agents/test_board_place.py`

Design 0022 §4: the agent places things on a board by where, never by pixels. `right_of`, `left_of`, `below` and `above` an item put the new one beside it, aligned with it (tops for beside, left edges for under and over), two grid steps away, snapped to the grid; one that would overlap another item moves on (down beside, right under) until it overlaps none. A slot is taken: the new item fills it, its aspect kept, centered in it. `in` a frame is the first free place inside it, below its name, and a frame too full for it grows downward. `{x, y}` is that place, snapped; no `at` is a new row under everything, at the board's left edge. An item that is not on the board is refused, saying so.

- beside and under an item aligned with it two grid steps away
- a place taken moves on until it overlaps nothing
- a slot is filled with the aspect kept centered and taken
- inside a frame the first free place below its name and the frame grows when full
- a place named is snapped and none named is a new row under everything
- an item not on the board is refused
- beside an item in a frame is in that frame which grows to hold it

### `tests/components/agents/test_board_tools.py`

Design 0022 §4 and §7: Clank's own MCP server gives the agent the board as tools, each a request to the gateway's `/agents/boards` routes naming the workspace the server serves: `board_new(name, folder, title)`, `board_read(board)` (read-only: the board in words), `board_add(board, kind, ..., at)` (the item's fields as the model gave them, placed by `at`), `board_update(board, id, ..., at)`, `board_remove(board, id)` and `board_export(board, frame or ids, format, file, keep)`. The descriptions say to place by where, never by pixels; each answer is the service's words, a refusal the tool's error in its words.

- the board tools are listed and say to place by where
- each tool asks the gateway naming its workspace and answers the services words
- board export asks for a frame as a figure file

### `tests/components/agents/test_boards.py`

Design 0022 §1, §2 and §4: a board is a `.board` file of the folder, JSON: its title, settings and items. `new` makes one (refused when it exists); `list` finds the folder's. The agent adds a figure (sized from its file, its aspect kept, its caption under it), LaTeX, a note, a slot, a frame (around items it names) or an arrow, each placed by where; a figure in a slot takes the slot; a frame grows to hold what is placed in it. `update` changes an item's words or place, `remove` takes it away. Every change of the agent's keeps the board before it in the board's history and records `agents.board_changed`. The board is said in words for the model: each item with its id, kind, place and what it shows, what each frame holds, the open slots, and the owner's annotations by what they are over ("a pen loop around the upper middle of fig-1"). A figure that is not a file of the folder, a kind the agent may not add, or an item that is not on the board, is refused, saying why. The routes answer the same.

- images are measured from their files
- a new board is a file of the folder and is listed
- the agent adds figures latex notes slots and frames each placed by where
- update moves and rewords and remove takes away each kept in history
- the board is said in words with the owners annotations by what they are over
- what cannot be on a board is refused saying why
- the routes make read and change a board
- clank board makes lists and shows a folders boards
- the agent closes a pin it dealt with

### `tests/components/agents/test_changes.py`

Design 0007 §5: a change the agent asks for, worked out as the file it would leave, from what OpenCode 1.19 says (`tests/fixtures/opencode/1.19.0/changes.json`, recorded) and from made-up asks; its diff and counts; where it is kept.

- a recorded edit is worked out exactly from its tool call and from its diff alone
- a recorded write of a new file is its content
- an edit that is not exact is worked out from its diff
- a diff that no longer fits the file cannot be worked out
- a windows file keeps its line endings
- the diff clients show is made from the file before and after with its counts
- changes are kept the newest first and the oldest forgotten

### `tests/components/agents/test_context_margin.py`

Design 0012 §4.1 (revised 2026-10-20, decision 0089): OpenCode is told the engine's window less a margin kept free for one step's tool results (`config/opencode.json`'s `context_margin`, 16,384 unless the owner sets another), so it compacts a session once a step's total reaches 229,376 tokens (262,144 less twice 16,384), before that step's results, sent with the next request, can push the request and its answer's room past the engine's window (KVMem refuses such a request: a session failed so at step 143, 35 tokens under the old line of 245,760). The margin is never more than a quarter of the window, and 0 tells OpenCode the whole window. What `prepare` writes, what the folder page is told and the Monitor's compaction line agree, and the Monitor still shows the engine's own window. The sessions are synthetic.

- the margin is 16384 by default in a readable settings file and never negative
- opencode is told the window less the margin and compacts before a steps results can overflow it
- the margin is at most a quarter of the window and 0 tells opencode the whole window
- prepare tells opencode the window less the margin and the monitors line agrees
- a margin the owner sets is what opencode is told at its next start

### `tests/components/agents/test_conversation.py`

Design 0021 §4: the agent panel's conversation. `Sessions.reach` finds the server that reaches a session: the one the service heard it on, else one of the workspace's servers that lists it, else the first that runs (every OpenCode server of a folder reads the sessions it keeps on disk), else none. `GET /agents/conversation?folder=` composes, for the folder's workspace, its session (the one named, else the newest the service keeps a flow of, else the newest root session a server lists, so a panel opened after the service restarted still has one), the server that reaches it, a page of its messages as the bridge reads them (the newest, or those before a cursor) with the cursor of the page before, whether it is busy, and the permissions and questions waiting in it; a folder with no workspace, or a workspace with no session, answers what there is. The panel's Terminal shows the session in the workspace's OpenCode terminal, refused when none runs.

- the server that reaches a session is the one heard else one that lists it else the first that runs
- the conversation is the folders session its page of messages whether it is busy and what waits in it
- a session is shown in the workspaces terminal and refused when no terminal runs

### `tests/components/agents/test_conversation_mirror.py`

Design 0023 §2 (decision 0139): the agent panel's conversation is read from the service's mirror, never from the container at each read, run against the fake OpenCode with the bridge on this machine. The first read of a session follows it (the watch told) and reads its history once, whole, through the watcher's own process (design 0023 §4: no process of its own); after that, what the model writes reaches the conversation through the watcher's lines alone: ten reads while a step streams ask the container nothing (no process, no request), the text comes whole (longer than the Monitor's brief), and the newest words are there. A session's older messages are read from OpenCode once more only when a client asks for what the mirror does not hold.

- the conversation is read from the mirror and no process starts after the history

### `tests/components/agents/test_conversation_stream.py`

Design 0023 §3: the conversation stream, `GET /agents/conversation/stream`, over real HTTP. It says it is connected, then gives a snapshot (the conversation route's answer, with its version), then each change of the mirror as an event whose id is its version: the status, a message, a part's text as it is written (`delta`), the waits; while the session works, a `readout` event each second; a change the mirror hears is on the stream within the gather window (design 0023 §6); `after` (or `Last-Event-ID`) resumes from a version, and a client behind what the log keeps gets a snapshot again; when the folder's newest session changes and none was named, the stream says which and ends, so the client opens the new one. A folder with no session yet gets a snapshot saying so. A conversation the bridge cannot read ends the stream with a `problem` event saying why, the response whole, so the client opens it again later.

- the stream gives a snapshot then each change with its version and the readout while busy
- a change the mirror hears is on the stream within the gather window
- a client resumes from its version or gets a snapshot when behind and follows a new session
- a folder with no session yet gets a snapshot saying so
- a conversation the bridge cannot read ends the stream saying why and the response whole

### `tests/components/agents/test_figures.py`

Design 0008 §4: each mark the owner draws on a figure, in words: the plot it is on (its title, and where it is among several), its range in data coordinates in the axis's own scale (a log one too), the lines inside it with their points and values; an arrow's tip and the line it points at (or the legend it stops just short of); a note's words and place; a mark on a legend or a title says so; without the figure's geometry, its place in the image.

- a box is said in data coordinates with the lines inside it
- a circle holds only what its ellipse holds and a log axis is read in its scale
- an arrow says what its tip points at and a note says its words
- an arrow that stops just short of a legend points at it
- without geometry a mark is placed in the image

### `tests/components/agents/test_find_figures_tool.py`

Design 0018 §7.4: Clank's own MCP server gives the agent `find_figures(query, limit)`, which finds the figures of the folder that best match words, from what is known of each: its name and folder; the title, axis labels and legend Matplotlib recorded when it was saved (`.clank/figures/`) and the script that saved it; the LaTeX `\caption` and `\label` of a figure environment that includes it; a script that names it; and when it was made (today, yesterday, this week, last week, a weekday, the latest). Each figure found says why it matched. Folders of tools and environments (`.git`, `node_modules`, `.venv`, caches) are not searched. It runs in the container, reads only, asks nothing of the gateway, and says when nothing matches, naming the newest figures instead.

- find figures is listed read only with a query and a limit
- a recorded figure is found by its title labels and legend with why
- a pdf figure is found by its latex caption and the script that names it
- when it was made counts and the limit caps the list
- nothing matching says so and names the newest figures

### `tests/components/agents/test_flow_servers.py`

Design 0013 §4, §5 and §7 (decision 0062): what the flows' runner needs of a workspace's agents. A session at work in the workspace that is no session of a flow's box, nor a subagent of one, is the owner's own, which a switch of the main slot waits for. The flow's own OpenCode server, without a terminal, can be ended (to start it again with a box's limits), and a window never is. A box's MCP servers are connected on the flow's server alone, those it does not name disconnected there, Clank's own always kept (it carries the flow's tools), the window's left as they are, a box naming none (or an empty list) getting every one the workspace's agent gets; a server the workspace's agent does not get is refused, saying why, while one the bridge fails to connect or disconnect is said and the others still change. Run against the fake bridge (`tests/fakes/bridge.py`), with no container and no OpenCode.

- a session at work that is no boxs is the owners and its subagents too
- a server without a terminal is ended and a window never
- a boxs mcp servers are connected on its server alone and clanks own kept
- a box that names its mcp servers as an empty list gets every one the workspaces agent gets
- an mcp server the bridge cannot start or stop is said and the others still change
- an mcp server the workspaces agent does not get is refused saying why

### `tests/components/agents/test_flow_tools.py`

Design 0013 §9 and §15: Clank's own MCP server in the container gives a box's model its flow's tools, each a request to the gateway's `/flows/` routes naming the workspace the server serves: `flow_verdict`, a VERIFY's verdict (its result, its findings and `criteria`, each line of the brief and of what done means that the check touched, met or not, with its evidence); `flow_read`, the flow as the service tells it, marked read-only; `flow_propose_box`, a box proposed after a box of the flow, its arguments sent as the model gave them; `flow_submit`, the proposals ready for the owner; `flow_draft` (design 0013 §15), a whole flow drafted in a planning session, sent as the model gave it; and `flow_job` (§17), a box's long computation, its command, title and minutes. Each answer is what the service said; a refusal is the tool's error, in the service's words; a server that does not know its workspace asks nothing. None writes anything in the folder.

- flow verdict is listed with its result findings and criteria
- flow verdict asks the gateways flows route naming its workspace and answers what the service said
- flow verdict sends the criteria as the model gave them so the services refusal names what to fix
- a refusal is the tools error and a server that does not know its workspace gives none
- the tools of a flow are listed and flow read is marked read only
- flow propose box sends its arguments with its workspace and answers what the service said
- flow submit sends its summary and flow read asks with its workspace
- flow draft sends the whole flow as the model gave it naming its workspace
- flow job sends its command title and minutes naming its workspace

### `tests/components/agents/test_flow_tree.py`

Design 0012 §6.2-§6.4 and §11 step 6 (decision 0050): what the Monitor's Flow panel draws of a session's flow, as a tree. The owner's message is the root and the main branches (a plan's steps, the todo list's items, or the owner's messages) are its children, each marked by its state, a planned one on a line not taken yet; a branch's work comes in time order: a step's calls made at once are one node that fans out to each, a run of one tool folds into one node (`read ×3`), a call that failed then passed folds into its tries; a subagent is a branch of its own, with its own flow's work when it is given; the owner's note joins the branch of the step under way when it was sent; the step working now is the newest node; a step that left the context is drawn apart from one the owner stopped and kept; a dead end ends in its mark; steps undone are a side lane, folded; a compaction is a band, and the branches above it fold into it. Folds: a done branch folds to one line, the branch in progress is always open, a fold that holds the newest node is open while it works, and the owner's own fold or open wins over the default. Design 0012 §9.4 (§11 step 11): a hold is drawn on its step's line, before its work, "held by you 12:22 to 12:25", and one a note closed says so ("until a note"). Design 0012 §9.5 and §9.7 (§11 step 10): steps undone say that u redoes them until they are final, and then that they are; a result the Guide cleared says "result cleared by you" with the tokens it freed, one OpenCode cleared itself only that it is cleared. The flows are made by step 5's `Flow` from synthetic sessions.

- the owner is the root and the main branches are its children each marked by its state
- a branchs calls come in time order and a run of one tool folds into one node
- calls made at once are one node that fans out to each
- a call that failed then passed folds as its tries
- steps that join no branch hang from a branch of their own
- a subagent is a branch of its own with its own flows work when given
- a note joins the branch of the step under way when it was sent with its state
- the step working now is the newest node
- a step that left the context is drawn apart from one stopped and kept
- a dead end ends in its mark and a failed call still under way does not
- steps undone are a side lane folded
- a result cleared by you and steps undone for good are drawn so
- a compaction is a band and the branches above it fold into it
- a branch keeps its id when a compaction comes so the owners folds and cursor stay on it
- a done branch folds to one line and the branch in progress is always open
- the owners fold or open wins over the default
- a fold that holds the newest node is open by default
- a hold is drawn held by you from when to when
- a hold a note closed is drawn until the note

### `tests/components/agents/test_guide.py`

Design 0012 §9.1, §9.2, §9.3 and §9.6 (§11 step 9): what the Guide offers for the session the Monitor follows, a pure function of its flow, the server that runs it, its context totals and the gateway's figures of its requests.

- each action says its key what it does its exact call and its cost
- an action that cannot run says why
- the reading speed comes from the gateways records else the time is not known
- each actions state is read from the flow
- an undo offer never fails on a step that has no number

### `tests/components/agents/test_guide_actions.py`

Design 0012 §9.1, §9.2, §9.3 and §9.6 (§11 step 9), decisions 0054 and 0079: the Guide's actions, run against two fake OpenCode servers in one workspace's container (`tests/fakes/opencode.py`) with the bridge on this machine.

- stop aborts the session and rejects a waiting ask on the server that runs it
- a note carries the agent the model and what it is about
- a note while a call waits for approval rejects that call with the note
- compact now asks summarize with the agent role and auto on and returns at once
- each action leaves an agents acted event with who did it
- a rules note goes through the guide as the rule
- an action that cannot run sends nothing and says why
- the routes offer the actions act and refuse with the reason

### `tests/components/agents/test_guide_undo_actions.py`

Design 0012 §9.1, §9.5 and §9.7 (§11 step 10), decisions 0056 and 0079: the Guide's Undo, Redo and Drop, run against two fake OpenCode servers in one workspace's container (`tests/fakes/opencode.py`) with the bridge on this machine.

- the session is found on its server however slowly its stream begins
- undo reverts at the previous steps last part on the server that runs it
- undo the turn names your message and gives its text back
- redo unreverts and each action leaves an agents acted event
- an undo the session moved on from is refused and sends nothing
- drop sends the whole part with its time compacted and the flow says by you
- a drop of the step in progress sends nothing and says why
- the routes offer undo and drop act and refuse with the reason
- an undo that names no revert is refused and sends nothing
- an undo that would now take back more than it showed is refused
- the fake opencode reverts as opencode does
- an undo opencode takes further back gives back the words it took and says so

### `tests/components/agents/test_guide_undo_and_drop.py`

Design 0012 §9.1, §9.5 and §9.7 (§11 step 10), decision 0056: what the Guide offers for Undo (`u`) and Drop (`x`), a pure function of the session's flow, the server that runs it, its context totals and the gateway's figures.

- undo cuts at the previous steps last part and previews its steps and files
- a step with nothing before its last part is taken back with the step after it
- undo of the first step after your message is refused and offers undo the turn
- undo waits for the session to stop and redo follows until your next message
- drop is refused for the step in progress and says what it frees and costs
- undo and drop states are read from the flow

### `tests/components/agents/test_job_tools.py`

Design 0021 §6: Clank's own MCP server gives the agent `run_job(command, title, minutes)`, `job_status(job)` and `job_stop(job)`: requests to the gateway's `/agents/jobs`, `/agents/jobs/status` and `/agents/jobs/stop` naming the workspace the server serves. `run_job`'s description tells the model to start as a job any command expected to run more than two minutes, and that it is told when the job ends; its answer is the service's words. `job_status` reads only (the planner may use it): one job with its log's last lines and the files it wrote, or every job of the workspace in a line each. A refusal is the tool's error in the service's words.

- the job tools are listed and run job says when to use it
- run job asks the gateway naming its workspace and answers the services words
- job status gives one job whole or every job in a line

### `tests/components/agents/test_latex_build.py`

Design 0020 §3: LaTeX Workshop builds in the container. `POST /agents/latex/build` builds the document a `.tex` file of a workspace belongs to (its root) in the workspace's container, and keeps its PDF, its log and its SyncTeX file beside the root's `.tex`, SyncTeX's paths made this machine's (so LaTeX Workshop goes from the PDF to the source and back); it answers whether it built, where the PDF is, its errors and its undefined references, citations and labels defined twice. A file in no workspace, or no `.tex`, is refused. Clank's VS Code window gets a LaTeX Workshop recipe that runs `clank latex build` on the root, first, unless the owner has recipes of their own.

- a tex file builds its document in the container kept beside it
- the helper makes synctexs paths this machines
- the window builds latex with clank unless the owner has recipes of their own

### `tests/components/agents/test_latex_building.py`

Design 0020 §8: the document a changed file belongs to is found (its `% !TEX root`, the file itself when it has `\documentclass`, else the file that includes it with `\input`, `\include` or `\subfile`, from its folder or the folder's root), and its engine (`% !TEX program`, LuaLaTeX for `fontspec` or `unicode-math`, else pdfLaTeX). A build's log is read (logs recorded from real builds, `tests/fixtures/latex/`): its errors with their file and line, its undefined references and citations (LaTeX's, natbib's, BibTeX's), its labels defined twice. A change is refused when the document no longer builds, or when its build has an undefined reference or citation, or a label defined twice, that the build before it did not have; warnings the document already had do not refuse it.

- the root is the magic comment the file itself or the file that includes it
- the engine is the magic comment lualatex for its fonts or pdflatex
- a clean build has nothing to say
- the warnings of a build are read from its log and bibtexs
- a failed builds errors are read with their file and line
- a change is refused for a failed build or what its build adds

### `tests/components/agents/test_latex_citations.py`

Design 0020 §6: the citations of a LaTeX file are read in every form natbib and biblatex give them (`\cite`, `\citep`, `\citet`, `\parencite`, `\textcite`, `\autocite`, `\citeauthor`, `\citeyear`, `\nocite`, starred, with optional arguments, several keys), comments left out, each with its line; the bibliographies a document loads (`\bibliography`, `\addbibresource`) and the entries of a `.bib` file are read. A change is refused for each citation it adds whose key is no entry, or when it cites with no bibliography loaded; a key the file cited before is not its. A new or changed `.bib` entry must have a DOI or an arXiv id that resolves and matches its title, first author and year (Crossref and arXiv, from recorded answers); an entry with neither, one whose identifier does not resolve, or one that does not match is refused, saying what the record says; a lookup that cannot be made leaves the entry for the owner.

- every citation form is read with its line and comments are left out
- a bib files entries are read with their fields
- a change is refused for each key it adds that is no entry
- new entries that match their records pass and are looked up once
- an entry without an identifier or that does not match or resolve is refused
- a lookup that cannot be made leaves the entry for the owner

### `tests/components/agents/test_latex_comments.py`

Design 0020 §11 and §12: comments the owner left on lines of LaTeX files go to the agent as one message, numbered, each with its file, its lines and the text it covers, and the rule to change only what they ask and answer each by its number (`POST /agents/latex/comments`, to the session most recently active; refused while no agent runs, or with no comment). After a numbered message (comments, or marks on a PDF's page), once the agent's turn ends, its reply is read: each number it did not answer is asked for once more, in one message, and never a second time.

- comments are one numbered message with their files lines and text
- the numbers a reply answers are read in the forms models write them
- the route sends the comments to the agent and refuses with no agent or no comment
- a number left unanswered is asked for once when the turn ends
- a reply answering every number asks nothing

### `tests/components/agents/test_latex_pages.py`

Design 0020 §10: a PDF of the folder is annotated page by page. Its pages are rendered in the container with each page's words in boxes (a PDF beside the `.tex` of its name is built by Clank first, so SyncTeX can say where each mark comes from), kept under `.clank/latex/pages/`. A mark on a page goes to the agent in words: its kind and color, its page, the source file and line SyncTeX gives for it, and the words under it (a highlight, a box, a drawing), through it (a strike), after it (an insertion, with what to insert), at it (a note) or pointed at (an arrow); a box with no words under it is a figure or an equation's image.

- a pdf beside its tex is built first then its pages are rendered and kept
- a pdf with no tex beside it is rendered as it is
- each mark on a page is said with its line and the words under it

### `tests/components/agents/test_latex_pages_service.py`

Design 0020 §10, through the service: `POST /agents/latex/pages` renders a PDF of a workspace's folder as pages to mark up (refused for a folder with no workspace, or a path that is no PDF of it), and a page marked up and sent goes to the agent as a page of its PDF, each mark with its source line and the words under it.

- the route renders a pdfs pages and refuses what it cannot
- a marked up page reaches the agent as a page of its pdf

### `tests/components/agents/test_latex_review.py`

Design 0020 §4 and §5: the review's rule for LaTeX files and the checks on every LaTeX change, run against a fake OpenCode server with the bridge's watcher, the builds answered from logs recorded in the TeX layer.

- a change to the owners tex is asked about in auto mode once it passed the checks
- a change that fails a check is rejected with its reasons and nobody is asked
- the build failing refuses and the third refusal in a row asks the owner
- a tex file the agent created follows auto mode
- without tex in the container the owner is asked and told why
- a bib entry without a doi or arxiv id is refused

### `tests/components/agents/test_latex_sources.py`

Design 0020 §7: a paragraph a change adds or alters that states a value carries `% source:` within it, naming a file of the folder, a key of the bibliography, a DOI or an arXiv id, or `% UNVERIFIED:` with what is not checked. A value is a decimal, a percentage, a number in scientific notation, or an integer of three digits or more that is not a year; small integers, lengths (`0.8\linewidth`, `2em`), labels, references, citation keys, optional arguments, the preamble and comments are not. Paragraphs the file had before are not the change's. Each refusal names the paragraph's line and the values found, or the source that is not one.

- values are decimals percentages scientific notation and large integers not years
- a new paragraph with a value and no source is refused naming its line and values
- a source must be a file a key a doi or an arxiv id
- a file named from the documents own folder counts
- unverified needs its words and is counted
- paragraphs the file had before are not the changes

### `tests/components/agents/test_look.py`

Design 0008 §7: the agent's tool `look_at_figure` (Clank's own MCP server in the container) asks the vision model, through the gateway's role `vision`, about a figure of the folder, and gives the figure's geometry beside the answer (its plots, their axes and lines, as Matplotlib's save left them); when the vision model does not answer (vision is off), the answer says why and the geometry is still given; a file that is not a PNG or a JPEG is refused.

- the vision model answers about a figure with its geometry beside
- without the vision model the geometry is still given and a non image is refused
- a slow reading answers within the tools time limit and is given when asked again

### `tests/components/agents/test_mirror.py`

Design 0023 §2 (decision 0139): the mirror the service keeps of the sessions clients follow, built from the watchers' lines alone. A followed session's messages and parts are assembled from `message`, `part` and `delta` lines (a delta appends to its part's text, a part line replaces the part whole, a removal takes a message or a part away), its status from `status` lines, and the permissions and questions open from `asked`, `replied`, `question` and `answered`; a session not followed leaves nothing. Its history merges once; a page from memory names the message to read before, and the cursor OpenCode gave for what the mirror does not hold. The sessions a workspace's watcher saw are known, newest first. The mirror is bounded: past `FULL_BYTES` the oldest parts' text falls back to its brief, and a session no client read for `FORGET_MINUTES` is forgotten.

- a followed sessions messages and parts are assembled from the watchers lines
- history merges once and older pages come from memory then from opencodes cursor
- permissions and questions open are those asked and not yet answered
- the sessions a watcher saw are known newest first with their status and port
- the mirror is bounded old text falls back to its brief and sessions unread are forgotten
- a server the watch no longer follows leaves its sessions without a port and idle
- each change is kept with its version and subscribers hear each new one

### `tests/components/agents/test_model_changed.py`

Design 0013 §7 (decision 0062): after a flow's box switched the main slot, the owner's window in the workspace is told its model changed: in the event `agents.model_changed` (which VS Code shows), and in a notice the review's view carries for This folder, until OpenCode is next started there. A preset whose context is smaller than what one of the owner's own sessions holds is named as too small for it; a box's session is not the owner's. The window itself is left as it is: its configuration changes only when it next starts. Run against the fake bridge, with no container and no OpenCode.

- the owners window is told in an event and on the reviews view
- with no window open the next opencode started is said to get it
- a preset with a smaller context than the owners session holds is named
- the windows configuration changes only when it next starts which ends the notice

### `tests/components/agents/test_modes.py`

Design 0009 §7-§8: the mode as the owner sees it (plan, review or auto) and handing a plan to the agent.

- the mode shown follows the messages sent in the window and not the phones
- plan review and auto switch the window by what tab does and set the change rule
- with no window the change rule is set and a note says why the agent was not switched
- a plan is handed to a new session in the window with implements instructions
- only a plan of the folder is handed over
- a plan is not handed over without a window nor in plan mode
- the folders plans are listed with their steps done

### `tests/components/agents/test_monitor_bridge.py`

Design 0012 §3, §5.1, §6.1 and §9 (§11 step 4) and design 0013 §5 and §7: the bridge the Monitor and flows need, run on this machine against a fake OpenCode server replaying a synthetic session's events (`tests/fixtures/opencode/1.19.0/session.json`). Design 0023 §2: a page read whole, for the mirror.

- the bridge pages through a sessions messages each part in the watchs shape
- the bridge gives one call whole its input and output cut only past a cap
- a planner session stays planner after a note
- a message finds the owners last message however far back and its variant
- stops reject leaves nothing waiting in the session or its subagents and touches no other
- reject goes on past an ask or a question answered meanwhile
- a compaction not confirmed in time is ended so it cannot start after the error
- a cleared result is sent back whole with its time and the step in progress is refused
- revert names the part and unrevert brings it back
- summarize returns at once and its request stays open until opencode answers
- a steps error keeps its name so a stop tells apart from a failure
- a page read whole carries each parts text and output uncut

### `tests/components/agents/test_monitor_context.py`

Design 0012 §3, §4, §4.1, §4.4 and §11 step 3, decision 0067: a session's context totals come from the loaded slot's facts only (its engine adapter's context facts, its model's catalog entry), with both engine styles, KVMem and upstream llama.cpp: how much of the window the session's last finished step used (its prompt and its answer, as its engine reports them), how much is left before OpenCode compacts (262,144 and 16,384 give 245,760) and what a compaction keeps (15,000), the share read from the cache where each engine reports it, what sits on the GPU (under KVMem a stored size of at most 36,864 all of it, above it 36,864 and the rest in system memory; under llama.cpp its whole window attended), a figure an engine does not report absent and never a number, the window changed at the next step after a slot switch, and the engine marked as taken by another session's request. A step whose stream the engine broke off is not a finished step, and an engine that stopped, started again or was switched holds no conversation, whoever's request it read last. The sessions here are synthetic.

- 262144 and 16384 give a compaction line of 245760 and a compaction keeps 15000
- used is the last finished steps prompt and answer and left is the line less it
- under kvmem a stored size of at most 36864 is all on the gpu
- under kvmem above 36864 the rest is in system memory
- under llamacpp the whole window is attended
- the cache share is read where each engine says it reports it
- a figure an engine does not report is absent never a number
- a slot switch to another preset changes the window at the next step
- another sessions request marks the engine as taken
- a step whose stream the engine broke off is not the last finished step
- an engine that stopped started again or was switched holds no conversation
- with no step ended the window is the slots and what is used is not known
- the totals read each fake engine styles own fields through the gateway
- a stream the engine broke off after a good step leaves that steps totals through the gateway
- the services context route gives a sessions totals

### `tests/components/agents/test_monitor_estimate.py`

Design 0012 §4.2, §4.3, §4.6 and §11 step 12 (decisions 0051, 0052 and 0067): the context's parts, estimated in the service from the gateway's census of the last finished step: for a synthetic request with known parts, the parts' tokens add up to the engine's own `prompt_tokens` exactly, each part's share its characters' (weighted by the model's calibration, each part's tokens per character, when it has one); earlier thinking is left out by the template's rule (Qwen's reference template drops it before the last user message) and kept by a template that keeps it, and a model whose template has no thinking has no thinking part; tool results map to their call ids and so to the steps that made them, a result OpenCode cleared is marked, and so are the items inside the tail a compaction keeps; where the room went and what could be freed; and the context totals carry the parts of the last finished step and of each step before. The requests and sessions are synthetic.

- for a synthetic request with known parts the shares add up to prompt tokens
- earlier thinking is left out by the templates rule and kept by another
- a model whose template has no thinking has no thinking part
- a calibrated model weighs each part by its tokens per character
- tool results map to their call ids and their steps and a cleared one is marked
- items in the tail a compaction keeps are marked
- where the room went and what could be freed
- the totals carry the parts of the last finished step and of each step before
- a result the guide cleared says by you and what it freed and is left out of what could be freed
- two answers without calls in a row each get their own step never the one before
- could be freed offers drop only for results and the rest of the loop text to compacting

### `tests/components/agents/test_monitor_now.py`

Design 0012 §5 and §11 step 7: what a session does now, as the service says it for the Monitor's Now panel. Every row of §5.1's phase table is said from its source (the gateway for its requests, OpenCode for the session), and a request the engine has not answered yet is "reading the prompt (or queued)", never claimed queued alone (§5.1). The thinking and the answer are shown against their budgets, from the slot's facts (§5.2, decision 0067): live counts say "about", and once the answer ended its count is the engine's own and the thinking's is scaled to it, still "about". The prompt is this step's time to its first token and the previous step's size and cache share (§5.3); the speed keeps each step's; the call being written is the gateway's (§5.3); and the loop guard says whether it is on as the running gateway applies it (a change to its settings file waits for its restart, which is said), each part's newest share against its ratio, or what it stopped (§5.4). The sessions here are synthetic.

- each row of the phase table is said from its source
- each phase says its detail and whether it works
- reading the prompt is said or queued and queued is never claimed alone
- live counts say about and the answers end corrects them from the engines count
- the prompt is this steps time and the previous steps size and the speed keeps each steps
- the call being written is the gateways
- the loop guard says off watching each parts share or what it stopped
- the route says what the followed session does now
- the loop guard is said as the running gateway applies it and a change waiting for its restart too

### `tests/components/agents/test_monitor_sessions.py`

Design 0012 §3, §6.1 and §9 (§11 step 4) and design 0013 §5: the service knows which server runs each session from its watcher's lines, and every action on a session goes to that server, run against two fake OpenCode servers in one workspace's container (`tests/fakes/opencode.py`) with the bridge on this machine.

- every action reaches the server that runs the session and no other
- a server gone leaves its sessions unplaced and listeners hear every line
- a watcher started again forgets a server its sync no longer follows
- listeners hear a line once the review has handled it
- the sessions shown are told to the watch and told again when it starts again
- the sessions followed are told to the watch and told again when it starts again
- the actions the owner waits on go through the watcher and start no process
- a request the watcher does not answer goes through a process of its own and the watcher starts again

### `tests/components/agents/test_monitor_watch.py`

Design 0012 §3, §5.1 and §6.1 (§11 step 4) and design 0013 §5 and §7: the bridge's watch forwards the Monitor's events, run on this machine against a fake OpenCode server replaying a synthetic session's events (`tests/fixtures/opencode/1.19.0/session.json`).

- the watch says each thing a step changes once per burst with its last state
- the watch says retries compactions removals errors questions and an instance that ended
- compactions removals and errors said again in a burst are said once with the last
- asks questions answers and an instance that ended come at once after what the burst held
- a stream opened again says idle for a session that no longer runs
- a stream still open after its instance ended is opened again and hears what follows
- a stream that never says it is connected is opened again
- a stream quiet for a while is opened again and says what ended meanwhile
- a server first followed says its busy sessions and its waiting questions
- a shown sessions text comes as it is written joined per part and no other sessions
- a request on the watch input is answered at once with its id among the events
- a followed sessions parts come whole with their text as it is written and others cut

### `tests/components/agents/test_notion_tools.py`

Design 0010 §4-§5: Clank's own MCP server in the container gives the agent Notion's tools, each a request to the gateway's `/notion/` routes: reading (the shared databases, a query, a page as Markdown with its properties, a search; marked read-only, so the planner may use them) and proposing (a new entry, a change, a removal, submitting the batch, each naming the workspace the server serves); a refusal comes back as the tool's error, in the service's words; a server that does not know its workspace cannot propose.

- the notion tools are listed the reading ones marked read only
- reading asks the gateways notion routes and a page reads as markdown
- a proposal names the servers workspace and a refusal is the tools error
- a server that does not know its workspace cannot propose

### `tests/components/agents/test_one_window.py`

Design 0004 §5: one OpenCode window per workspace. Opening OpenCode in a terminal (VS Code's, or the console's) first closes every OpenCode window open in the workspace's container, so there is only ever one and the mode switches reach it (found live, 2026-10-16: five windows, two of them relaunched by VS Code with a port another already had, so Clank could not reach the one in front of the owner). OpenCode without a terminal (`opencode serve`, the phone's) is left alone; a window that does not end when asked is ended. The workspace's record turns it off; the review view says which it is.

- opening opencode in a terminal first closes the windows open in its workspace but not one without
- a window that does not end when asked is ended
- with one window turned off opening opencode adds a window and closes none

### `tests/components/agents/test_opencode.py`

Design 0004 §4 and design 0009 §3-§5: OpenCode's configuration has one provider, Clank's gateway at the edge, with one model, the role `agent`, its limits the running preset's (so OpenCode compacts a conversation before the engine refuses it); it is OpenCode's only provider; nothing outside the folder is OpenCode's to touch and it has no web tools (there is no route out); and OpenCode runs offline: no updates, no model list, no downloads, no plugins, no sharing, no watching of the folder (slow across Docker's mount of a Windows folder), no reading of another assistant's files.

- its configuration is clanks gateway with the agent role and its limits
- the planner writes markdown only reads freely and gets only the tools marked read only
- the planner and the explore helper it calls never ask for a permission
- a server with no read only tools known gives the planner none
- implement hands a plan to build with its instructions
- the instructions say how to plan measure and carry out a plan
- every agent reads clanks instructions in the container the latex rules among them
- it runs its terminal interface offline

### `tests/components/agents/test_opencode_output.py`

Design 0016 §7 and design 0012 §4.1 (revised after approval 2026-10-29, decision 0153): OpenCode caps each answer at 32,000 tokens unless `OPENCODE_EXPERIMENTAL_OUTPUT_TOKEN_MAX` says more (read in OpenCode 1.19.4's source, the image's version), so for a model whose most output is above 32,000, Clank sets the variable to that most output when OpenCode starts, from a terminal or without one, and its notes say so; at 32,000 or under it sets nothing. OpenCode then counts the whole most output in its compaction line, and so do the Monitor's rule and Watch for's line, while what a compaction keeps is still counted with 32,000, as OpenCode counts it. Run against the fake bridge, with no container and no OpenCode.

- the variable is the most output above 32000 and unset at or under it
- the compaction line counts the whole most output and what it keeps counts 32000
- watch for counts the whole most output above 32000
- opencode started from a terminal gets the variable and its note says so
- opencode started without a terminal gets the variable too

### `tests/components/agents/test_pausing.py`

Design 0012 §9.1, §9.4, §3 and §11 step 11 (decision 0055): the Guide's pause in the service. It pauses the root of the session it is given (the Guide acts on the root session), through the gateway, each pause and resume an `agents.acted` event with who did it; its state is pausing after this step, then held with its timer, then resumed; its action says its key, what it does, its exact call, its cost and why it cannot run, and the Guide offers it after Compact now, before Undo; a message sent to a paused session closes the request it holds, after the message was sent, and only one held before it was sent, never the request that carries it; a Watch for rule set to pause pauses the session, as the rule, through the Guide; and the routes pause, resume and refuse with the reason. The gateway is a fake here; the sessions are synthetic.

- pausing a subagent pauses its root and leaves an agents acted event
- the pauses state is pausing after this step then held with its timer then resumed
- the pause action says its key what it does its exact call and its cost
- the guide offers the pause after compact now
- a message sent to a paused session closes its held request
- a messages release closes only what was held before the message was sent
- a rule set to pause pauses the session through the gateway
- the routes pause and resume and refuse with the reason

### `tests/components/agents/test_plans.py`

Design 0009 §4: a plan is a Markdown file whose header (its front matter, between a first line `---` and the next `---`) says `kind: plan`, wherever it lives in the folder; a file that only mentions it elsewhere is not one, and no file is a plan outside the folder, in a folder agents and assistants load their settings from, or among the files that instruct them (`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`).

- a header that says kind plan makes a plan
- anything else is not a plan
- a markdown file in the folder may be a plan
- no other file is one

### `tests/components/agents/test_reach.py`

Design 0004 §5 (revised 2026-10-26): whether a workspace's agent reaches its model now. `GET /agents/reach` follows the way OpenCode's requests take and says the first thing wrong, in words, with what fixes it: a folder with no workspace, or whose container does not run (open it); the gateway that does not run (start it); from inside the container, the gateway's host `edge` that does not resolve, or a gateway that does not answer through it (open the workspace again); each of these stops OpenCode at once, so a client does not start it. The model in the main slot comes last: loading is said, none running is said with loading the last that worked, and neither stops OpenCode from starting. Every check is kept with its words, a check that cannot be run is said as unknown, and nothing is written.

- a workspace whose gateway answers through the edge with a model ready reaches it
- a container that cannot resolve the edge is blocked with opening the workspace again
- a gateway that does not answer through the edge is blocked too
- a gateway that does not run is said first with its start and the container is not asked
- a folder with no workspace or a container that does not run is blocked with opening it
- no model in the main slot is said with loading the last that worked and does not block
- a model loading is said and does not block
