- a look from inside that cannot run is unknown and stops nothing
- the route answers by folder or by workspace

### `tests/components/agents/test_readout.py`

Design 0021 §3: the model's readout. KVMem says in its log the tokens it drafted and how many were accepted, the newest line counting; an engine that does not say it gives nothing. The runtime reads it from the newest launch's log of a slot, and a slot never launched has none. `GET /agents/readout?folder=` composes, for the folder's workspace, its newest kept session (or the one named) with that session's `now` and context totals, the main slot (its state, its preset and title, and the options that change its speed), the GPU's newest meter and the drafts; a folder with no workspace, or a workspace with no session yet, answers what there is. Its jobs running (design 0021 §6) come with it; a workspace removed while its readout is read answers what there is, without jobs.

- kvmem says its drafted tokens accepted in its log and the newest line counts
- the runtime reads the drafts from the slots newest launch log
- the readout composes the folders newest session its now its context the slot and the machine
- a workspace removed while its readout is read answers what there is without jobs

### `tests/components/agents/test_remote_code.py`

Design 0018 §8.1 and §8.2: VS Code on another computer. VS Code's remote command, which a client in a terminal of VS Code's Remote-SSH sends with its window's pipe, is run by the operation `agents.vscode` as its `code.cmd` runs it on Windows (the server's Node with its `server-cli.js`, never through cmd.exe), the pipe in its environment; only the remote command of the service's own user's VS Code server is run. Opening sets the PC's side up first, each step only when needed: Clank's extension installed in VS Code's server when that version is not, each extension of the settings' list that is missing, Clank's settings added to the server's machine settings where missing (the owner's own kept); then the folder opens in a new window of the other computer's VS Code. An extension that cannot be installed is said, and the folder opens all the same; a remote command that does not answer fails the opening, saying why.

- the remote command runs as its cmd runs it and its server is found
- only the remote command of the services own vs code server is run
- opening sets the pcs side up then opens the folder and the next time only opens it
- an extension that cannot be installed is said and a command that does not answer fails the opening
- a remote command that is not the services own vs code servers is refused

### `tests/components/agents/test_review.py`

Design 0007 §3-§7 and design 0009 §5: reviewing the agent's changes, run on this machine against a fake OpenCode server. A change to a plan is accepted at once in review mode too, and shown afterwards.

- the watcher says each ask with its tool call each answer and each written file
- the watcher says the agent each message was sent to once
- the watcher says a server that stopped is gone
- a rejected change carries the note for the model
- in review a change waits for your answer and the agent hears it
- a question answered the moment it is asked still reaches the agent
- a reject tells the agent why an answer in the terminal or from the phone settles the question
- in auto mode clank answers at once
- in review the implementers edits to a plan are accepted at once and any other change still waits
- changes nobody waits for any more are withdrawn
- a container whose bridge cannot watch is said to need reopening
- the routes give a folders mode switch it and give each change with its files

### `tests/components/agents/test_session_flow.py`

Design 0012 §6.1-§6.4 and §11 step 5 (decisions 0049 and 0050): a session's flow, built from the bridge watch's lines and pages of the session's history (the synthetic session of plan s14 and small sessions made here, turned into the bridge's shapes by the bridge's own functions).

- parts become a step with its calls made at once fanned out
- a step that ended in an error is marked not in the context and a stopped one is kept
- a failed call becomes a dead end once the agent moves on
- a message or a part removed leaves the flow
- a line about another session or a status changes only its own facts
- task metadata becomes a subagents branch from its call
- a subagent session continued by a later task call is a branch from each call
- a permission asked for a call waits on that call until it is replied
- a compaction becomes a band across the flow
- the band of a compaction the guide asked for is the owners
- a note sent while busy is delivered at the next step
- two notes sent during one step are both read by the next step
- a note the run ended without reading is marked not read
- steps a revert took back are marked undone
- the console gets only what changed since its version
- a version older than a rebuild gets the whole flow
- a flow keeps texts briefly with their whole lengths

### `tests/components/agents/test_session_flow_branches.py`

Design 0012 §6.2 and §11 step 5 (decision 0050), design 0009 §4 and §5.1: a session's main branches. When the session carries out a Clank plan (its message is `/implement`'s, naming the plan's file), they are the plan's checklist steps, even beside a todo list, and a step joins the step first not ticked when it began; without such a plan, the todo list's items, matched across revisions by their text, a guessed match flagged, and a step joins the item in progress when it began; without either, the owner's messages and the runs of steps that answer them.

- a clank plans checklist steps become the main branches even beside a todo list
- a step joins the plan step first not ticked when it began
- a plans steps section is read and its other checklists are not
- without a plan todo revisions become items matched by their text a guess flagged
- an unchanged todo keeps its item when a new one alike is listed before it
- a long todo list read from the history is kept whole
- a step joins the todo item in progress when it began
- without either the owners messages and the runs of steps between them are the branches

### `tests/components/agents/test_session_flow_pairing.py`

Design 0012 §6.1 and §11 step 5 (decision 0049): a session's steps are joined to the gateway's requests by session, and pair with them in order, one model call at a time. An answer's call ids, equal to OpenCode's, confirm a pairing and win over time; without them a request pairs in order by time, another request for the same step is a try of it, the last its answer; steps whose requests the gateway no longer keeps, and steps from before Clank watched, stay unpaired; the clocks of the container and of this machine may differ by a second or so. Design 0012 §8: a step's request carries its tokens by kind and whether its engine broke off, which the Watch for rules read. Design 0012 §9.4 (§11 step 11): and the hold the gateway kept of it, while its session was paused; a request a note released pairs only with an empty step of its own, never in place of the answer of the step before it.

- n requests pair with n steps in order
- call ids confirm a pairing and win over time
- a request again for one step is a try and the last its answer
- steps whose requests the gateway no longer keeps stay unpaired
- a clock a second apart still pairs in order
- a steps request carries its counts by kind and whether its engine broke off
- a step whose request the gateway held carries its hold
- a request a note released pairs with its own empty step and never takes the step befores place

### `tests/components/agents/test_session_flow_undo_and_drop.py`

Design 0012 §9.5 and §9.7 (§11 step 10): what a session's flow says for the Guide's Undo and Drop.

- a step names the part a revert cuts at and the files it changed
- steps undone stay in the flow final once opencode removes them
- a result the guide cleared is marked by you with the tokens it freed

### `tests/components/agents/test_session_flows_kept.py`

Design 0012 §6.1 and §11 step 5 (decision 0049): the service keeps a flow for every session its watchers see, in memory. A session joined mid-run is read from OpenCode's history, its earlier steps marked done before Clank watched (run against the fake OpenCode with the bridge on this machine, and read through the API, whole or what changed); a flow is read again after its server's instance ended, and after its watcher started again once that watcher synced; the gateway is asked for a session's requests after its steps change, and they are paired with its steps; the plan a session carries out is read from the workspace's folder, and again when the agent edits it; a subagent's flow tells its parent's what it says; only the newest flows of a workspace are kept. Design 0012 §9.4 (§11 step 11): a session whose request the gateway holds is asked again each round until it goes on, since no line comes while it is held. A flow whose server ended is not read (nor tried again) until a server answers on its port again or its session is heard again, and the log says once that the window closed; a reading that keeps failing is said once.

- a session joined mid run marks its earlier steps before clank watched
- a flow is read again after its servers instance ended
- a flow whose reading failed is read again later and still marks its earlier steps
- a watcher started again leaves a new flows first reading its own
- a flow whose server ended is read no more until its server is back and that is said once
- a watcher started again reads its flows once it synced and sets aside those whose server ended
- a reading that finds its server gone is not tried again until the server is back
- a reading that keeps failing is said once until it is read
- a round reads a few flows and stops between them when the service stops
- stopping the service does not wait for a reading under way
- a sessions requests are asked of the gateway after its steps change and paired
- a session whose request the gateway holds is asked again each round until it goes on
- a plans file is read in the workspaces folder and again when it is edited
- a subagents flow tells its parents what it says
- only the newest flows of a workspace are kept
- a session deleted leaves the flows kept

### `tests/components/agents/test_session_modes.py`

Design 0013 §4 and §5 (decisions 0059 and 0060): a box's mode applies to its own session. The review answers a session's changes, and its subagents', by the mode the flows' runner gave it, while the workspace's own mode answers every other session's; and the service can start an OpenCode server without a terminal, and a session on it, apart, so a flow's boxes each get a session of their own on the flow's server. Run against the fake bridge (`tests/fakes/bridge.py`), with no container and no OpenCode.

- a session given auto has its changes accepted while the workspace stays in review
- a subagent of that session takes its mode
- another sessions change waits for review
- a session given review waits even while the workspace is in auto
- a session whose mode is taken back takes the workspaces again
- a mode that is not review or auto is refused
- a server and a session on it are started apart each recorded

### `tests/components/agents/test_sessions.py`

Design 0005 §2-§3: the session bridge and the agent's outbox, run on this machine against a fake OpenCode server.

- the bridge lists each servers root sessions their status and what waits
- the bridge gives a sessions messages briefly
- the bridge sends starts stops and replies and says why it cannot
- the bridge names the windows agents presses tab and hands a plan to a new session
- the bridge knows a terminal interface by its command line
- the bridge gives starts and stops the mcp servers of a running agent
- the service drives a session through the bridge and records who did what
- an action on a server that ended is refused at once saying so
- a page read whole names its cursor first so an older bridge reads none from the flag
- an mcp server is stopped and started while the agent runs and kept for its next start
- send to phone puts a file of the folder in its outbox with its caption
- the bridge waits for a window that answers late sends to the planner and shows the session again
- a planning session waits for a new windows slow first answers and starts
- a new session on a server just started waits for its slow first answer
- the bridge shows a session on a window that answers late and again
- a session the window does not show stands and says so

### `tests/components/agents/test_setup_checklist.py`

Design 0028 §4 and §5: `clank setup`'s checklist. Each item says its state and, while it is not done, what to do: its fix, and whether Clank does it on a yes. Each Docker state of §5 has its words and its fix: not installed, the installer's Docker step in the terminal (winget on Windows, Homebrew on macOS, Clank's installer on Linux), or where to get Docker when no command can; the snap's or rootless Docker, not supported, the commands shown and never run; Docker Engine stopped, `sudo systemctl start docker` in the terminal; Docker Desktop stopped, started by Clank through its own command; not started with the computer, `sudo systemctl enable docker`, offered and not required; no access outside the docker group, `sudo usermod -aG docker <account>` in the terminal, then nothing more where `sg` is installed (Clank takes the group at once, decision 0150), else a log out and in; no access inside it, Clank's service restarted where it started before the account joined and `sg` is installed, else the log out and in alone, which Clank does not do; no memory limits, the kernel setting, which Clank never makes; a folder the agent cannot write, how to let it. An administrator command runs only in the terminal. Without the service, the model and the folder are not known, and the service is to start, unless this account may not use Docker yet: a service started then would keep the session's groups. An item waits while one it needs is not done. Ready says what was set up and what each thing is for. A client runs only the commands the checklist builds, the docker group's for its own account alone (`runnable`), whoever gave it the checklist. The console opens on Setup only for what This folder does not put right on its way to the agent (`opens`, decision 0148): a Docker Desktop only stopped and a sandbox image not built for this Clank are This folder's to do.

- with everything done it is ready and says what each thing is for
- docker not installed on linux offers clanks installer in the terminal with administrator rights
- docker not installed on windows and macos offers their own installer in the terminal
- without a command to install docker the fix says where to get it and clank runs nothing
- docker from the snap or rootless docker is not supported and its fix is shown never run
- an old docker desktop is told to update
- a stopped docker engine is started with sudo systemctl in the terminal
- a failed docker service says where to read why and starts it again
- a stopped docker desktop is started by clank through its own command without administrator rights
- docker engine not started with the computer offers systemctl enable and is not required
- where the platform says nothing of the boot there is no such item
- an account outside the docker group joins it in the terminal then logs out and in
- with sg an account that joins the docker group has nothing more to do
- with sg a service that started before the account joined is restarted by the setup
- an account in the group whose session predates it logs out and in and clank runs nothing
- docker without memory limits names the kernel setting which clank never makes
- a folder the agent cannot write says how to let it and clank does not change it
- a folder that cannot be a workspace says why and where to go
- without the service the model and the folder are not known and the service is to start
- items wait while docker is not installed or cannot be used
- while this account may not use docker the service waits for it not for docker to run
- the image not built is to do and one being built shows its operation
- the model says the gpu found or that the cloud is the way
- a model that answered says where it runs
- clanks vs code window is optional and says how to get it
- only a fix that runs a command in the terminal needs administrator rights
- the view gives each items fix with its command as a list
- the console opens on setup only for what this folder does not put right on its way
- a client runs only the commands the checklist builds the docker groups for its own account alone
- every command the checklist builds is one a client runs

### `tests/components/agents/test_show_files_tool.py`

Design 0018 §7.1: Clank's own MCP server in the container gives the agent `show_files(paths, why)`, which opens files of the folder in the owner's VS Code window: a request to the gateway's `POST /agents/show` naming the workspace the server serves, the paths as the model gave them (one path given as a string is a list of one) and why. Its description is an order: when the owner asks to see a file, open it and stop, without reading or checking it. The answer is what the service said; a refusal is the tool's error, in the service's words; a server that does not know its workspace asks nothing. It writes nothing in the folder, so it is marked read-only.

- show files is listed read only and its description tells the model to open and stop
- show files asks the gateway naming its workspace and answers what the service said
- one path given as a string is sent as a list of one
- a refusal is the tools error and a server that does not know its workspace asks nothing

### `tests/components/agents/test_showing.py`

Design 0018 §7.1: the service opens files the agent names in the owner's VS Code window. Each path is named from the workspace's folder or under `/workspace`; the files of the folder are opened (the event `agents.shown`, with the workspace, its folder, the files and why, which the window follows), and the answer names each path that is not a file of the folder (outside it, or missing), and tells the model the owner looks at the files themselves. With no file of the folder, too many, no workspace of that name, or no VS Code window following the service, nothing is opened and the answer says why. Through the API, `POST /agents/show` is the agent's: its event's actor is the agent's session.

- the files named are opened as an event the window follows
- paths outside the folder or missing are named and the rest are opened
- nothing is opened with no file too many no workspace or no window
- the route is the agents and knows whether a vs code window follows

### `tests/components/agents/test_vscode.py`

Design 0004 §5: Clank's VS Code window is a separate instance of the same program, its settings and extensions under CLANK_HOME/vscode, so the main one is untouched. Opening a folder in it sets it up the first time (its settings: telemetry and VS Code's own AI features off, a title that says Clank, the agent opened beside the files; Clank's extension and the curated ones), installs only what is missing afterward, says an extension that cannot be installed and opens the folder all the same; without VS Code it fails saying how to name it. On Windows VS Code's own program is run, never its `code.cmd`, so no folder's name goes through cmd.exe.

- the instances settings are added once and the owners own are kept
- long lines wrap in every editor and in a changes diff unless the owner chose otherwise
- on windows vs codes own program is run never its cmd file
- opening a folder sets the instance up once then opens the folder
- an extension that cannot be installed is said and the folder opens all the same
- without vs code opening fails saying how to name it
- clanks vs code window opens a file of the folder
- a file named with a drive or a root or out of the folder is refused

### `tests/components/agents/test_vscode_release.py`

Design 0028 §6: Clank's VS Code window without building its extension. In a release install (no extension's sources in Clank's folder), opening a folder takes the extension that the GitHub release of Clank's own version carries: it is downloaded, its SHA-256 checked against the release's SHA256SUMS, kept under CLANK_HOME/vscode/cache and installed; the next time, the kept copy is used without asking GitHub, once its SHA-256 is checked again. A file whose SHA-256 is not the release's is refused, neither kept nor installed. No such release, a release without the extension or its sums, or no answer from GitHub is said with what to do, and the folder opens all the same. A clone keeps its own build and never asks GitHub. GitHub is the fake of `tests/fakes/github.py`, never the network.

- a releases extension is downloaded checked kept and the kept copy used next time
- a file whose sha256 is not the releases is refused and not kept
- no release one without the extension or its sums and no network each say why and what to do
- in a release install the releases extension is installed and its kept copy used the next time
- a refused extension no release or no network is said and the folder opens all the same
- a clone uses its own build and never asks github

### `tests/components/agents/test_watch_channel.py`

Design 0023 §4 (decision 0140) and design 0007 §4: the service's request channel to a workspace's watcher, run against the real bridge on this machine and a fake OpenCode server. A request written on the watcher's input is answered with its id, its answer the bridge's own (a refusal included), and the review never hears it. The watch names each server it follows (`server`), and the watcher asks its bridge `hello` once it synced: a bridge from before the channel (in a container made before Clank's programs were mounted) never answers, so its requests go through a process of their own at once, never after the request's wait, the watcher is left as it is, and the log says so once. The bridge answers each request within its deadline, in words when OpenCode does not answer, and a slow request holds up no other; a request for a server that ended is answered at once that it is gone. A watcher whose bridge answered `hello` and then does not answer within `REQUEST_SECONDS` is ended, so it is started again, and the request raises `Unanswered`; a workspace without a watcher alive and synced raises `NoChannel`, so the caller sends the request through a process of its own; so does a request made from the watcher's own reader thread (a line heard), which could never see its answer. A workspace whose watcher cannot start leaves the others watched, and is said once.

- a request goes through the watcher and its answer comes back with the bridges result
- a watch names each server it follows and answers hello with its deadline
- a request for a server that ended is answered at once that it is gone
- a request opencode does not answer is answered within the deadline in words and holds up no other
- a request from the watchers own reader says there is no channel since it would wait for itself
- a watcher whose bridge reads no request sends none and is left watching
- a watcher that stops answering is ended and started again and the request says so
- without a watcher alive and synced the request says there is no channel
- a workspace whose watcher cannot start leaves the others watched and is said once

### `tests/components/agents/test_watch_for_marks.py`

Design 0012 §8 and §11 step 8 (decision 0053): the marks the Watch for rules leave, kept in the service, each taken once; what each rule then does follows its setting in `config/monitor.json`: the mark alone, pause, tell the phone, tell the model and go on, telling the model at most `notes_in_a_row` times in a row for one rule in a session, after which the rule marks, pauses and tells the phone; the count starts again at the owner's own message, and at a step that finished cleanly after the rule's last note (decision 0083). An action that cannot run (the pause, step 11's; the note when no Guide is given) tells the phone instead and says why (decision 0082). A mark on a step, or on a note, from before Clank watched does nothing more. Marks seen leave the count of those not seen; a mark of an ask no longer waiting leaves the phone's list. Design 0012 §3: a subagent's mark never notes the subagent (the Guide acts on the root session), and tells the phone instead; a mark dropped to keep the newest is never made, nor acted on, again; a session whose flow the service no longer keeps is forgotten.

- a rule marks once and each action follows its setting
- a rule turned off marks nothing
- tell the model stops at its cap then marks pauses and tells the phone
- the cap starts again after the owners own message
- a step finished cleanly after a note starts the count again
- an action not built yet tells the phone instead and says why
- a mark on a step from before clank watched does nothing more
- a note not read from before clank watched does nothing more
- a note waiting when clank joined whose run ended after acts
- a mark of an ask no longer waiting leaves the phones list
- a session whose flow is no longer kept is forgotten
- marks seen leave the count of those not seen
- a subagents mark tells the phone and never the subagent
- a mark dropped to keep the newest is never made again

### `tests/components/agents/test_watch_for_rules.py`

Design 0012 §8 and §11 step 8 (decision 0053): each Watch for rule is a pure function over a session's flow, and of the facts of the slot its requests reach; it fires on a synthetic flow made for it and stays quiet on a clean one, and each mark it leaves says why, naming what it rests on. A rule over a stretch of calls marks the call where a streak reached its count, and again only after the streak ended; a rule whose fact is not known stays quiet. The flows are made from OpenCode's shapes by the bridge's own functions (plan s15's `tests/support/flowlines.py`).

- the cases cover every rule and the loop guards words are the gateways
- each rule fires on its synthetic flow and says why
- the question tool waiting is a mark of its own and doom loop is left to its rule
- every rule stays quiet on a clean flow
- a streak is marked where it reached its count and again only after it ended
- a rule whose fact is not known stays quiet
- an answer within a tenth of the output limit is marked naming the limit
- a call waiting for you is said by what it touches never by its raw input

### `tests/components/agents/test_watch_for_service.py`

Design 0012 §8 and §11 step 8 (decision 0053): the Watch for rules run in the service whenever a session's flow changes, so they act while no console is open, and only then (an unchanged flow is not read again); the facts they read are those of the agent role's slot (design 0012 §4: whatever model is loaded); the service answers the marks, how many are not seen and marking them seen, and each flow's answer carries its own marks.

- the rules run when a flow changes while no console is open
- the routes list the marks mark them seen and a flow carries its own
- the facts come from the agent roles slot

### `tests/components/agents/test_watch_for_settings.py`

Design 0012 §8 and §11 step 8 (decision 0053): what each Watch for rule does is the owner's to set, in a readable settings file, `config/monitor.json`, a settings kind with its schema: mark it, pause the session, tell the phone, or tell the model and go on, telling the model at most twice in a row. Its defaults are §8's table.

- the rules defaults are the designs table
- a rule changed in its file is read and an unknown action is refused
- a rule given in part keeps its own defaults for the rest
- the schema shows each rules own defaults

### `tests/components/agents/test_work.py`

Design 0004 §1 and §4: making a workspace's agent ready writes the program's configuration into the workspace's running container (the gateway at the edge, the running preset's limits, or modest ones said when no model runs) and answers the command that starts it there; a workspace that is not there, or whose container does not run, is refused saying how to open it. `clank work` starts Docker when it is stopped, opens the folder's workspace, loads the slot's last configuration that worked when no model runs, and runs the agent in this terminal.

- prepare writes the programs configuration and answers its command
- prepare refuses a workspace that is not there or does not run
- prepare writes every file in one go and reads dockers limits once in a while
- clank work starts docker opens the folder and runs the agent here
- work never asks a docker the system runs to start
- code never asks a docker the system runs to start
- with no model running clank loads the slots last good one and says why it cannot
- with no model running and none that worked clank loads the preset the runtimes settings name
- clank code gets the folder ready then opens it in clanks vs code window
- clank code over ssh gets the folder ready and says the command for the other computer
- clank code in a remote ssh terminal opens the other computers vs code

### `tests/components/agents/test_workspace_key_config.py`

Design 0016 §9 and §10 (decisions 0075 and 0111): each workspace's OpenCode sends its workspace's own key to the gateway, never `local`, and so does Clank's MCP server in its container, whether a cloud provider was ever agreed or not (the agent's calls to Notion, its flow and the agents' part are refused without it); a workspace allowed the cloud gets no MCP server marked local only (held back, saying why) and Clank's server lists no Notion tool there; a workspace kept on this machine gets them all; and the gateway's routes carry the names of the tools kept from the cloud: Clank's Notion tools always, and each server marked local, as OpenCode names them, once the agents component gave them (until then the routes say so, and the gateway sends nothing to the cloud).

- before any cloud provider is agreed opencode and clanks server send the workspaces own key
- opencode and clanks server send the workspaces own key
- a workspace allowed the cloud gets no server marked local and no notion
- a workspace kept on this machine gets them all
- the gateways routes carry the tools kept from the cloud
- clanks server sends the key and lists no notion tool when told so

### `tests/components/flows/test_accept_command.py`

Design 0013 §4 and §5: a box's *accept when* may also be a command, which the runner runs in the workspace's container, in its folder, after the box's session ended and its deliverables were written: it passes when it exits 0, and the box is done; otherwise the box fails, saying the command and how it ended, and its next try's first message has the end of the command's output. The command and all it said are kept in the try's folder; a command that runs past the box's time is ended in the container and fails the box, and one killed says it ran past its time or out of memory; a box that wrote nothing does not run it. The owner's stop, a cancel from the Operations page and the service's stop end a command still running, in the container, at once (§6: a stopped box's try counts as stopped); a deliverable the command rewrote is kept as the command left it, its copy and fingerprint agreeing. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder; the command's shell, on Linux, against a real `sh` and `timeout`.

- a box whose accept command exits 0 is done the command run in the container after its session
- a box whose accept command exits 1 fails saying the command and its next try has the end of its output
- a command that runs past the boxs time fails the box
- a box that wrote nothing does not run its accept command
- a stop while the accept command runs ends it in the container and its try counts as stopped
- a cancel from the operations page while the accept command runs ends it in the container
- the services stop while the accept command runs ends it in the container at once
- a deliverable the command rewrote is kept as the command left it
- the commands shell ends it and every process it started when asked
- a command killed says it ran past its time and did not end or ran out of memory

### `tests/components/flows/test_box_jobs.py`

Design 0013 §17 (decision 0132): the box at work starts a long computation as a job with `flow_job`, one at a time, within its box's minutes; Clank starts it in the workspace's container and keeps it on the box's try, and says how its end comes back. A job asked for while no box works, a second while one runs, or one longer than its box may run is refused, saying why; the route answers 409 with those words. A box whose turn ends while its job runs waits on it, its clock stopped: when the job ends (its exit written, Clank ending it past its minutes, or its process gone) the same session is sent how it ended, its time and its log's last lines, and the box goes on to its end. Stopping the run ends its job.

- the box at work starts a job kept on its try and is told to end its turn
- a second job while one runs is refused
- a job longer than its box may run is refused
- a job while no box works is refused and the route says why
- a box whose turn ends while its job runs waits and is sent its end in the same session
- a job past its minutes is ended by clank and the box told
- a job whose process is gone without an exit is said so
- stopping the run ends its job

### `tests/components/flows/test_box_server.py`

Design 0013 §4 and §5 (decision 0060): the flow's own OpenCode server is started with the box's context and output limits, those of the model the main slot runs for it (Clank's configuration, as written when the server started), and started again only when a box's limits differ (whether its model reads images among them): the old server is ended once no session at work on it is left, and the run log says so; the run keeps the limits the slot has once that wait is over. Each box gets its own MCP servers on the flow's server alone, the window's untouched; a box naming a server the workspace's agent does not get fails at a gate, saying why, while one that fails to connect is said in its log. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder, the main slot's model given as the gateway's routes say it.

- the server is started with the boxs limits and again only when they differ
- a server to start again waits for a session at work on it
- a server started again after a wait keeps the limits the slot has once it is free
- a box whose model reads images where the servers did not gets the server started again
- each box gets its mcp servers on the flows server the windows untouched
- a box naming an mcp server the workspaces agent does not get fails at a gate
- an mcp server that fails to connect is said in the boxs log and the box still starts

### `tests/components/flows/test_building.py`

Design 0013 §9 and §15 (decisions 0097, 0099 and 0101): a planning, the owner's talk with the model that builds a flow. Its first message, kept beside it, names the workspace, the flow, each preset of the main slot with its window, the owner's three answers, the brief, `flow_draft`, the template's model and Clank's template without its plan's gate; one planning is open in a workspace at a time, a newer one setting the older aside only when told (else refused, the open one never overwritten, through the API too); a name an existing flow has is refused. A draft (`flow_draft`) that breaks a rule is refused naming each problem with its field, with the check's warnings; one that keeps them is written as the flow's file with Clank's settings, and the owner is asked "flow built: look, then r to run" with the next flow's answers; a draft again replaces it and asks again, unless the owner changed it since; with no planning open, or once its flow ran, a draft is refused; *Run it now* starts the flow, and a run started otherwise withdraws the question. A draft reaches the service as the agent's, its refusal a 409 in the model's words. The planning's session is started on the workspace's OpenCode window, sent to the planner with the first message and titled after the flow, once that window answers: here, the window whose command the planning gives; in VS Code, the window running, else the one VS Code's extension prepares; a window that never answers fails the planning, said. The owner opens a planning through the API; the agent cannot. A workspace whose container runs an older sandbox image is refused, naming the fix, when a planning opens and when it is attached. A session the window does not show stands, the planning saying to pick it from its list. Each draft is kept with its number, time, state and problems, the latest's values beside the planning in Clank's state; a new planning starts with none, though the one before of its name had drafts. A planning opened to attach takes no window running; attach prepares its window (another closed first, said), the waiting planning starting there, a talking one shown there, none started; one that failed, with no run since, tried again there; one whose session is still starting refused, saying to wait, no other window prepared; with none open, refused saying how to open one.

- a plannings first message names the flow the presets the answers and the template
- a newer planning in the workspace sets the older aside
- a name an existing flow has is refused
- a draft that breaks a rule is refused naming each problem with its field and the warnings
- a brief left from before the planning is refused until it is written with the owner
- a field the draft should not have is named with the fields of what it is in
- a draft that keeps the rules is written with clanks settings and the owner is told
- a draft written again replaces the flow and asks again unless the owner changed it
- a draft with no planning open or once its flow ran is refused
- run it now starts the built flow and a run started otherwise withdraws the question
- the draft route is the agents its refusal a 409 in the models words
- here the planning gives the windows command and its session starts on that window once it answers
- a session the window does not show stands and the planning says to pick it
- in vs code the window running takes the session
- in vs code with no window the planning waits for the one vs code prepares
- a window that never answers fails the planning said
- a planning opened to attach takes no window running and waits for attach
- attach prepares the window closing another first and the waiting planning starts there
- attach to a planning already talking shows its session and starts no other
- attach with no planning open is refused saying how to open one
- the owner opens a planning through the api and the agent cannot
- the flow view follows a flow built and not run yet with its drafts warnings
- a container made from an older image is refused naming the fix
- attach to a container made from an older image is refused naming the fix
- each draft is kept with its number time state and problems the latest beside the planning
- a new planning starts with no draft though the one before of its name had drafts
- attach tries a planning that failed again and its session starts on the new window
- a planning that failed before a run is not tried again
- a draft refused since its flow changed or ran is kept in the history too
- attach while its session is starting says to wait and prepares no other window
- a planning open is never overwritten a new one sets it aside only when told
- the api sets the open planning aside only when asked

### `tests/components/flows/test_built_run.py`

Design 0013 §2, §10 and §15 (decision 0098): a flow built with the model runs on its own until its final review. Its first box, a YOU, is done by itself when the brief written with the model is in the folder, at each run, its event saying so; without the brief, or with the brief as it was before the planning began, it asks, as any YOU; a flow not built asks for its brief as before. A built flow runs from its brief to its last gate asking nothing else: its VERIFY passes on, the tasks its DECOMPOSE proposes are accepted on their own, every one whatever the flow's cap, and run in auto mode, each change they ask to make answered at once while the workspace stays in review, the INTEGRATE, and only then the owner's review. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a built flows run starts past its you box when its brief is in the folder
- a brief left as it was before the planning does not pass and the you box asks
- without its brief a built flows you box asks
- a flow not built asks for its brief as before
- a built flow accepts every task its decompose proposes whatever its cap
- a built flow runs from its brief to its last gate asking nothing else

### `tests/components/flows/test_cloud_box.py`

Design 0016 §7 and §9 (decisions 0062 and 0112): a flow's box may name a cloud preset. Before the runner switches the main slot to it, the workspace is asked whether its agent may use the cloud model, the run saying it waits for the answer: allowed, the slot switches as for any preset; kept on this machine, the box fails at a gate saying why and how to allow it. `clank flow prepare` prices each box's first message at its model's input price. Run with the fake OpenRouter, the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a box on a cloud preset waits for the workspaces answer then switches
- a box on a cloud preset in a workspace kept local fails at a gate saying why
- prepare prices each boxs first message at its models input price

### `tests/components/flows/test_deliverables.py`

Design 0013 §8: a deliverable counts only when written during its try, so a file left from before does not; and a plan's progress (each step's tick, its Log's lines, its header's `status`) is told apart from the rest of the file (the steps' own words, its other sections and its other header lines).

- a file written during a try counts and one left from before does not
- a plans ticks log and status are its progress and the rest its other lines
- a plans fingerprint keeps its progress apart and another file has none

### `tests/components/flows/test_diffs.py`

Design 0013 §8 and §10: each try's deliverables are copied into its folder, and two tries' copies of a file are compared: a unified diff, and the lines changed counted as board D3 says them ("changed since try 1: 7 lines", a replaced block counted by its longer side). The runner compares a box's last two tries that kept the file, or the two asked for; a try that kept the file by its fingerprint only, or wrote no such file, says so; and a gate shows, beside each file it shows, how many of its lines changed since the try before, counted outside the service's event loop and within seconds even for a large file of repeated lines (design 0004's no stutter). Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- two copies are compared the lines changed counted as board d3 says them
- a large file of repeated lines is compared within seconds
- the runner compares a boxs last two tries and a gate shows what changed since the try before
- a gate counts what changed outside the services event loop
- a try that kept the file by its fingerprint only says so

### `tests/components/flows/test_first_message.py`

Design 0013 §2, §3, §5 and §9 (decisions 0060 and 0085): a box's first message is, in order, its type's instructions, the flow's brief, its prompt, the files it sees (a text file whole up to a size, a setting; a larger or binary one by its path and size), what the boxes before it delivered (an INTEGRATE: every deliverable before it, by the box that wrote it; a DECOMPOSE's task: those of the tasks before it), its deliverable and *accept when*, and on a later try what happened to the last one; each file says the box it came from. Design 0013 §17: the corpus's notes, each by its path and first line, when side tasks wrote any.

- a decomposes task is named what the tasks before it delivered

- a deliverable already in the folder is named and must be written again
- the corpuss notes are listed by path and first line when there are any

Design 0013 §2 and §5 (decisions 0085 and 0086): every box a model runs is prepared for its fresh session. Its first message hands the flow's brief whether or not its `sees` lists it, and once; then, in order: the type's instructions, where it sits in the flow, the brief, its task, the notes of the boxes before it, the files it sees, what the boxes before it delivered, its deliverable and its own handoff note, and its last try.

- a plan a task and an integrate are asked for their handoff note after what they are accepted when
- a verify and a decompose write no note a decomposes summary is its note
- each types instructions say how it hands on what it knows

### `tests/components/flows/test_flow_view.py`

Design 0013 §12 and §14 step 6 (with §3, §8, §9 and §10): what the Monitor's Flow view shows of a flow, made in the service from the flow, its latest run, the run's proposals and its events, so the console draws it knowing none of the runner's rules. Board D1's moment says each box's state, its tries and verdicts, its files and when it last ended; the lines say which ran, which runs now and which not yet, the fail line its findings; The brief, checked lists the brief's lines and every *accept when*, the newest verdict on each; Deliverables, Ahead, Models in this flow and Waiting for you are said; proposals waiting join where they would join, marked proposed; a folder's Monitor shows the flow of the active run, else the one run last, else the first; the run log (`h`) is the run's events, oldest first. - the brief checked lists its lines and every accept when with the newest verdict on each
- deliverables ahead models and waiting for you are said
- proposals waiting join where they would join marked proposed
- a folders monitor shows the active run else the last run else the first flow
- a flow not run yet shows every box waiting
- the view is pure and its version moves only with what it shows
- the routes give the view and the run log oldest first
- the next flow is said once made else where its brief will come from
- ahead says each switch of the main slot and how long its preset last took to come in
- a brief that is a link out of the folder is not read
- a try that rewrites a file with the same bytes is said to have written it as the runner counts it
- how long each preset took is read once and again only when an operation has finished
- a folders monitor shows the flow running before one queued behind it
- a flow named without its workspace is refused saying how to name it
- a brief in a folder linked out of the workspaces folder is not read

### `tests/components/flows/test_flows.py`

Design 0013 §3, §6, §7, §11 and §14 (decisions 0061, 0063 and 0066): the flows component reads every flow, kept by workspace in Clank's home, and checks it: the file's own rules, then what needs other records (a preset that does not exist is refused naming those that do, when the flow is saved and when its file is read; a flow is for the workspace whose folder keeps it); a file is checked as it is on disk now, and one that is gone is said to be; a good one says the switches of the main slot a run of it makes; and its `/flows/` routes list, show and check flows.

- the flows are listed by workspace with their boxes and a file that does not read with its place
- a flow is shown by its name in its workspace or by its title
- an unknown preset is refused naming those that exist
- a flow is for the workspace whose folder keeps it
- a file is checked as it is on disk now before the service reads it again
- a flow whose file is gone is listed shown and checked saying so until the service reads again
- a flow that does not exist is not found naming where flows are kept

### `tests/components/flows/test_handoff.py`

Design 0013 §2, §5 and §8 (decision 0086): a PLAN, a TASK and an INTEGRATE end by writing a short handoff note in the folder, `.clank/handoff/<flow>/<box>.md`; a note written during its try is copied beside the try as `handoff.md` and kept on the try, one left from before is not; a box whose deliverable is right is done without its note, its end saying it left none; `.clank/handoff/` is kept out of git. Every later box but a VERIFY is handed the notes of the boxes before it, in order: a VERIFY's verdict and a DECOMPOSE's summary stand for theirs. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a note written during its try is copied beside it and kept on the try
- a box whose note is missing or left from before is done and its end says it left none
- the handoff folder is kept out of git once a box that writes a note starts
- the boxes after are handed the notes before them and a verify none
- a decomposes summary is handed on as its note and a task that left none is said

### `tests/components/flows/test_jobs.py`

Design 0013 §17 (decision 0132): a job, a long computation a box starts with `flow_job`, run detached in the workspace's container. Its folder in the workspace (`.clank/jobs/<flow>/<run>/<box>-<n>/`) holds its command, its process id, its log and, once it ends, its exit code, so the service reads its state as files; Clank ends one past its minutes or when its run stops. The scripts run in the container's shell: tried for real on Linux, and through the fake platform elsewhere.

- a jobs folder is named by its flow run box and number
- its state is read from its files
- a job that ended says its status and the end of its log
- how a job ended is said in words
- the start and end scripts run a command detached and end it with its group
- a job started in the workspace writes its files and is ended by clank
- a job whose workspace does not run does not start
- its time is said in words
- the files it wrote are the folders newer ones but its own and gits

### `tests/components/flows/test_model_per_box.py`

Design 0013 §7 (decisions 0062 and 0063): a box's model is a preset, switched into the main slot before the box starts when the slot does not run it, by the runtime's own switch: a flow alternating two presets switches exactly before the boxes that need it, and a VERIFY runs on the preset of the box it checks with no switch. No switch starts while a request is in flight on the slot, while the gateway does not say what is, while another operation changes the slot, or while the owner's own session in the workspace works: the run says what it waits for; a switch refused as the slot was taken a moment ago is started once it is free. A switch that fails returns the slot to the last configuration that worked and leaves the box failed at a gate. A run stopped, or a box canceled, while its box waits starts no switch, and a wait the service's stop cut short is not said after the restart; a run stopped while the slot switches lets the switch end, and the window is still told. After a switch, the owner's window is told, a preset too small for one of the owner's sessions named, and the window is left as it is. The switches a flow makes say how long each preset's last whole switch took. Run with the fake engine (two presets of a tiny model, real processes of this machine's CPU), the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a flow alternating two presets switches exactly before the boxes that need it
- after a switch the owners window is told naming a preset too small and left as it is
- no switch starts while a request is in flight on the slot and the run says so
- no switch starts while the owners own session in the workspace works
- a failed switch returns the slot and leaves the box failed at a gate
- a run stopped while its box waits starts no switch
- a wait cut short by the service stopping is not said after the restart
- a run stopped while the slot switches lets the switch end and still tells the window
- a box waits for a load that changes the slot and says so then switches
- a switch refused because the slot was taken a moment ago is started once it is free
- a box canceled while it waits starts no switch and its try is stopped
- no switch starts while the gateway does not say what it has in flight
- a flows switches say how long the last whole switch to each preset took

### `tests/components/flows/test_models.py`

Design 0013 §7 (decisions 0062 and 0063): a box's model is a preset: its own, else the flow's; a VERIFY's is the preset of the box it checks unless it names another, so a check and its loop cost no switch; a box that names none runs on what the main slot runs. A run of a flow switches the main slot exactly before each box whose preset is not the one loaded by then, along the order its boxes run in.

- a box runs on its own preset else the flows
- a verify runs on the preset of the box it checks unless it names another
- a box that names no preset runs on what the slot runs
- a flow alternating two presets switches before each box that needs the other

### `tests/components/flows/test_next_flow.py`

Design 0013 §2, §3, §6, §10 and §11 (decisions 0058 and 0091): once a flow's last gate is approved, the next flow is made from Clank's template with its last INTEGRATE's brief (its `NEXT.md`, else the last of two or more files), named `<name>-<n>` and titled by the brief's first heading, linked both ways (`follows`, `next`), and the owner is asked whether to run it now; nothing starts without their answer unless the flow's `next_flow` says so. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- integrates brief is its next md else the last of two or more files else none
- the next flows name counts on from this ones
- its title is the briefs first heading else this flows title next
- the next flow is clanks template with its workspace model words and where it came from
- approving the last gate makes the next flow linked both ways and asks starting nothing
- a run stopped at its last gate or an integrate that wrote no brief makes none
- an integrate that wrote no brief makes none saying why
- run it now starts the next flow whose first run copies its brief in
- later starts nothing and clank flow run starts it then
- a brief file the owner changed since is kept as they left it
- a flow set to run starts its next flow unasked and one set off makes none

### `tests/components/flows/test_orchestrator.py`

Design 0013 §16 (decisions 0123-0124): the orchestrator. While a flow's orchestrator is on, a checkpoint follows each box a model ran, and each question the run asks: a fresh Plan-mode session titled `<flow> · orchestrator · <n>`, handed the box's report, the question with its answers, the run so far, the budget left and the ledger, which decides with `flow_decide`. Its answer to a question is given through the questions API, by the orchestrator, and the run goes on as it would for the owner's; after a box it may go on with a note for the next box, change a box not yet run, or stop. Clank keeps the ledger beside the run (`orchestrator.md`): the facts and plan, then each decision. A decision is refused, saying why, when no checkpoint works in the workspace, when its choice is not one it may make, when it needs more changes than are left, and when the run has stalled and it would go on. The owner may answer a question first; the last gate is the owner's when the flow says so; a checkpoint that ends without deciding is reminded once, in its session, to call `flow_decide`, and leaves the question to the owner when it still does not. Its word for the owner (`tell_user`) is a question of high urgency the run does not wait for, whose answers let the owner pause or stop the run; after a box it may pause the run, and at a question leave it to the owner, each telling the owner why; and in its role `watch` it reads each box, goes on or pauses, and decides no question. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a checkpoint follows a box handed its report the question none the budget and the ledger
- its answer to a gate is the orchestrators and the run goes on with its note told to the next box
- a change edits a box not yet run and counts against its limit
- a change to a box that ran or past the limit is refused saying why
- once stalled it may not go on
- the owner may answer first and its later answer is set aside
- the last gate is the owners when the flow says so
- a checkpoint that ends without deciding is reminded once and its decision then counts
- a checkpoint that ends without deciding when reminded leaves the question to the owner
- its word for the owner reaches them at once and the run goes on
- answering its word with pause pauses the run and with stop stops it
- after a box it may pause the run telling the owner why
- at a question it may leave it to the owner telling them why
- watching it reads each box and leaves every question to the owner
- at a failed boxs question it may send the work back to an earlier box with a note
- a decision while no checkpoint works is refused
- the decide route refuses with 409 saying why
- a failures class says whether to try again as it was

### `tests/components/flows/test_planning_view.py`

Design 0013 §12 and §15 (decision 0101): the Flow view shows a planning while it is open. A folder's view follows its workspace's open planning (waiting, opening, talking, or failed with no run started since) before a flow that ran; its steps say it started, what it waits for (`clank flow attach`), the session's tool calls so far, that it waits for the owner's answer, `BRIEF.md` written with its time, and each draft refused with its problems or accepted; the latest draft is drawn with the view's own layout and marked as a draft even when refused, as far as it reads; with none yet, nothing is drawn; once a draft is accepted, the view is the built flow.

- the folders view follows an open planning before a flow that ran and says what it waits for
- its steps say the calls so far the answer it waits for the brief and each draft
- a draft that breaks a flows rules is drawn as far as it reads
- once a draft is accepted the view is the built flow

### `tests/components/flows/test_prepare.py`

Design 0013 §5, §6 and §7 (decision 0087): `clank flow prepare`'s preview shows each box a model runs with its first message as it would be sent now, from the files as they are now and the active run's state, so it is the message the runner then sends; each with its size in tokens, an estimate labeled as one unless the engine counted it, against the window of the preset the box runs on (for a box naming none, the preset a box before it switches the main slot to, else the main slot's, else not known) and its share of it; one box shown alone; an owner's box has no first message. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- the preview of a box is the message the runner then sends
- each box says its size an estimate labeled as one against its presets window
- a box naming no preset takes the main slots else its window is not known
- a box naming no preset after one that switches the main slot takes that ones window
- one box is shown alone and an owners box has no first message
- the route serves the preview
- a box whose first message is over a quarter of its window is warned of
- a file a box sees or shows that nothing gives it is warned of and no other

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals.py`

Design 0013 §9 (decision 0064): a box's model proposes boxes with `flow_propose_box`, which the service gives to the box at work in the workspace its MCP server names, while its try runs. Each proposal is checked when staged and refused with its reason, so the model can fix it: a deliverable outside the folder, or one agents load as settings or instructions, an unknown type, an `after` naming no box, an unknown preset, an empty prompt, and one that would break the flow's rules (in the rule's words). A proposal that passes is kept in its try's batch with an id of its own, waiting for the owner: it is not in the flow. A proposal while no box works, or from another workspace's server, is refused, and reaches no flow. Each proposal is an event with its actor, which says its mode of its own. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a decomposes proposal is staged in its trys batch with an id of its own not in the flow
- a proposal outside the folder is refused with its reason
- a deliverable agents load as settings or instructions is refused
- an unknown type an after naming no box an unknown preset or an empty prompt is refused saying why
- a proposal that would break the flows rules is refused in the rules words
- a proposal while no box works is refused
- a proposal from one workspaces server reaches only that workspaces flow
- each proposal is an event with its actor

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_again.py`

Design 0013 §9 (decision 0064): a DECOMPOSE that runs again proposes anew. Its new proposals replace the tasks it added that have not started in the run, when the first of them joins the flow; those that ran stay. The proposals of its earlier try still waiting are set aside (replaced) when its next try begins, their question withdrawn, as are those of a box skipped. A run that ends withdraws its batches' questions and leaves their proposals to decide from the command line; accepted then, they join the flow's file for its next run. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a decompose run again replaces only its tasks that have not started
- the proposals of its earlier try still waiting are set aside when it tries again
- a decompose run again from its box while its proposals wait withdraws their question
- skipping a decompose sets its proposals aside and the flow goes on without them
- taking a failed decompose as it is asks about what it proposed
- a run that ends withdraws its batchs question and leaves its proposals to decide

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_auto.py`

Design 0013 §9 (decision 0064) and design 0002 §2.2: auto-accept, a flow's setting off by default. With it on, a proposal that passes the checks joins the flow's file at once, marked as accepted by the setting, up to its cap in a run; past the cap, proposals wait for the owner. Auto-accept writes the flow's file through the settings like any change, so an edit the owner makes on the version it has just replaced is refused, with both versions shown. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- with auto accept on a proposal joins the flow at once marked as accepted by the setting
- auto accept stops at its cap and the rest wait for the owner
- an owners edit made on the version auto accept has just replaced is refused with both versions shown

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_decided.py`

Design 0013 §2, §6, §9 and §10 (decisions 0061 and 0064): a DECOMPOSE ends by submitting its proposals (`flow_submit`), which asks the owner one question about them, saying each one's mode of its own (in Auto, its changes are accepted without review); one that ends without submitting fails and waits at a gate. The run waits after the DECOMPOSE until each proposal is decided: the owner accepts, edits then accepts, or rejects each, or all at once, from the command line first, or answers the question from any client (the phone accepts or rejects them all). Accepting all puts T1-T4 in order between the DECOMPOSE and the INTEGRATE in the flow's file, each marked with its run and the DECOMPOSE, and they run one at a time in that order; a rejected proposal never runs; an edited prompt is the one sent. The agent cannot accept. Each decision is an event with its actor. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a decompose that ends without submitting fails and waits at a gate
- submitting asks the owner one question about the proposals
- accept all puts t1 to t4 in order between decompose and integrate and they run one at a time
- a rejected proposal never runs
- an edited prompt is the one sent
- an edit is checked as a proposal is
- the question answered as the phone answers it accepts them all
- the question answered reject all lets the flow go on without them
- the agent cannot accept
- each decision is an event with its actor
- proposals the flow no longer takes wait saying why and are asked about again

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_followed.py`

Design 0013 §6 and §9 (decision 0064): deciding a DECOMPOSE's proposals never hangs the service while a client follows its events, as the console, VS Code and the phone do: each event is handed to the service's event loop and its teller waits for it there, so the driver, which holds the run at the DECOMPOSE on that loop, never waits for what a decision holds. The owner accepting through the API's route, and the phone accepting them all by answering the question about them, both while the run is held, go on at once, and the tasks run next. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- the owner accepting while a client follows the events never hangs the service
- the phone accepting all while a client follows the events never hangs the service

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_join.py`

Design 0013 §3 and §9 (decisions 0057 and 0064): the boxes a model proposes join the flow's file once accepted, each marked with the run and the box that proposed them. TASKs proposed after a DECOMPOSE fan out from it and join at the box after it, one after another in the order proposed, whatever order they are accepted in; one task alone is a chain between them; a box proposed after a box of the chain goes between it and the next, and a VERIFY checks the box it follows, its fail line back to it; a task proposed later goes to the box the tasks join at, past a VERIFY that checks one of them, so the rules refuse it (a DECOMPOSE's tasks each lead to that box) rather than join it at that VERIFY. A DECOMPOSE's tasks taken out (replaced by its new proposals) leave the lines reconnected around them. Whatever breaks a rule of §3 is refused in the rule's words. Pure functions over the flow's values; nothing runs.

- tasks proposed after a decompose fan out from it and join at the box after it in the order proposed
- one task alone is a chain between the decompose and the box after it
- tasks accepted out of order keep the order proposed
- tasks proposed after a task of the decompose join its tasks right after it
- a box proposed after a box of the chain goes between it and the next
- a task proposed later is refused never joined at a verify that checks one of the tasks
- boxes proposed after a verify go on its pass line
- a verify proposed after a box checks it with its fail line back to it
- a decomposes tasks taken out leave the lines reconnected around them
- boxes joined by auto accept are marked so
- a flow that breaks a rule is refused in the rules words
- a box done by you with a model is refused naming the box

### `tests/components/flows/test_proposals_routes.py`

Design 0013 §9 (decisions 0020 and 0064), end to end through the service's routes and the gateway: a DECOMPOSE's model reads its flow (`GET /flows/read`: the flow, the box it is, each box's state, its proposals), proposes boxes and submits them through the gateway, each refusal in the service's words; its acceptance through the gateway is refused, and so is one the service hears from the client `agent`; the owner's acceptance through the API (`POST /api/v1/flows/<workspace>/<name>/proposals/accept`) joins the tasks to the flow, and `GET .../proposals` lists each batch and what waits. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a decompose proposes through the gateway and only the owner accepts
- the owners routes reject and edit a proposal

### `tests/components/flows/test_restart.py`

Design 0013 §6 (decision 0061): after a restart of the service, a box's operation is interrupted, then reconciled through the bridge: a session still working is followed and one that ended meanwhile is collected, neither starting any session again; one that is gone (its server stopped) fails its box, which waits at a gate. A gate asked before a restart still waits after it, and its answer lets the flow go on. A run stopped while its box was ending has that box's session stopped and its try ended after the restart, so it can run again from a box. The tokens a box wrote before a restart count toward its limit after it. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder; the container and its OpenCode servers go on while the service restarts.

- a restart in the middle of a box follows the session still working and starts none
- a session that ended while the service was down is collected
- a session gone with its server fails its box at a gate
- a gate asked before a restart still waits after it and its answer lets the flow go on
- a run stopped while its box was ending is settled after a restart and runs again from a box
- the tokens a box wrote before a restart count toward its limit after it

### `tests/components/flows/test_runner.py`

Design 0013 §5, §6 and §10 (decisions 0060, 0061 and 0065): the runner runs a flow in the service, one box after another: YOU boxes and gates are questions that wait for the owner; each model box is a `flows.box` operation that starts a new session on the flow's own OpenCode server, sends its first message with the box's agent and model, keeps that message as a file, follows the session to its end and collects its deliverables; a box that ends without its deliverable, or with one left from before, fails and waits at a gate; *send back* puts the owner's note, and that the box was sent back, in the box's next first message; each step is an event with its actor. A session that ended while its workspace's watcher started again is read once the watcher is back, and its box ends. The flow's next run uses the server its last run started. A gate says which try the box it sends back to runs, and when that is its last or past its tries. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a flow of you plan gate and task runs in order one new session per box
- send back puts the note in the boxs next first message
- a box that ends without its deliverable fails and waits at a gate
- a deliverable left from before does not count
- skipping a failed box goes on and stopping ends the run
- a brief not written yet is asked for again saying which file is missing
- a box whose when done waits asks before the next starts
- an idle heard before the sessions answer began does not end the box
- a box whose session ended while its watcher started again ends once the watcher is back
- the flows next run uses the server its last run started
- a gate says which try the box it sends back to runs and when it is past its tries

### `tests/components/flows/test_runner_controls.py`

Design 0013 §4, §5 and §6 (decisions 0059 and 0061): a box over its time limit, or past the tokens it may write, has its session stopped and fails; a box whose session ended while its watcher said nothing is found ended by the runner's own look at its status; a box in Auto has its session's changes accepted while the workspace stays in Review; and the owner's controls: pause lets the running box finish and starts no other until the run goes on; stop stops the running box's session and ends the run; run from here runs the box and every box after it again, as new tries, each first message saying why it runs again; and a second flow's run waits behind the first. A nudge of the runner from another thread never waits for the event loop (§6: the runner runs in the service, which nothing may hang). Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a box over its time limit has its session stopped and fails
- a box past the tokens it may write has its session stopped and fails
- a box that ended unheard is found ended by the runners own look
- a box in auto has its changes accepted while the workspace stays in review
- pause lets the running box finish and starts no other until the run goes on
- stop stops the running boxs session and ends the run
- run from here runs the box and every box after it again as new tries
- a second flows run waits behind the first and begins when it ends
- a run stopped while its box is starting starts no session
- a nudge from another thread returns at once while the event loop is busy

### `tests/components/flows/test_runner_routes.py`

Design 0013 §6 and §10 (decisions 0061 and 0065): the runner's routes, `/flows/<workspace>/<name>/run|pause|stop` and `/runs`, each recorded with the client that asked; and a gate answered from the phone, as the phone page sends it (an option, no words), lets the flow go on. The service runs in the test (its routes, its operations, the flows' runner), against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a gate answered from the phone lets the flow go on
- the routes start pause resume stop and run from a box each recorded with its client

### `tests/components/flows/test_runs.py`

Design 0013 §6 and §8 (decision 0061): a run of a flow is a readable JSON file in the run's folder, beside each try's folder; runs of a flow are numbered from 1; the boxes run along their lines, a DECOMPOSE's tasks one after another in the order of the boxes, and what comes after a box is every box later in that order. Design 0013 §10 and §14 step 7: the phone and VS Code read the runs' files from their own threads while the runner writes them, and a write waits for a read to end (Windows refuses to replace a file open for reading) rather than failing the box; a read waits for a replace to end (Windows refuses for a moment to open a file being replaced) rather than taking the run for one that does not read.

- a run is a readable json file in its folder read back the same
- runs of a flow are numbered from one and every run is found
- a file that does not read as a run is left out and said
- a try has a folder of its own under its box
- the boxes run along their lines and a decomposes tasks in the order of the boxes
- a runs file is written while another thread reads it
- a runs file is read while another thread replaces it

### `tests/components/flows/test_side_work.py`

Design 0013 §17 (decision 0132): while a box waits on its job, the orchestrator has the model. With it on, a checkpoint of kind `job` starts as the box's turn ends, handed the job's command and its log so far; it decides `wait`, `side` (one to three side tasks) or `change`. Side tasks run one after another while the job runs, each a session of its own titled `<flow> · side · <n>`, in Auto mode, writing one note into the corpus (`corpus/<run>-<n>-<slug>.md`), whose index Clank keeps (`corpus/CORPUS.md`); a task that writes no note fails, saying so. When the job ends, no new side task starts, the one at work goes on to its end, and the box is told the notes written meanwhile. A side decision without tasks, with more than three, or past the run's side budget is refused, saying why. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a box waiting on its job hands the model to a job checkpoint whose wait lets it run
- a second job in the same try gets a checkpoint numbered after the first
- side tasks run one after another each writing its note into the corpus and the box is told
- the job ending while a side task works starts no other and the box waits for it
- a side task that writes no note fails saying so
- a side decision without tasks past three or without a prompt is refused
- past the runs side budget a job checkpoint may only wait or change

### `tests/components/flows/test_stale.py`

Design 0013 §8 (decision 0061): a finished box is changed when its settings are edited, or when its deliverable in the folder no longer matches its fingerprint; every box after it that has run is then stale, and nothing runs again on its own. Progress is not a change: a TASK box that ticks its step in PLAN.md, adds its Log line and sets the plan's status marks nothing stale, even when the runner looks while the TASK still works, while a change to the plan's other lines, or a tick made between boxes, does. An edit the owner makes at a gate is the owner's: it marks nothing stale. Running again from a box, or sending one back, takes the boxes before it as they stand, so a later change to them marks the boxes after them stale again. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- an edit to a finished box marks the boxes after it stale and starts nothing
- a tasks own progress in the plan marks nothing stale
- a task that changes the plans other lines marks the boxes after the plan stale
- a tick made between boxes marks the boxes after the plan stale
- an edit approved at a gate marks nothing stale
- a finished run whose workspace is gone is passed over quietly
- a tasks progress seen while it still works marks nothing stale
- a box edited again after running from the box after it marks that box stale again
- a box edited again after a box after it was sent back marks the boxes after it stale again

### `tests/components/flows/test_summaries.py`

Design 0025 §6 (design 0013 §12): one line per flow for a list, made by the service so every client says a flow's state the same way and knows no rule of the runner: not run yet; queued behind another flow; running at a box, so many of its boxes done; paused; waiting for you (a gate, your part, a failed box, a box done, proposals) at a box, with the question's id; being built with the model, at its planning's state; done, approved by you when a gate passed; stopped, with why. Each with the preset its running box uses (else the flow's default), and the run's time. The route lists every flow, what waits for you first, then what runs, then what ended, newest first, and what never ran last.

- a flow that never ran says so with its default model
- a running flow names its box and counts the boxes done
- the running boxs own preset is the models title
- a flow waiting for you says what at which box with its question
- a paused run says which box runs on and a queued one whom it waits behind
- a done run says when a gate approved it and a stopped one why
- a flow being built says its plannings state until it runs
- the list puts what waits for you first then what runs then what ended newest first
- the route lists every flow with its words and the question it waits for

### `tests/components/flows/test_verify.py`

Design 0013 §2, §8 and §9: a VERIFY ends by calling `flow_verdict`, which the service gives to the VERIFY box at work in the workspace: *pass*, or *fail* with numbered findings, and `criteria`, each line of the brief's limits and of what done means that the check touched, met or not, with its evidence. The verdict is kept on the VERIFY's try and as `verdict.md` in the try's folder; a pass goes on along the VERIFY's pass line; a VERIFY that ends without a verdict is reminded once in its session, then is a failed box, never a pass, and waits at a gate. A verdict is refused, saying why, while no VERIFY works in the workspace, before its try began or after it ended, and a fail without findings is refused, as is a criterion not as `flow_verdict` takes it, naming the criterion and its field; the newest verdict of a try counts, and each is an event with its actor. Run against the fake bridge and a synthetic folder.

- a verifys pass is kept on its try and as its verdict file and the flow goes on along its pass line
- a verify that ends without a verdict is reminded once then fails and waits at a gate
