Design 0003 §6.5, design 0001 §3.6 and §3.8: a preset's check starts nothing and says, item by item, whether it can run: the preset is valid; its engine is installed on a host; the program is there; the model's files are there at their size; the engine's own `--help` lists every flag its plan gives it; and the plan fits in the GPU's free memory. Each item that fails says why; a GPU that cannot be read leaves the fit unknown.

- a preset that can run passes every item and nothing starts
- vision adds the projector to what is checked
- a preset without vision checks no projector
- each item that fails says why and the check stops where it cannot go on
- a flag its engine does not list fails the check naming it

### `tests/components/runtime/test_cloud_facts.py`

Design 0016 §13 (design 0012 §4, decision 0067): the Monitor reads a cloud model's figures from its preset's facts, as it reads an engine's: its window (the smaller of the preset's and the model's) and its most output; no thinking budget, no exact window, not on this machine's GPU, no token count, no one conversation; its prompt, output and cache figures read from OpenRouter's usage; where the facts were read; the context totals of a step it answered say it sits in the cloud (`gpu.device`), all of it attended. The model's facts have no template and no calibration, so the estimate shares the census out to OpenRouter's `prompt_tokens` at Clank's plain default, saying so. With records off, the census of a cloud request keeps no text of the prompt (decision 0096).

- a cloud presets facts say its window and what does not apply
- the context totals of a step a cloud model answered say it sits in the cloud
- a cloud presets facts without its list are none
- the estimate shares the census out to openrouters prompt tokens at the plain default
- with records off the census of a cloud request keeps no text

### `tests/components/runtime/test_cloud_preset_change.py`

Design 0016 §7 (revised after approval 2026-10-29, the owner's request): a cloud preset's settings change after it is written, through `PATCH /api/v1/runtime/presets/{name}`: any of its context, its most output (`max_output`) and its thinking effort (`reasoning`), merged into its options and checked against its model's limits as the provider lists them, then written over the record at its revision with who changed it. A value beyond the model is refused in the adapter's words, and so is a most output above half the context (the agent keeps room for a whole answer in every request); a preset that does not exist is 404, and a local one is refused, saying where its options change. The slot that runs it follows at once (its kept route planned again, nothing sent), and keeps it as the last configuration that worked; the change is an event naming the preset, its settings and that slot. The answer says, in the service's words, what changed, where it applies, and, while a slot runs it, that OpenCode windows open now keep the limits they started with until they start again. A cloud preset's view gives its settings for a form, the model's limits in their words.

- its most output is raised within the models limit and written with who changed it
- a value beyond the model is refused in the adapters words and nothing is written
- the most output is at most half the context
- an unknown preset is 404 and a local one says where its options change
- nothing to change is refused and the same values change nothing
- the slot that runs it follows from the next request and keeps it as the last that worked
- a cloud presets view gives its settings with the models limits in their words

### `tests/components/runtime/test_cloud_presets.py`

Design 0016 §7: a preset names an engine or a provider (`endpoint`), never both and never neither; a cloud preset is for the main slot only; every preset Clank ships reads as before; a cloud preset's problems name a provider not added, not agreed, or without a key; one is written from a model of the provider's list (never a model without tool calls), its options from the model; each preset as clients see it says its provider and, for a cloud one, its prices; a cloud preset needs nothing installed or downloaded; and a provider a preset names cannot be removed.

- every preset clank ships reads as before with its engine as its provider
- a preset with both an engine and an endpoint or neither is refused
- a cloud preset for completion or vision is refused saying why
- a cloud presets problems name the provider its agreement and its key
- a cloud preset is written from a model with tool calls and says its provider and prices
- a cloud preset needs nothing installed or downloaded
- a cloud presets check and count say where its model runs
- a provider a preset names cannot be removed

### `tests/components/runtime/test_cloud_slot.py`

Design 0016 §7 and §14 (decisions 0105 and 0107): the main slot runs a cloud preset in an engine's place. Its load plans the connection (the provider, its agreement, its key, the model in the provider's list), starts nothing on this machine, passes its health with the key accepted and one tiny request whose cost is kept, and leaves the slot ready with a route the gateway follows (the launch keeps it, never a key; its reach and agreement read live); without a key or the agreement the plan refuses, saying what to do. A switch from an engine stops the engine first, and back starts it after; a failed health returns to the last configuration that worked. After a restart of the service the slot stays ready while its record, agreement and key remain; the watch asks for the key now and then, a refused key failing the slot and an unanswered one leaving it ready, marked. A model is usable when the main slot is ready, or a preset for it can load now: an engine preset whose engine is installed and whose model is downloaded, or a cloud preset whose provider is agreed and whose key is set; with none, the answer says to add one. The answer names the runtime's fallback preset, which loads when none has worked yet.

- a load of a cloud preset starts nothing here and leaves the slot ready with its route
- the route reads the agreement live so withdrawing it applies at once
- an engines route says its provider and reach
- without a key or the agreement the plan refuses saying what to do
- a model the provider no longer lists is refused at the plan
- a switch from an engine stops it first and back starts it after
- a failed health returns to the last configuration that worked
- unloading a cloud slot stops nothing at the provider
- the cloud slot stays ready across a restart of the service
- a key refused at the watch fails the slot
- a provider that does not answer the watch leaves the slot ready marked
- the runtime settings name the cloud watch and the fallback preset
- with nothing installed and no provider no model is usable and it says what to do
- the answer names the preset the runtimes settings fall back to
- a cloud preset with its key is a candidate
- an engine preset whose model is downloaded is a candidate
- the main slot ready is usable at once without asking any host for its files

### `tests/components/runtime/test_console_models.py`

Design 0003 §8.1 and the M1.5 plan's task 2: the console's Models page shows each slot (its state since when and why, what it runs and where), the memory its engine took beside its estimate and the GPU's use now, and the last switches; `l` loads a preset picked from a list, `c` checks the slot's preset in a window, `u` unloads; a load refused as busy says so on the message line, in the service's words.

- the models page shows the slot and l loads c checks and u unloads
- a load refused as busy says so in the services words

### `tests/components/runtime/test_console_options.py`

Design 0003 §8.2 and the M1.5 plan's task 4: the console's Launch options page shows a preset's options, each with its value, where it comes from (the preset, Clank's default), what the engine receives for it or why nothing, and what it does; then the command line and the memory estimate with its fit. It shows the preset `o` chose on the Presets page (else the main slot's, else the first); `p` picks another; `e` opens its file in the editor the system gives JSON files. Design 0011 §8 (board E12): its panel is titled "(preset): launch options" after the preset it shows. Design 0003 §4.1: an option left unset shows the engine's own default in words, an option set the default it replaces, and a line under the options says that a request's own sampling wins.

- each option says its value source and flags and e opens the presets file
- an option left unset shows the engines own default in words
- an option set shows the engines default it replaces
- under options with sampling a line says a requests own values win
- the page shows a presets sampling set and the engines own beside it

### `tests/components/runtime/test_console_presets.py`

Design 0003 §8.2 and the M1.5 plan's task 3: the console's Presets page shows each preset with its serving setup and model, its fit in the GPU's memory now or why it cannot run, and its last start; Enter switches the main slot to the highlighted one, `c` checks it in a window, `v` shows its file. Design 0003 §4.1: the highlighted preset's settings list the sampling in effect, the values it sets first, then the engine's own defaults in words.

- each preset shows its fit or why it cannot run and enter switches to it
- the settings list the sampling in effect the values set first

### `tests/components/runtime/test_console_providers.py`

Design 0003 §8.2 and the M1.5 plan's task 4: the console's Providers and models page shows each serving setup with its versions, where each is installed and how, and which Clank has a recipe for; and each model's files, with where each is on the hosts whose engine reads it and whether it is there. `i` installs an engine by its recipe on a host picked from lists (the install asks first, as any install does); `d` downloads a model's file (which asks too); `x` removes a model's files from a host (asking first) and `u` removes an engine (one Clank did not build is forgotten).

- providers show installs and files and i installs and d downloads after asking
- x removes a models files and u an engine each as its operation says

### `tests/components/runtime/test_context_facts.py`

Design 0012 §4 and §4.4, decision 0067: each engine adapter declares its context facts for a preset's options (the window and the output limit, the thinking budget when there is one, the exact window and how it picks, or the whole window attended, where it runs, whether it counts tokens, whether it keeps one conversation, and where its answer reports each figure), so the Monitor reads every figure from the loaded slot and never from one engine's numbers. Each model of the catalog may give its template's facts (how it renders earlier thinking and tool calls, its reasoning markers) and its calibration, and says when it has neither. Design 0012 §4.1 (revised 2026-10-20, decision 0090): each adapter says how its engine refuses a request its context cannot hold (KVMem's 400 "prompt + max_tokens exceeds n_ctx", llama.cpp's 400 of type `exceed_context_size_error`), so the gateway can tell OpenCode the context was full.

- kvmem declares its window its output its thinking budget and its exact window for a presets options
- kvmem with a budget of 0 attends its whole window and its method says how it picks
- kvmem with thinking off or unlimited has no thinking budget
- llamacpp attends its whole window in system memory counts tokens and reports its cache in its timings
- every engine adapter declares its context facts for its default options
- a catalog entry may give its templates facts and its calibration and says when it has neither
- the qwen38 models say their template leaves earlier thinking out and writes calls as xml
- no model of the catalog is calibrated yet
- a presets facts are its engines for its options and its models
- each engine says how it refuses a request its context cannot hold

### `tests/components/runtime/test_count_tokens.py`

Design 0013 §5 and design 0012 §4 (decision 0087): a text is counted for a preset by its engine only when the main slot runs that preset now and its engine counts tokens (llama.cpp's `/tokenize`); otherwise it is estimated, from the model's calibration when it has one, else from Clank's plain default, and the count says which; an engine that does not answer leaves the estimate, saying why. Nothing is loaded to count.

- a text is counted by the engine when the main slot runs its preset and its engine counts
- a preset not loaded or an engine that cannot count is estimated at the plain default
- a models calibration is used when it has one
- an engine that does not answer leaves the estimate saying why
- an engine names a route to count exactly when its context facts say it counts

### `tests/components/runtime/test_dev_mode.py`

Design 0016 §4, §8 and §16 (decision 0149): dev mode, for testing. It is off until the owner turns it on, and kept in the runtime's settings (`config/runtime.json`'s `dev_mode`) with who turned it on or off. While it is on, every cloud provider is asked for any of a model's providers (`data_collection allow`) from the next request, whatever its record says: the route the gateway follows, a load's tiny request and the provider's check, each request's notes naming dev mode. The record keeps its own policy, which applies again from the next request once dev mode is off; a record that already allows any provider, and its `zdr`, are left as they are. Each provider says dev mode beside its own policy, as `GET` and `PUT /runtime/dev` say what dev mode does.

- dev mode is off until turned on and kept with who turned it on
- while it is on the next request asks any provider and off again the records policy
- a load while it is on sends its tiny request to any provider
- the providers check asks as dev mode says and its policy item says so
- each provider says dev mode beside its own policy
- dev mode relaxes only the data policy and a request says where it came from

### `tests/components/runtime/test_download.py`

Design 0003 §7.2, design 0001 §7.5 and the M1.4 plan's review focus 1-2: a download says the file's size and the host's free disk and asks first; it is fetched by the host into `<file>.part` in the host's models folder, resumes from what the `.part` has after an interruption, and becomes the file only once its SHA-256 is the catalog's; a file whose checksum differs is kept aside as `<file>.bad` and refused. A file the user already has is adopted where it is once its size and SHA-256 are the catalog's (a `model-file` record), and refused when either differs.

- a download says the size and free disk and no fetches nothing
- a download cut off resumes from its part and is kept once verified
- a download whose checksum differs is kept aside and refused
- a file already there is not fetched again
- a file whose revision is no longer known is not downloaded
- a file the user has is adopted where it is once verified
- the model commands download a file and adopt one
- a file the user has that differs is refused

### `tests/components/runtime/test_endpoint_check.py`

Design 0016 §6 (design 0015 §11, decision 0076): a provider's check asks it only Clank's own made-up requests (its key, its list, one short reply, a call to a made-up tool, a little thinking) and says, item by item and in order, its agreement, its key, its account, its models, the model, the answer, tool calls, thinking, its data policy and Clank's cost limits, each with its words; a refused key fails the key and leaves the rest unknown; the result is an event and the provider's last check. The test of a cloud preset is one short request: its answer, time and cost, or the refusal's code and what to do; what either cost is kept.

- the check says each item in order with its words
- a refused key fails the key and leaves the rest unknown
- a model named for the check is the one asked
- the test of a cloud preset says each refusal by its code
- the test of a cloud preset says its answer time and cost
- the test says when openrouter does not answer

### `tests/components/runtime/test_endpoint_routes.py`

Design 0016 §4, §6, §9, §10 and §14: adding a cloud provider needs the owner's agreement, writes its record from the adapter's template with `agreed` and marks the MCP servers the agreement screen marked local only (every one already added when none are named); its key is kept only once OpenRouter accepts it, in `secrets/endpoint/openrouter/key`, shown by its last four, and never in any answer, event or log; a refused key is not kept and says why; the account is read with the key at most once a minute; changing the record's options or withdrawing the agreement is recorded; and removing the provider removes its key with it. An MCP server added without saying which, while a provider is agreed, is local only; before any is, it is not. Its live list of models is read with the key: the models that take tool calls, programming's most used first, narrowed by a search, the free ones, or every one; sorted by price, window or newest; kept an hour unless asked fresh.

- adding a provider without the agreement is refused
- adding writes the template agreed now and marks the mcp servers named
- the agreement marks every mcp server already added when none are named
- an mcp server is marked local only or not and one added says which
- an mcp server added without saying which is local only while a provider is agreed
- a key openrouter refuses is not kept and says why
- a key is not kept when openrouter does not answer
- a key it takes is kept shown by its last four and never anywhere else
- the account is read with the key at most once a minute
- changing the options or withdrawing the agreement is recorded
- removing the provider removes its key with its record
- removing the key alone leaves the record
- the list asks for tool models most used first
- a search narrows the list by name or id and free shows the free ones
- the list sorts by price window or newest and all shows models without tools
- the list is kept an hour unless asked fresh
- the list needs the key and says when openrouter does not answer

### `tests/components/runtime/test_endpoints.py`

Design 0016 §4 and §6: a cloud provider's record (`config/endpoints/<id>.json`, the kind `endpoint`) is written from its adapter's template and reads back as the design's record; a URL other than the adapter's (so the key never goes elsewhere), a reach other than `cloud`, a way of sending the key other than `bearer`, or options the adapter does not take are refused, naming the file, the line and the field, both when the file is edited and when a client writes it; an empty `agreed` reads as not agreed.

- the template written reads back as the designs record
- the designs record edited by hand reads without a problem
- a url a reach or an auth other than the adapters is refused naming the file line and field
- options the adapter does not take are refused each by its field
- an adapter clank does not know is refused
- an empty agreement reads as not agreed

### `tests/components/runtime/test_engine_commands.py`

Design 0003 §7.1 and §8.4: `clank engine install` follows the install, asks at the terminal unless told `--yes`, and prints where the engine went (exit 1 when it was not installed); `clank engine list` prints each serving setup's versions, where each is installed and how, and which Clank has a recipe for; `clank engine adopt` follows the check and says why it refused; the API starts an install as an operation.

- engine install asks follows and says where the engine went
- the api starts an install as an operation
- engine adopt follows the check and says why it refused

### `tests/components/runtime/test_fake_engine.py`

Design 0003 §9: the fake engine the runtime's tests run starts from the real adapter's plan, says it is ready in its engine's own words (so the adapter's `ready` reads it), answers the API a check uses, and stops by its identity; told to fail at its start, it says it ran out of GPU memory and ends, unless it was told so for another context only.

- it starts from the real plan says it is ready in its engines words answers and stops
- told to fail it says the gpu ran out of memory and ends
- told to fail for another context only it starts

### `tests/components/runtime/test_fast_slot.py`

Design 0006 §3: the `fast` slot runs an engine on the CPU beside the main one. Its load is not held back by a full GPU, its engine listens on the slot's own port, nothing of the GPU is measured or learned from it, and the main slot is left as it was.

- the fast slot loads beside a full gpu on its own port

### `tests/components/runtime/test_gpu.py`

Design 0001 §3.6 and design 0003 §6.5: the GPU's memory is read from `nvidia-smi`, and a machine where it does not answer is "unknown", never an error; a plan's estimate beside the free memory is *fits*, *tight* (less to spare than the setting) or *does not fit*, said with its breakdown; an engine that sizes its KV cache itself (as vLLM's does) fits when its smallest KV cache and the rest do with the headroom it keeps free, and is told how many tokens its KV cache grows by into what is left, when it can grow.

- the gpus memory is read from nvidia smi and unknown without it
- what the driver reserves is not free
- a plan fits is tight or does not fit with its breakdown
- an engine that sizes its kv itself needs its smallest kv and the headroom it keeps free

### `tests/components/runtime/test_guide.py`

Design 0016 §14 and decision 0105: Add a model's steps and their words are the service's, for every client (the owner's rule of 2026-10-21, so the phone's app walks the same steps). The steps come in one order, each titled and worded; Cloud starts at the provider, its key, or the model as the provider stands; the test loads into an empty main slot, or asks beside a local model, naming it; Ready says Docker's state and where to get it. A model's words are its line, itself and what one short request costs; its settings are filled from it and checked against it in words. A local preset's steps come in order, each with its words, its question and its call, or say what to do instead when Clank has no recipe for its engine. The key's account is one line, with OpenRouter's advice when the key has no limit.

- every step comes in order titled and worded
- cloud starts where the provider stands
- the test loads into an empty slot and asks beside a local model
- ready says dockers state and where to get it
- a models words are its line itself and what a short request costs
- a models settings are filled from it and the limits as they are
- a local presets steps come in order each with its words and call
- an engine without a recipe is a step that says what to do instead
- the keys account is one line with openrouters advice when it has no limit

### `tests/components/runtime/test_guide_routes.py`

Design 0016 §14 and §15 (plan s35, task 18): Add a model's steps and words come from the service, so every client walks them alike: `GET /runtime/guide` gives each step in order with its words, from the service's state (no model usable yet and why, this computer, the MCP servers the agreement marks local, the main slot, Docker, the limits the settings fill; once a preset is written, its test: a load into a free main slot, or a short request beside the local model that runs); `GET /runtime/guide/local` gives the main slot's presets, each with its fit and what it still needs here. The runtime's views carry their own words: each listed model (its line, itself, what its test costs, its settings), the list, the key's account, the provider's data policy, a cloud preset's prices, and a cloud load's answer, time and cost.

- the guide says each step in order from the services state
- once a preset is written its test loads it or asks beside the local model
- the local step lists the main slots presets with what each needs
- each listed model carries its words and settings
- the account and the providers view carry their words
- a cloud preset says its prices
- a cloud loads result says its answer its time and its cost

### `tests/components/runtime/test_hosts.py`

Design 0003 §2-§3 and decision 0030: the runtime's hosts are this machine and each host record; a record naming a WSL distribution gives a WSL host there; `clank host add` writes one only for a distribution WSL has, recorded as you (cli); and `clank host list` and the API list them with their kind and Clank folder.

- this machine is a host and a record naming a distribution is a wsl host
- a host is added only for a distribution wsl has
- the commands add and list hosts and the api lists them

### `tests/components/runtime/test_install.py`

Design 0003 §7.1, design 0001 §7.4 and decision 0032: an install shows what its recipe will do and asks first; "no" leaves nothing done; "yes" runs each step on the host with its output in the operation's log, checks the program it made against its adapter, and records the install; a step that fails stops the install with its last lines, and the next install goes on from that step, skipping what is already made; a canceled install ends the step's command and what it started.

- an install asks first and no leaves nothing done
- yes runs each step with its output checks the program and records the install
- each step and the check of its program run with the recipes environment
- a canceled install ends the steps command with what it started
- a failing step stops the install and the next goes on from there

### `tests/components/runtime/test_kvmem.py`

Design 0003 §4 and design 0001 §3.6: the KVMem/llama.cpp adapter gives KVMem exactly what the prototype's daily profile gives it (the prototype's command line for `recipe-iq3-cpuvision`, but for flags that only repeat KVMem's own defaults), keeps CUDA graphs off by its variable, and estimates the KV cache and the MTP head's pool its log then reports to the MiB, with the whole context and no reserve beside it when the budget is 0; the frontier preset (decision 0068) gives KVMem the whole context exactly, thinking without a limit, 32,768 tokens out, MTP and no projector; a preset with a budget whose answers can outgrow the reserve is refused, and "frontier 262K" (decisions 0069 and 0088) keeps a reserve that holds its longest answer, on the IQ3_S file in the same GPU memory as "frontier 96K"; "frontier 262K lean" (decision 0078) is the IQ3_XXS file with a larger window in less of the GPU.

- the daily preset gives kvmem what the prototypes daily profile gives it
- the kv cache it estimates is what its log reports
- the mtp heads own pool it estimates is what its log reports
- with no budget its kv cache is the whole context with no reserve beside it
- the frontier preset gives kvmem the whole context and thinking without a limit
- the frontier preset took what its estimate says to the mib
- a preset whose answers can outgrow kvmems reserve is refused
- the default frontier 262k is the iq3 s file with 57 344 tokens exact and a 40 960 token reserve
- frontier 262k lean is the iq3 xxs file with a 73 728 token window in less of the gpu
- it is one of the serving setups

### `tests/components/runtime/test_kvmem_sampling.py`

Design 0003 §4.1 and decision 0119: KVMem's GPU layers and its sampling are launch options. Left unset, KVMem gets no flag and keeps its own defaults, which differ by thinking mode, and each option says them in words, as its recorded `--help` gives them; set, the flag is always given, even at a value one mode's default has, since it then holds for both modes; the GPU layers at the engine's own 99 give nothing. Each value is bounded as KVMem's server bounds it, and a value outside is refused in a sentence naming it. With fewer layers on the GPU than the model has (the catalog's `layers`, 66 for the Qwen3.8 files, as KVMem's log counts them), the estimate puts that share of the weights on the GPU and names the rest in system memory, the KV cache and the buffers kept whole, and says it is an estimate; a model whose catalog entry has no layer count keeps its weights whole and says so.

- left unset kvmem gets no sampling flag and no gpu layers
- each sampling option set gives kvmem its flag
- a value one modes default has is still given since it then holds for both
- the gpu layers give ngl but not at the engines own 99
- each option says kvmems own default as its recorded help gives it
- the gpu layers say the engines own 99 is every layer
- a value outside kvmems bounds is refused in a sentence naming it
- the bounds are the ones its recorded help gives
- with fewer layers on the gpu the estimate puts that share of the weights there
- every layer on the gpu estimates the weights whole as before
- a model with no layer count keeps its weights whole and says so
- the qwen3 8 files have the 66 layers kvmems log offloads

### `tests/components/runtime/test_llamacpp.py`

Design 0006 §3 and decision 0035: llama.cpp's adapter gives the completion preset exactly the command line measured on this computer (the measurement of 2026-10-15, the recording's startup), estimates the system memory its log then reports, and takes none of the GPU: its fit is "on the CPU" however full the GPU is. The completion preset is Clank's own, for the `fast` slot, which listens on a port of its own.

- the completion preset runs exactly what was measured
- it estimates the system memory its log reports and none of the gpu
- the completion preset is valid and its slot listens on a port of its own

### `tests/components/runtime/test_llamacpp_sampling.py`

Design 0003 §4.1 and decision 0119: llama.cpp's sampling (build b11588) is a set of launch options with the keys KVMem's has. Left unset, llama.cpp gets no flag and keeps its own defaults, or the model file's own where the file carries one (`general.sampling.*`, for the temperature, top_p, top_k, min_p and the repeat penalty), which each option says in words, its numbers as its recorded `--help` gives them; set, the flag is always given, over the model file's own. A value outside what it means (a share from 0 to 1, a count or a temperature of 0 or more, a repeat penalty above 0, a seed of -1 for random or more) is refused in a sentence naming it.

- left unset llamacpp gets no sampling flag
- each sampling option set gives llamacpp its flag
- a value its help gives as the default is still given over the model files own
- each option says llamacpps own default as its recorded help gives it
- a value outside what it means is refused in a sentence naming it
- its penalties are unbounded as its help leaves them

### `tests/components/runtime/test_load.py`

Design 0003 §6.1 and design 0001 §4.1-§4.2, §4.6: a load starts the engine by its plan, waits until it says it is ready and answers a tiny request, measures the GPU memory it took, and the slot is ready with the engine's identity; an unload stops it; a port another program holds refuses the start, naming it and leaving it alone; a load while the slot runs, or while another load runs, is refused; and an engine that fails at its start leaves the slot failed with its own words.

- a load starts the engine waits until it answers and the slot is ready
- a stop the host cannot answer for fails and keeps the engine in its slot
- an unload stops the engine and the slot is stopped
- a port another program holds refuses the start naming it
- wsls own relay is no holder of a port no program in wsl listens on
- a load while the slot runs or another load runs is refused
- an engine that fails at its start leaves the slot failed in its own words
- the last words of an engine that ends while it starts are read once it has ended
- the routes load and unload by operations and list the slots

### `tests/components/runtime/test_local_host.py`

Design 0003 §2 and §6.3, design 0001 §4.6, decision 0030: the local host starts a process in a session of its own, its output in a log, without a window (on Windows, neither it nor the interpreter a virtual environment's python.exe starts for it shows one); knows it by its id, start time and command line, once it shows the command line it was started with (one that never does is ended and refused), and by a start no setting of the system clock moves; says it is gone once its id names another process, and never stops that one; stops its own politely, and kills it when it does not end in time; refuses a program it cannot start in its own words; and reads a file's facts and who listens on a port.

- a started process logs runs on its own and is known by its identity
- a started process shows no window nor does the interpreter it starts
- an identity whose id now names another process is not alive and is not stopped
- a process is still itself when the system clock is set again
- a start waits until the process shows the command line it was started with
- a process that never shows the command line it was started with is ended and refused
- a process that ignores the polite stop is killed when its time is up
- a program that cannot start is refused in the hosts words
- a files facts and a ports listener are read
- a step whose program is missing is refused in the hosts words

### `tests/components/runtime/test_model_commands.py`

Design 0003 §6.1 and §8.4, design 0001 §3.6 and §5.8: the next estimate for an engine on a host learns from what its last launch there measured, and says so; `clank model load`, `switch` and `unload` follow their operations step by step and print how they ended (exit 1 when they failed); `clank model status` prints each slot; `clank preset check` prints each item.

- the next estimate learns from what the last launch measured
- an engine that reserves more than it touches teaches what it needed to start
- the model commands follow their operations and print how they ended

### `tests/components/runtime/test_openrouter.py`

Design 0016 §3, §6, §7 and §8: what the OpenRouter adapter reads of OpenRouter's answers and writes into its requests.

- the list keeps tool models unless asked with the smaller window and prices per million
- a free model is marked with its limits and its retirement
- the models are asked for with tools most used first and never with a category beside them
- the account reads the keys own facts and no limit as none
- a request goes as the tool sent it with the listed id and the records policy
- the presets effort and zdr are added only where the tool set none
- a provider object the tool sent never loosens the records data policy
- the headers carry the bearer key and the session for openrouters cache
- each refusal says what to do and whether opencode may retry it
- no provider under deny says how to allow it and is not retried
- a data policy refusal under allow points to the accounts privacy settings
- a free models daily limit is not retried and says when it starts again
- no full context words are declared until a real key shows them
- a requests cost is openrouters figure else an estimate that says so
- a generations record gives its total cost
- a presets options follow the model
- a window of zero beside the providers real one says nothing
- a router priced per request is left out since its cost cannot be said before

### `tests/components/runtime/test_options.py`

Design 0001 §3.1 and §3.6, design 0003 §4 and §4.1: an engine adapter checks a preset's options against its version: an option the version does not have, a value outside its range or choices (or not past a bound it must pass), or an option whose requirement is not met is refused in a sentence naming it; a version no adapter knows is refused naming the known ones; and the command line is made from what each option maps to, leaving out a value that is the engine's own default. An option with no default, left unset, gives the engine nothing, and says in words what the engine does then (decision 0119); set, it is given even at a value one of the engine's defaults has.

- a wrong option or value is refused in a sentence naming it
- a version no adapter knows is refused naming the known ones
- the command line comes from each options mapping leaving out the engines own defaults
- a wrong preset has no plan
- an option says what it maps to and what its version supports
- a default whose requirement is not met is left out not refused
- an option left unset gives nothing and says in words what the engine does then
- an option set is given even at a value one of the engines defaults has

### `tests/components/runtime/test_presets.py`

Design 0003 §3 and design 0001 §3.4, §3.7: presets are readable files, Clank's own and the user's, each naming its engine, the engine's version, its model and the options that differ from the defaults; the API lists them and the engines, and `clank preset list` and `clank preset show` print them, as JSON with `--json`. Design 0003 §4.1: each preset says the sampling it gives its engine, each value it sets or else the engine's own default in words, and `clank preset show` prints it, saying that a request's own values win.

- a preset file is read with its engine model and options
- a preset with an unknown field is refused naming its line
- the api lists the presets and the engines
- the preset commands print them and json for scripts
- each listed preset says what would stop it from running
- preset show says what would stop a preset from running
- each preset says its sampling each value set or the engines own default
- preset show prints the sampling in effect and that a requests own values win

### `tests/components/runtime/test_recipes.py`

Design 0003 §7.1 and decision 0032: every recipe Clank ships is for a serving setup and a version Clank has an adapter for, makes its program in the engine's folder, and builds the source the adapter's version names; nothing it runs needs root. None ships now (KVMem is adopted where it was built, decision 0034 retired NInfer's): the tests of a shipped recipe wait for the next one, and Clank with none has none rather than an error.

- with no recipe shipped clank has none
- a shipped recipe is for a version an adapter knows and fetches what it names
- a shipped recipe keeps every tool out of the users home
- each step that makes something makes it whole or not at all

### `tests/components/runtime/test_remove.py`

Design 0003 §7.4: what Clank put on a host can be taken off it again, and brought back.

- a removal says what goes and how to bring each file back and no removes nothing
- yes removes the files what was left of them and their empty folders and keeps the presets
- a file the user keeps elsewhere is forgotten without a question and not deleted
- nothing of the model on the host is said and nothing is asked
- a model a running slot uses is refused
- an engine built by its recipe is removed with its folder after a question
- no keeps the engine and its folder
- an engine whose folder clank did not make is forgotten and its folder kept
- an engine a running slot runs or one not installed there is refused
- the commands remove a models files and an engine

### `tests/components/runtime/test_request_fields.py`

Design 0001 §6.3 and design 0004 §2: a request reaches each engine in that engine's own fields. Thinking on or off is `chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking` for KVMem (llama.cpp's form), and the reasoning budget is `reasoning_budget_tokens`: a request in the other common form (`enable_thinking`, `reasoning_budget`) reaches KVMem in its own, with a note for each change; a request already in llama.cpp's form, or one that says nothing of these, is left as it is. (NInfer took the other form and refused llama.cpp's, until decision 0034 retired it.)

- kvmem gets thinking and its budget in llamacpps fields
- a request already in llamacpps form reaches kvmem as it is
- a request that says nothing of thinking is left as it is

### `tests/components/runtime/test_runtime_api.py`

Design 0003 §8 and the M1.5 plan's task 1: what clients read of the runtime: the newest launches; the GPU's memory now; a preset's plan, with its command line, its memory estimate and fit now, and each option with its value, where the value comes from (design 0001 §3.5), what the engine receives for it or why nothing, and what it does; for a preset that cannot be planned, its options and why it cannot; and a host is made once, so a WSL host's home is asked once.

- a presets plan says its command line memory and fit and where each value comes from
- a preset that cannot be planned still shows its options and says why
- the launches come newest first and the gpu as it is now
- a host is made once and again when its record changes
- the models say where each file is on each host whose engine reads it
- a preset says what it needs on its host and whether each is there

### `tests/components/runtime/test_shipped_presets.py`

Design 0003 §4.1 and decision 0119: options added to an engine adapter change nothing a shipped preset launches. Each preset in `clank/defaults/presets/` gives, with a fixed launch, exactly the command line, the environment and the memory estimate it gave before the GPU's layers and the sampling became options: "frontier 262K", the owner's model, among them. Written before those options were added, and kept as they came.

- every shipped preset is held
- a shipped preset launches exactly as before

### `tests/components/runtime/test_state.py`

Design 0003 §3 and §6, design 0001 §4.1 and §4.8, design 0002 §2.2: the runtime keeps each slot's state (a new one is stopped), each change with its reason, announced as `runtime.slot`; it keeps each launch and each engine installed; and the record routes read these state kinds through the runtime's part, as they read the kernel's own.

- a new slot is stopped and each change is kept with its reason and announced
- the last configuration that worked is kept with the slot
- launches and installs are kept
- the record routes read the runtimes state through its part

### `tests/components/runtime/test_switch.py`

Design 0003 §6.1 and §6.4, design 0001 §4.2-§4.3: a switch stops what runs and starts the new preset; one whose new engine fails at its start, or is not ready in time, stops what it started, starts the last configuration that worked again (with the options it had then), and says why the new one failed in the engine's own words; with no configuration that worked, the slot is left failed. The new preset here is "fake KVMem small" (a context of 4K), which the fake engine's `--fake-only-context 4096` makes fail while "fake KVMem" (8K) still starts from the same install. A port still held a moment after Clank stopped its own engine is waited for, not blamed on another program.

- a port still held a moment after clank stopped its engine is waited for
- a switch stops what runs and starts the new preset
- a switch waits for the gpu to free what the stopped engine took
- a switch whose engine fails goes back to the last that worked and says why
- a switch whose engine is never ready goes back too
- with nothing that worked a failed switch leaves the slot failed
- the route starts a switch and an unknown slot is not found

### `tests/components/runtime/test_watcher.py`

Design 0003 §6.2-§6.3 and design 0001 §4.4, §4.6: the runtime watches each running slot. An engine that ends makes the slot failed with its own last words, and the launch crashed; one that had been ready ten minutes or more is started again once, saying so; a second crash within the hour leaves it failed and asks the user, who can start it again; an engine whose health fails three times in a row is stopped and the slot failed. When the service starts, an engine still running is adopted, not restarted, and a slot whose engine ended meanwhile is said to have stopped while the service was not running. A host that cannot say whether the engine runs (a busy WSL leaves a question unanswered) says nothing about it: the slot is kept, and asked again.

- an engine that ends leaves the slot failed in its own words
- one ready ten minutes is started again once and says so
- a second crash within the hour leaves it failed and asks
- a crash of a preset that names a fallback loads the fallback and says why
- an engine whose health fails three times is stopped and the slot failed
- after a restart of the service a running engine is adopted and an ended one said
- an engine whose host cannot say whether it runs is not taken for ended

### `tests/components/runtime/test_wsl_host.py`

Design 0003 §2 and decision 0030: the WSL host does everything inside its distribution with one `bash -c` script per question, each answering one line: a start answers with the engine's identity once its command line is its own, or the distribution's own words for why it could not start; a signal goes only to the same process; a question the distribution leaves unanswered says nothing of the engine (neither alive nor gone, and a stop not done); a file's facts, the home folder and a port's listener are read there; WSL's list of distributions is read from its UTF-16; and a step of an install runs in the distribution, its output followed line by line, its process group ended there when it is canceled.

- a start runs its script in the distribution and answers with the engines identity
- a start the distribution refuses is said in its own words
- alive and stop take the whole identity to the distribution
- a distribution that does not answer says nothing of the engine
- a files facts the home folder and a ports listener are read there
- the distributions are read from wsls utf16 list
- a short commands output is read there whatever its exit status
- a step runs its script in the distribution and each line but its group is given
- a canceled step ends its process group in the distribution
- a step wsl does not run is said

### `tests/components/tools/test_console_servers.py`

Design 0004 §10 and the M8.0 plan, task 3: the console's MCP servers page lists every server this folder's agent can use, one row each with where it comes from, its state and its tools; the highlighted one's details show what starts it and its secrets by name and last four characters only; `a` adds one in a form (its command line split as a shell would); `c` checks one (on this computer when the folder is not a workspace), after which its row counts its tools and Enter lists them with their parameters; `s` asks for a workspace first; `d` removes one after a question. Design 0011 §4 and §8 (board E14): the details' panel is titled with the highlighted server's name.

- the page lists adds checks and removes servers

### `tests/components/tools/test_mcp.py`

Design 0002 §1 (the tools component) and the M8.0 plan: an MCP server's standard entry (`.mcp.json`'s) is read and written as other harnesses write it; a check starts a stdio server with its environment, asks it to initialize and for its tools, and ends it; one that never answers is ended at its time limit, saying so; a program that cannot start is named; an http server is checked over the streamable HTTP transport, its session kept, whether it answers in JSON or in server-sent events.

- a servers standard entry is read as other harnesses write it
- a stdio server is started with its environment asked for its tools and ended
- a tool its server marks read only is known as one and no other is
- a server that never answers is ended at its time limit saying so
- a program that cannot start is named
- an http server is checked keeping its session

### `tests/components/tools/test_servers.py`

Design 0002 §2.8 and §2.11, and the M8.0 plan, task 1: an MCP server added for the user is a settings record holding how to start or reach it, the values of its environment and headers kept as secrets, never in a record or an event; a project's servers are its `.mcp.json`, written as other harnesses write it, the rest of the file left as it was; a name is a plain word, new in its place, and one used in both places is asked about; removing a server removes its secrets; a check starts the server with its secrets, asks it for its tools, and ends it.

- a server added for you is a record and its secrets are kept apart
- an http servers headers are secrets too
- a projects servers are its mcp json as other harnesses write it
- a name used in both places is asked about
- a name is a plain word new in its place and an entry must be one
- removing a server removes its secrets
- a check starts the server with its secrets asks for its tools and ends it

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_agent_access.py`

Design 0014 §4: on Docker Engine a mounted folder shows the host's own owners, and the agent works as user 1000 in its container, so a folder that user may not read, write and enter is refused when its workspace opens, saying why: that the agent works as user 1000 in its container, whose folder it is, and how to let it write (an access control list for user 1000, or the folder opened to every user). The folder's owner bits, its group's (user 1000's group is 1000), the others' and a POSIX access control list all count, as Linux counts them. Docker Desktop's file sharing maps the folder's owner, so it says nothing.

- what user 1000 may do comes from the owner the group the others and the acl
- the refusal says the agent is user 1000 whose folder it is and how to let it write
- docker desktops file sharing says nothing
- on linux a folder of another user that user 1000 may not write is refused

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_choosing_a_platform.py`

Design 0014 §2 (decision 0070): the container platform adapter is chosen by platform and by what answers, asking no engine. `auto`, the setting's default, is Docker Desktop on Windows and macOS. On Linux it is the adapter of the engine the docker command would use: Docker Desktop when the command's current context is Docker Desktop's (desktop-linux); else Docker Engine when systemd knows Docker's service; else Docker Desktop when its own command answers (a stopped Docker Desktop, with no Docker Engine beside it); else Docker Engine, which asks the engine directly, says that Docker from the snap and rootless Docker are unsupported, or says how to install Docker when there is no docker command. A name set in the settings wins. Each choice says why, and the platform made keeps it. Docker Desktop on Linux names its own context on every command; elsewhere its commands do not change. The workspaces say which adapter is in use, and Docker's view says which, why, and whether Clank starts it.

- docker engine is used where docker desktop is not
- docker desktop is used on windows and macos and where its context is current on linux
- a stopped docker desktop is still chosen where no docker engine is installed
- a docker command without dockers system service asks the engine directly
- the snap and rootless docker go to docker engine which says they are unsupported
- without a docker command docker engine says how to install it
- an adapter named in the settings is used as named
- choosing an adapter asks no engine
- docker desktop on linux names its own context and elsewhere changes nothing
- the platform made from the settings keeps why it was chosen
- dockers view says which adapter was chosen why and whether clank starts it
- dockers view asks nothing more where docker cannot be started at all

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_cloud_bound.py`

Design 0016 §9 (decisions 0103 and 0112): every workspace, new or existing, stays on this machine until the owner says otherwise; with a cloud model in the main slot, a workspace not yet answered is asked once whether its agent may use it (the open question given again while it waits), with words that say what goes, what never does and, for a workspace that existed before the provider was agreed, that its earlier sessions and folder may hold research; *allow* makes its bound `cloud` and gives it to the gateway, *keep local* is kept and not asked again; a question that expires leaves it unanswered, asked again next time; each answer is an event that says who gave it; and the bounds reach the gateway at every change and at each start of the service. Every answer of the question's route says whether the agent may start now (`start`), and a folder's view carries its workspace's bound beside its other facts.

- every workspace starts on this machine and its bound reaches the gateway
- no question is needed while the main slot runs a local model
- with a cloud model the workspace is asked once and allowing gives the gateway its bound
- keep local is kept and never asked again until the owner says
- a question that expires leaves the workspace unanswered and asked again
- a workspace older than the agreement is told its history may hold research

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_cloudwords.py`

Design 0016 §9 and §10, decisions 0103 and 0112: what a workspace's cloud bound says is the service's, for every client (the owner's rule of 2026-10-21): its bound is one line, and what changing it does is said first.

- the bound is one line
- changing the bound says what changes before it does

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_docker.py`

Design 0002 §2.9 and §3.2: Clank tells its own containers from the others by their labels (this CLANK_HOME's, another home's, another program's labels or name), and the Docker route shows the platform's state, a one-line summary and each container with its owner, behind the token.

- a container is clanks only by this homes label whatever its name
- the docker route shows the state and each container with its owner

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_docker_cli.py`

Design 0014 §2 (decision 0070): every container command Clank sends is Docker's own CLI, the same whichever program runs the engine, so the container methods of the interface are one base's, `DockerCli`, which every adapter extends without overriding any of them; and every command's arguments start in one place, where an adapter names the engine its commands go to.

- every container command is the plain docker cli both adapters share
- every command starts with the engine its adapter names

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_docker_containers.py`

Design 0004 §3 and design 0002 §2.9: every adapter's container commands, on a scripted `docker` (each command's exact arguments; its answer): Docker Desktop's and Docker Engine's are the same CLI (design 0014 §2), Docker Engine's naming the system engine's socket first, once systemd says Docker's service is active. It reads the platform's CPUs and memory; tells whether an image is built and builds one, each line as it comes; makes a network only when it is not there; runs a container from its spec, joining its other networks after it starts; reads, starts, stops and removes a container, and removes a volume, a missing one being no error; and says how a terminal enters a container. A command Docker refuses raises its own words. Revised 2026-10-26: a container read says the networks it is on, joining a network says whether it joined now or was on it already, and a start refused because a network the container was on is gone says so (`NetworkGone`), so Clank makes the container again.

- the platforms cpus and memory are read from its engine
- an image is built only when asked and its lines come as written
- a network is made only when it is not there
- a container runs from its spec and joins its other networks after it starts
- a container joins and leaves a network and a network is removed each twice without error
- a command docker refuses raises its own words
- a container is read started stopped and removed and a missing one is no error
- a terminal enters a container as its user in its folder with its environment
- a file is written in a container as its user
- a container says its networks and joining one says whether it joined now
- a start refused because a network is gone says so
- several files are written in a container in one command
- a container without python gets its files one by one
- every container command names the engine its adapter names
- each running containers use is read from docker stats in its own fields
- whether docker can limit memory is read in its words
- a docker info that reached no engine says no cpus or memory
- a workspaces network gives this computer no address on docker engine and is as before elsewhere
- a networks options are read as it was made and a missing one has none

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_docker_desktop.py`

Design 0002 §2.10 and PROTOTYPE.md: the Docker Desktop adapter finds the docker command on the PATH, else in Docker Desktop's own folder; without `docker desktop` (Docker Desktop older than 4.37) it says to update and refuses to start or stop; a start that Docker Desktop's command says it need not make (it believes Docker Desktop runs) starts Docker Desktop's own program when Docker Desktop is stopped, leaves a Docker Desktop that is starting alone, and says what to do when the program cannot start; and on Windows, after a start, it puts back WSL's handler for Windows programs when Docker Desktop removed it, leaves a present one alone, and asks nothing of WSL elsewhere.

- the command is found on the path first then in docker desktops folder
- without the docker desktop command it says to update and refuses to start or stop
- a start its command says it need not make starts docker desktops own program when it is stopped
- a docker desktop that is starting is left to start
- a stopped docker desktop whose program cannot start says what to do
- after a start a missing wsl handler is put back and said
- a present handler is left alone and wsl is asked nothing when it does not run or elsewhere
- label names come from dockers own map so a value holding a comma adds none
- a container gone since it was listed has no labels and the others keep theirs

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_docker_engine.py`

Design 0014 §2 (decision 0070): the Docker Engine adapter reads Docker Engine's state and never changes it. It asks systemd first, which starts nothing, so a stopped engine is never woken: activating is starting, deactivating stopping, inactive stopped, failed failed (its detail naming `systemctl status docker`). No command reaches the engine while systemd says Docker's service is not active (a reading from the last second is reused): neither those it runs, nor those it follows (a build, a setup), nor those it hands back for another to run (`docker exec`); each is refused with the command that starts Docker. An active engine is asked `docker version`, its whole answer kept: "permission denied" is no access, whose detail says how to join the docker group (equivalent to root on that computer) and to restart Clank's service, or only to restart it when the account joined after the service started. Every command names the system engine's socket, whatever DOCKER_HOST and the current context say. Without systemd, the engine is asked directly. Where systemd knows no docker.service of the system's, Docker from the snap and rootless Docker are unsupported, saying to install Docker Engine from Docker's packages. The capture tool asks what the adapter asks.

- a user outside the docker group is told how to join it
- a service started before the user joined the docker group says to restart it
- a stopped engine is read from systemd and never woken
- no command reaches the engine while systemd says it is not active
- a reading of systemd from the last second is reused
- every command goes to the system engines socket
- systemds word for dockers service gives its state
- a failed engine says where to read why
- without systemd the engine is asked directly
- docker from the snap or rootless docker is unsupported saying how to install docker engine
- where systemd knows dockers service its engine is used whatever the context
- the capture tool asks what the adapter asks
- memory limits are read as recorded and an answer that reached no engine says nothing
- the running engines containers use is read as recorded

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_docker_operations.py`

Design 0002 §2.4-2.5 and §3.2: starting and stopping Docker are operations the service runs under one lock. A start asks the platform, waits for its engine within its time limit (and can be canceled while it waits), then puts right what the start disturbed; a stop asks first when containers Clank did not start are running, naming them, and "cancel" leaves Docker running; a press sent twice starts one operation, and a second start while one runs is refused; the background check records `docker.changed` once per change (the first reading is what clients load, not a change); after a restart an interrupted operation is reconciled from Docker's state. Design 0014 §2: on Docker Engine, the system's service, a start or a stop is refused at once in the status's words (the command to run; the docker group when this account has no access), a stop before any question about other programs' containers; while it is stopped, a workspace's container is unavailable in those words, and nothing reaches the engine.

- the check records docker changed once per change and not for the first reading
- a start asks the platform waits for its engine then puts right what it disturbed
- a start when docker runs is done at once
- a start that does not answer in time fails naming the time and frees docker
- canceling while it waits ends the start canceled and frees docker
- a stop with only clanks containers or none does not ask
- stopping docker with containers clank did not start asks first and cancel leaves it running
- a stop question nobody answers takes its default and leaves docker running
- answering stop stops docker
- without docker installed start and stop fail with what to do
- after a restart an interrupted start is reconciled from dockers state
- a press sent twice starts one operation and another start is refused as busy
- the routes start operations that record the client
- starting docker engine is refused at once with the command to run
- without access a start names the docker group not systemctl
- stopping docker engine is refused before any question about other containers
- a workspaces container on a stopped docker engine is unavailable saying how to start it

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_edge.py`

Design 0004 §3: the edge's program forwards one port to one target and nothing else: a request sent whole through it gets its answer back (the target still writes after the request side is done), a streamed answer comes piece by piece, and a connection the target refuses is closed at once. Design 0024 §2: several ports, each to its own target (the gateway's and the proxy's). Design 0014 §3: a target is a host and its port, or a Unix socket (on Docker Engine, the gateway's and the proxy's, on Linux only here). It runs as the program it is in the image (the same file), here on this machine.

- a request through the edge gets its whole answer
- a connection the target refuses is closed at once
- the edge forwards several ports each to its own target
- the edge reads each target as a host and its port or a unix socket
- the edge forwards to a unix socket

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_edge_reach.py`

Design 0004 §3 and design 0024 §2 (revised 2026-10-26): the edge is on the network of every workspace, whatever made it again. Made again by an opening (the ports it forwards changed), it joins back the networks of the workspaces that run and of those that are stopped, as it does when it is made or started; the service's check of Docker finds a running workspace whose network the edge is not on from the reading it takes anyway (no other Docker call while nothing is missing), joins it back, starts an edge that was stopped and makes one that is gone, and says what it repaired in its log and as an event; it leaves a running edge that forwards other ports to an opening or an agent's start, which make it again. An edge or a workspace's container that cannot start because a network it was on is gone (made again since) is made again. The views say a workspace cut off from the model plainly, with what Clank does about it: the route of the workspaces, the folder's view and `clank workspace list`. A folder named `edge` never gets the edge's container's name.

- an edge made again by an opening joins back the networks of the other workspaces
- the check of docker joins the edge back to each running workspaces network and says so
- the check asks docker nothing more while every running workspace reaches the edge
- the check starts a stopped edge and makes a missing one while a workspace runs
- the check says once when the edge cannot be made and does not try again at once
- the check leaves a running edge that forwards other ports for an opening to make again
- starting the agent puts the edge right first and says what it did
- an edge and a container whose network was made again are made again when opened
- a folder named edge never gets the edges containers name
- a workspace cut off from the model is said plainly with what clank does

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_edge_socket.py`

Design 0014 §3 (decision 0071, option (a)): on Docker Engine the edge reaches the gateway through its socket. The platform adapter's name decides the edge's way: `docker-engine` gets the socket, its folder mounted read-only at /run/clank and the edge running as the owner; every other adapter (Docker Desktop, on every system) keeps the host's loopback, exactly as before. Opening a workspace whose folder holds the gateway's socket folder, or is inside it, is refused, saying why; a socket folder inside a workspace's folder keeps every workspace from opening on Docker Engine, naming that workspace and `socket_folder`. While the gateway runs, opening a workspace on Docker Engine asks the gateway's health through the edge, and makes the edge again when it does not answer; one that still does not answer refuses the opening, saying why. These run on every system: the gateway is told it is on Linux, its socket folder a short one of the test's own.

- on docker engine the edge forwards to the gateways socket as the owner
- on docker desktop the edge still forwards to the hosts loopback
- a workspace folder that holds the socket folder is refused
- a socket folder inside a workspaces folder keeps every workspace from opening saying which
- an edge that no longer reaches the gateway is made again

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_figure_geometry.py`

Design 0008 §3: when Matplotlib saves a raster figure inside the workspace's folder, the sandbox's `sitecustomize` writes its geometry under `.clank/figures/` (a `.gitignore` keeps git out): the image's size and checksum, each plot's box in the image's pixels, its data limits and scales, its title and labels, its lines with their labels and data, its legend. A point of the data, placed through that geometry, falls on the pixel Matplotlib drew it on, with a tight bounding box and on a log scale too. A figure saved outside the folder, or as a PDF, gets none; a Python that never imports Matplotlib never loads it. Design 0018 §7.4: the script that saved it is kept too, when it is a file of the folder, so the figure can be found by it.

- a saved figure has its geometry and a data point falls where matplotlib drew it
- a line with error bars is one line named as its legend names it
- the script that saved a figure is kept when it is a file of the folder
- a python that never imports matplotlib never loads it

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_host_asks.py`

Design 0024 §3 (decision 0141): a host the agent reaches without an allowance is asked for. The service allows at once when the record does, refuses at once within the quiet period, else asks the owner one question per workspace and host (the same question while it waits): answered always, the host joins the record and the request goes out; once, it goes out this time; no, or unanswered within `HOLD_SECONDS`, it is refused and the host is not asked again for `QUIET_MINUTES`. The routes the gateway's proxy uses: `POST /workspaces/{name}/hosts/wanted` and `GET /workspaces/hosts/wanted/{question}`.

- a host not allowed is asked for once per host and always writes the allowance
- no or no answer refuses and keeps the host quiet for a while
- the routes the proxy uses ask and read the decision

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_image_layers.py`

Design 0020 §3: a workspace's record may name image layers (`tex` so far); its image is then Clank's sandbox image with each layer built on it, from the layer's folder with the base image's tag as the build argument `BASE`, tagged by the base's tag and the layers' files (`layered_tag`). Opening it builds the sandbox image and then the layered one, when missing, asked first with what the layers add; its container is made from the layered image, the edge from the sandbox image; the next opening does nothing again. Whether a container runs the current image is judged against its workspace's own. `POST /workspaces/{name}/layers` adds a layer to a workspace's record or takes it away; an unknown layer is refused.

- the layered tag is the base with no layer and names its layers otherwise
- the tex layer builds on the base and holds what the checks need
- opening a workspace with the tex layer builds it on the base and runs it
- the layers build is asked first saying what it adds
- a layer is added to a workspace and taken away through the api

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_image_operation.py`

Design 0028 §4 (S4): Clank's sandbox image is built during the setup, as an operation of its own that runs in the background (`workspaces.image`): it builds the image when no image of this Clank's tag is there, each line of the build in its log, and does nothing when it is built; it asks first unless `yes` (it downloads about a gigabyte), and nothing is built on a no; it is refused, in Docker's words, while Docker does not run. Opening a workspace holds the same lock, so an opening while the image is built is refused, naming the build (and a build while a workspace opens, likewise). The setup's checklist says the build while it runs, with its operation.

- it builds the image of this clanks tag when it is not built each line in its log
- it does nothing when the image is built
- it asks first unless yes and a no builds nothing
- it is refused while docker does not run
- an opening while the image is built is refused naming the build

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_jobs_runner.py`

Design 0021 §6: jobs run in a workspace's container for flows' boxes and for the agent the owner talks to alike, from one runner in the workspaces component, and every job runs at the lowest CPU priority (`nice -n 19`), so the model, on the same machine, keeps its speed while a job computes. The flows' job module gives the runner's names as before.

- every job runs at the lowest cpu priority under its time limit
- the flows jobs are the same runners

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_latex_helper.py`

Design 0020 §8-§10: the TeX layer's build helper (`layers/tex/latex_build.py`, run in the container). A build's scratch tree holds the folder's files so that a build never writes into the folder: LaTeX's own sources are copied, what TeX only reads (figures, data) is linked, and what a build writes (a document's `.aux`, `.log`, `.pdf` beside its `.tex`, SyncTeX's file, latexmk's) is left out, so no build can write through a link. SyncTeX's answer is read into the file and line a point of a page comes from, and Poppler's word boxes into each page's words; latexmk's argument for each engine is named. Real builds in the TeX layer's image are in `tests/docker/test_latex_layer.py`.

- sources are copied inputs linked and build outputs left out
- synctex says the file and line of a point
- poppler word boxes are read page by page

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_network_sets.py`

Design 0024 §5 and §6 (decision 0141): the named sets of hosts (Python packages, papers, code, data and models, documentation) live in `CLANK_HOME/config/network-sets.json`, written with the defaults when missing and read each time, so the owner can change it; a set allowed writes its hosts into the workspace's record one by one, each marked with the set, and taking it back removes those still marked (a host the owner also allowed by name stays); the route and the command (`clank workspace allow FOLDER --set papers`, `clank workspace sets`); the agent's instructions (`network.md`) name the proxy, the ask once in words, and the literature calls, every host of which the Papers set allows.

- the sets file is written with the defaults and read back when the owner changes it
- a set allowed writes its hosts marked with it and taking it back removes only those
- the route and the command allow a set and list the sets
- the instructions tell the agent how it reaches the internet and the literature calls fit the papers set

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_platform_setup.py`

Design 0028 §5: how the container platform is set up on this computer, as `clank setup` reads it from the platform's adapter. Docker Engine says whether systemd starts Docker's service with the computer (its unit file's state, which starts nothing), whether this account is in the docker group as the system lists it now, and the commands its owner runs with administrator rights: `sudo systemctl start docker`, `sudo systemctl enable docker` and `sudo usermod -aG docker` with this account's name (none when the system gives no name). Without systemd it says nothing of the boot and names no systemctl command; not installed, it names none (the contract holds every recorded platform to the same: Docker Desktop, which Clank starts through its own command, names none).

- whether systemd starts docker with the computer is its unit files state
- the owners commands need administrator rights and the join names this account
- whether this account is in the docker group is as the system lists it now
- without systemd nothing is said of the boot and no systemctl command is named
- docker engine not installed names no command
- an account whose name the system does not give is offered no join command

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_sandbox.py`

Design 0004 §3 and §6, design 0002 §2.9: a workspace's container mounts its folder at /workspace and nothing else of the machine (its home is a volume of its own), runs as the image's user with no capability and no new privileges, on an internal network of its own only, within its CPU, memory and process limits, under Clank's labels; the edge is on the network that reaches the host (each workspace's own it joins when that workspace opens), with a read-only root, small limits, and a command that forwards the gateway's port and the proxy's (design 0024 §2) and nothing else; a workspace's container is told the proxy with its own token. A folder whose path has a comma or a quote still mounts as itself. Design 0004 §10: a workspace's container tells numerical libraries how many CPUs it has. Decision 0142 (design 0023 §7): Clank's programs are mounted read-only at /opt/clank in every container Clank runs, not copied into the image, whose tag follows its Dockerfile alone. Design 0014 §3: the edge's arguments on each platform: on Docker Engine it mounts the gateway's socket folder read-only at /run/clank, runs as the owner and forwards to the sockets; on Docker Desktop it is exactly as before.

- a workspaces container mounts its folder only and runs within its limits
- every program in a workspaces container starts as many threads as the container has cpus
- a folder whose path has a comma or a quote mounts as itself
- the edge reaches the host and forwards the gateways and the proxys ports only
- a workspaces container is told the proxy with its own token and what bypasses it
- the images tag follows its dockerfile alone since the programs are mounted
- the names of one homes networks volumes and containers
- on docker engine the edge forwards to the gateways socket and mounts nothing else
- on docker desktop the edge still forwards to the hosts loopback
- the edges sockets are the ones the gateway makes

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_setup.py`

Design 0004 §10: a workspace's software. Clank proposes the setup from the folder's files (a `pyproject.toml`: an editable install, with its `dev` extra when it has one; a `requirements.txt`: those requirements; else nothing), into a virtual environment in the workspace's home. The setup runs in a container of its own, made for it and removed after it: the sandbox image, the folder and the home mounted as in the workspace's container, the same user and limits, on Docker's default network (the internet), which the workspace's own container never joins. The operation asks first unless told yes, keeps a command it is given in the workspace's record, logs each line of the install, and records the command that ran to its end; a setup that fails leaves that record as it was.

- a pyproject with a dev extra is installed editable with it
- a pyproject without a dev extra is installed editable
- requirements are installed and a folder with neither needs nothing
- an unreadable pyproject is installed without extras
- the setup container reaches the internet is removed after and is contained like the workspaces
- the setup installs the proposed software and the record says with what
- a command given is kept and one that fails leaves the record as it was
- the setup asks first naming the command and that it downloads
- a folder not opened yet or with nothing to install is said so
- the folders view says what its software is and whether it is installed

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_workspace_commands.py`

Design 0004 §1 and design 0002 §2.15: `clank workspace open [FOLDER]` (this folder unless one is named) follows the operation and says where its container runs; `clank workspace list` shows each workspace with its container's state; `clank workspace stop [NAME]` (this folder's workspace unless one is named) and `clank workspace remove NAME` follow theirs; the routes start the operations and list the workspaces. Design 0024 §1: `clank workspace allow FOLDER --host NAME` names a host the workspace's agent may reach through Clank's proxy (`--no-host` takes it back), kept in its record with when and how, carried to the gateway's routes, and recorded.

- the workspace commands open list stop and remove
- the setup command installs the folders software and says with what
- a command on a workspace that is not there says which there are
- the routes list the workspaces with their containers state
- the allow command names the hosts a workspaces agent may reach

### `tests/components/workspaces/test_workspace_operations.py`

Design 0004 §1, §3 and §6: opening a folder makes it a workspace (a record naming it; nothing is written in the folder), builds Clank's sandbox image if it is not built (asked first: it downloads about a gigabyte), makes the internal network and the one that reaches the host, starts the edge and the folder's container, and the next time does nothing it already did (a stopped container is started, not made again). A container's limits are its record's, else half of what the platform has. A folder whose container would see more than itself (a drive's root, the user's home, one holding CLANK_HOME or inside it) is refused, as is opening with Docker stopped.
