Stopping a workspace stops its container; removing one asks first (saying, when its container runs, that what runs in it ends), then removes its container and its home, and leaves its folder as it was. Design 0014 §2: on Docker Engine, which Clank never starts, opening while it is stopped is refused in the status's words, with the command that starts it. Design 0014 §4: Docker's memory limits are read as a workspace opens (never in the regular check, the last reading kept): where Docker cannot limit a container's memory no workspace opens, saying so in Docker's words with the fix (`cgroup_enable=memory` on a Raspberry Pi), unless `allow_unlimited_memory` is set, and then its log and Docker's summary say its memory is not limited. A folder the agent's user (1000 in its container) may not write, as the platform mounts it, is refused before its record is made, in the platform's words: whose folder it is, and how to let user 1000 write it. A workspace's own network is made with the platform's options that give this computer no address on it (Docker Engine's bridge would), the outer network keeping its address; one made before them is made again once the workspace's container does not run, and said until then.

- opening a folder makes its workspace and its container and the next time does nothing again
- the edge is made again when the ports it forwards change
- a stopped container from before the proxy is made again when opened
- the image build is asked first and no builds nothing
- a stopped container is started not made again
- a second folder of the same name gets a name of its own and its record sets its limits
- a folder whose container would see more than itself is refused
- opening with docker stopped says how to start it
- opening a workspace while docker engine is stopped names the command to start it
- removing a workspace asks then removes its container and home and leaves its folder
- removing a workspace whose container runs says that what runs in it ends
- a workspace does not open where docker cannot limit memory saying how to fix it
- allowing unlimited memory opens it and every client is told
- dockers memory limits are read when a workspace opens never in the regular check
- on linux a folder the agent cannot write is refused saying why
- a workspaces own network gives this computer no address where the platform would
- a network made before is made again without this computers address once its container does not run

### `tests/console/test_add_model.py`

Design 0016 §14 and decision 0105: Add a model, the console's guided first step, one panel at a time, Backspace back one screen and Esc to the menu (decision 0129). Its first screen asks how Clank should run its model: Cloud (`c`), or Local (`l`) with this machine's GPU and memory. Cloud names OpenRouter and where to get a key; the agreement says what goes to OpenRouter, where, what never goes (the MCP servers marked local, each already added marked, Space unmarking one), that each workspace asks once, and that each request costs money; `o` changes the data policy, `n` and Backspace go back writing nothing, and `y` writes the record with the servers marked as shown (none named when none is listed), and Backspace from the key then skips the agreement done; an agreement whose words were not read cannot be agreed to. A provider already added starts at its key, or at the model once its key is set. Every screen fits at 128, 88 and 60 columns, its keys bar at 60 keeping the keys the step needs. Design 0016 §11 and §14, screens 4 to 6: the key typed into the hidden field is sent once; accepted, the next screen says its account (its label, its own limit and what is left); refused, the service's words, the field cleared; no frame holds the key either way. The model list is OpenRouter's (a model without tool calls is never offered): `/` searches, `s` sorts, `f` shows the free ones with their limits; Enter on a model fills its settings, and Enter there writes the preset and a limit changed; a setting out of range is said, and nothing is written. Design 0016 §14, screens 7 and 8, and Local: from an empty main slot the preset is loaded, and Ready shows its answer, time and cost; with a local model running, the provider's short test comes first, then "load it now?", `n` keeping the local model and `y` switching; each failure says what to do, `b` back to the model and `k` back to the key. Local lists Clank's presets for the main slot with what each needs here, and Enter asks before its first step. Every word the steps say is the service's (its guide, its models' and its account's words): the page works out none of them.

- the first screen asks cloud or local with this machines gpu and memory
- cloud names openrouter and where to get a key
- the agreement says what goes where and what never goes each server marked local
- n and backspace go back esc goes to the menu and nothing is written
- y writes the record with the servers marked as shown and the policy chosen
- an added provider starts at its key
- the first three screens fit at 128 and 60 columns
- a key accepted shows its account and no frame holds the key
- a key refused shows the services words and clears the field
- search sort and free change the list
- enter on a model fills its settings and enter writes the preset and the limits
- a setting out of range is said and nothing is written
- the key the models and the settings fit at 128 and 60 columns
- a load from an empty slot ends on ready with the answer and its cost
- with a local model running the test comes first then the question and n keeps it
- y switches the main slot to the cloud model
- each failure says what to do and b and k go back to the model and the key
- a load that fails says why
- local lists the presets with their needs and enter asks before the first step
- an engine without a recipe here says what to do instead
- the test ready and local fit at 60 columns
- an agreement not read cannot be agreed to
- with no server listed the agreement leaves the servers to the service
- once agreed backspace from the key skips the agreement done
- at 60 columns the keys bar keeps the keys each step needs

### `tests/console/test_arrows.py`

Design 0001 §5.1 and design 0011 §12.5: the console is keyboard-first, so the arrows move the focus between the menu and the page on screen, without the mouse (the owner's request, 2026-10-15). The console starts on the menu. Right, or Enter, goes from the menu into the page: where the focus was when the page was left, else to its first list, or to the page itself when it has none, so that Up and Down scroll it. Right and Left move between a page's lists, and Left from its first list goes back to the menu, where Up and Down open the other pages again. A window's arrows leave the focus behind it alone. While no page is on screen (as the console quits and its pages are taken down), the focus moving is not remembered for a page and raises nothing.

- right goes from the menu into the pages list and left comes back
- right and left move between a pages lists and left from the first goes to the menu
- on a page without a list right focuses the page itself and left goes back
- enter on the menu goes into the page as right does
- back into a page the focus goes where it was left
- the console starts on the menu whose arrows still open the pages
- the arrows in a window leave the focus behind it alone
- while no page is on screen the focus moving remembers nothing and raises nothing

### `tests/console/test_box_chart.py`

Design 0013 §3, §12 and §14 step 6: the Flow view's flow laid out (`boxchart.render`, a pure function). Its stages (one box, or a DECOMPOSE's tasks fanned out and joined) read left to right in rows that wrap, a line from each row's last stage down and back to the next row's first; each box shows its type in its color, its title, its model and its last result, its border its state; a VERIFY's fail line runs under its row back to the box it checks, labeled, or is a label alone when that box is not in its row; lines are green for pass, red for fail, the "now" color on the path being run and dashed for what has not run; below 70 columns the flow is the list of board D2's left. The layout's edge cases (the owner's comment on the prototype, 2026-10-18): over generated flows of 1 to 12 tasks, with and without proposals waiting, a fail loop, an added box and the next flow, with titles longer than a box, at the Flow panel's widths at 128 and 88 columns and below 70, no box, line label or legend touches another and every line stays inside its panel; a flow taller than its panel keeps its boxes whole and apart. Snapshots: board D1's flow at those widths.

- board d1s stages are its boxes in the order they run its tasks one fan
- board d1 is laid out in rows of stages its tasks a fan at the widths of the flow panel
- the running box shows its step and speed and the selected box is lit
- below 70 columns the flow is the list of board d2s left
- a fail line runs under its row back to the box it checks labeled or is a label alone
- lines are green for pass red for fail the now color on the path run and dashed for what has not run
- over generated flows no box label or legend touches another and every line fits its panel
- a flow taller than its panel keeps its boxes whole and apart
- a boxs type and tries are kept whole its step shortened or left out first
- larger boxes keep every generated flow apart and inside its panel
- a larger box wraps its title and says its tries time and tokens

### `tests/console/test_button_groups.py`

Design 0011 §12.2, §12.3 and §12.4 (a group, pressing a button, how the focus moves), in a small app of three groups: a row, a column under it and a row under that. Along a group the arrows move its cursor and stop at its ends (← → in a row, ↑ ↓ in a column), Home and End go to its first and last; ↑ ↓ across a row reach the nearest group above or below, and stay when there is none; Enter and Space press the button under the cursor; a click presses the button under it and moves the focus there; the mouse over a button gives it the hover's ground without moving the focus; the focused button is drawn focused only while its group has the focus; a group in a list's row takes no focus but is clicked; a group's cursor stays where it was when its buttons change.

- the arrows along a row move its cursor and stop at its ends home and end too
- the arrows along a column move its cursor and stop at its ends
- up and down across a row reach the nearest group above or below and stay when there is none
- enter and space press the button under the cursor
- a click presses the button under it and moves the focus there
- a group in a lists row takes no focus and a click presses it
- the focused button is drawn focused only while its group has the focus
- the mouse over a button gives it the hovers ground and leaves the focus where it is
- a groups cursor stays where it was when its buttons change

### `tests/console/test_button_hints.py`

Design 0011 §12.4 and §12.6 (`?` on a focused button; the message line): while a group has the focus, the message line says what its focused button does and how it is pressed, in `quiet`, or why it cannot act now, in `attention`, cut to its one row; a message of an action replaces it; when the focus leaves the groups, it goes, unless a message came since. `?` with a button focused opens the details window about it: what it does and the keys that press it, or why it cannot act now; on the keys bar's own `?`, about what had the focus before the keys bar.

- the message line says what the focused button does and how it is pressed

- a message replaces it and leaving the groups clears it unless a message came since

### `tests/console/test_buttons.py`

Design 0011 §12.2 and §12.8 (one group, one cursor; the look): a button is its key and its words with a blank cell at each side, drawn in its state's elements of the style (normal, under the mouse, focused, pressed, cannot act now, and cannot act now while focused), the state's fill covering the blank cells, so that no state changes its width; its words are `quiet` on the keys bar and `text` in a panel. A group's lines for a width: a row goes on to its next line between two buttons, never inside one, a column's button wraps its words under themselves, a lead's words come first and are no button, and each button's cells are where its text is drawn, for a click.

### `tests/console/test_confirm.py`

Design 0012 §9.1 (§11 step 12's review): a window that asks before an action (`ConfirmScreen`: "a confirmation, Enter, or Esc to leave it") takes only an Enter pressed while it is on screen. The Enter that opened it, a page's key that pushed it, is never taken as "do it", wherever the window is opened from; the next Enter does it, Esc leaves it. Design 0011 §12.1 and §12.5: its keys are its last line, a group of buttons, the focus on "do it" when it opens; the right arrow reaches "leave it", which Enter then presses; Space presses too, and a click on either does what its key does, the same guard kept.

### `tests/console/test_console_style.py`

Design 0011 §3 and §12.8 (the style in the console; a button's colors): the console starts in the style its settings pick (`clank` until changed), registered as a theme whose variables are the style's elements, before its first frame, a button's five colors among them; an owner's style that changes an element shows; a style that is not found, or a file that does not read, leaves the console in Clank's own style and the message line says why (its file, line and field); the console always starts. The marks that say a state, the spinner's frames, a bar's cells, a sparkline's steps and the mark of cut text are the style's: a style with plain marks draws only those, and a state's mark takes its state's color in any style. Ctrl+P's "Change style" lists the styles the service has and writes the one picked to the console's settings through the service; the console draws in a style as soon as the service reports a change of its settings or of its style's file, the keys bar's keys and Help's included when only `key` changes, and a file that no longer reads leaves the last good style in force and says why. Textual's own theme command leaves the palette, and the theme an earlier console remembered is forgotten once: tokyo-night silently, one the owner had picked named on the message line.

- the console starts in the clank style with its elements as variables
- the style names a buttons five colors and a style file may set them
- an owners style that changes an element shows
- a style that is not found leaves clanks and says so
- a style file that does not read leaves clanks and names its file line and field
- a style with plain marks draws them on home and no others

- the drawing helpers draw the styles glyphs
- a remembered tokyo night is forgotten and never written again
- a theme the owner had picked is forgotten and named once on the message line
- the palette offers change style and not textuals theme

### `tests/console/test_data.py`

Design 0001 §5.1 and §5.7, design 0002 §2.15: the console loads what it shows once, then follows the one event stream: each event brings the fresh data of the topics it touches, read from the API off the screen's thread; a live log line arrives as it is written; a lost service is said, tried again, and reloaded whole when it is back; stopping the follower ends it at once. Design 0013 §7: a window told its model changed brings the slots again, so This folder says it at once. Design 0016 §11 and §14: whether a model is usable, the cloud providers and the cloud's cost are read at the start (unknown from a service without their routes), and again on the events that change each, and only those; design 0016 §7: a cloud preset's settings changed bring the presets and the slots again (This folder's limits). Design 0028 §4: so is `clank setup`'s checklist, again whenever Docker, a slot, an operation's end or a workspace's record changes.

- a lost service is said and one back on the same address is reloaded whole
- applying keeps at most the newest events and says what changed
- loading reads the runtimes slots presets engines and launches
- a slot changing brings the fresh slots and launches
- a preset written brings the fresh presets
- what ends an install a download or a removal touches the engines
- the setup checklist is read at the start and again on what changes it
- a window told its model changed touches the slots
- a cloud presets settings changed touch the presets and the slots

### `tests/console/test_flow_chart.py`

Design 0012 §6.3 and §11 step 6, board A: the session's flow laid out in lines. Time runs down and the branches open to the right of their node, joined by box-drawing lines whose junctions stand in one column; under 70 columns, or where the room to the right is short, a node's children stand under it. Snapshots of board A's flow and of a session with every kind of node (calls made at once, subagents within a subagent, a dead end, a compaction band, a note), at 128, 88 and 60 columns: the Session view's page without the machine column, beside it, and stacked. Every junction lines up with the branches it joins; nothing spills out of a node or the panel at any width (a node's words cut with "…"); the current node alone is highlighted; a folded node says what it holds.

### `tests/console/test_flow_panel.py`

Enter shows the node whole, with what an inference about it rests on (§6.4). With no flow it says "no session yet". A node's details show its text as it was written: a call's input in square brackets is not read as the console's markup (decision 0045: whole, one key away).

### `tests/console/test_home_and_folder_boards.py`

Design 0011 §2, §8, §9 and §11 (R3): Home and This folder as boards E1 and E2. Home's panels are Happening now beside Waiting for you, then What runs, then the History, declared in that order; the first two stand side by side where the page gives them room (170 columns, the machine column shown) and stack at 80, Happening now above; a question waiting asks for more room, so the two stack unless the row gives Waiting for you a question's width, inside the style's padding and past its room between panels, whatever the style sets them to. Each row of What runs that says a state is led by its state's mark, in its state's color (● as wanted, or changing; ○ not running; ✗ failed; … not read yet), its name and its key's hint in `quiet`; the mark is colored where it stands, whatever glyph the style gives it, and a row that wraps keeps each mark with the words it leads. ### `tests/console/test_keys_bar.py`

Design 0011 §4, §7 and §11 (R2): each page declares its keys once, as named actions with their words (`Page.KEYS`), and the keys bar, Help and `?` read that one declaration. The keys bar, one row on `bars`, shows the page's own keys first, then `? explain` and `f1 help`, then the other keys that act anywhere (`f5 machine column`, `q quit`) and `^p palette` at the right, each key in `key` and its words in `quiet`; when it is too narrow, the other keys that act anywhere go first, then the page's own from the right, and `?` and `f1` always stay. It shows the keys of the page on screen wherever the focus is. Help lists every key the pages declare, in board E13's four panels, and holds no table of keys of its own; no panel's key letter is one of its page's keys (on a page with views, of the keys of the view the panel is in: the Monitor's Session view's `n` is its Now panel's, its Flow view's builds a new flow, design 0013 §15). No page's key acts anywhere, but a page's own menu key, pressed again while the page is on screen (the Monitor's F10, its next view: design 0012 §2).

- the keys bar shows this folders keys first then explain and help
- the keys bar is one row on bars each key in key and its words in quiet
- a narrow keys bar drops the other keys that act anywhere first then the pages from the right
- each page declares its keys as named actions none of them a key that acts anywhere
- no panel letter is one of its pages keys
- the keys bar shows the keys of the page on screen wherever the focus is
- help lists every key the pages declare in four panels and holds no table of its own
- question mark says the keys the page declares on its menu entry and in the page
- the keys module names how a key is shown
- a click on a key of the keys bar presses it

### `tests/console/test_keys_bar_buttons.py`

Design 0011 §7, §12.1, §12.3 and §12.4 (the keys bar; what becomes a button; pressing it; how the focus moves): every key on the keys bar is a button, standing at the column its key stood at before buttons, as §7 fits them; Tab reaches the bar, last in the screen's order, and goes on to the menu; → and ← move along it and stop at its ends; Enter, Space and a click press a key through the console's one door; ↑ goes back into the page and Esc to the menu; the focused key is drawn on `button-focused`, its blank cells too.

- each key stands at the column it stood at before buttons
- tab reaches the keys bar last and goes on to the menu
- the arrows move along the keys bar and stop at its ends
- enter space and a click press a key of the bar through the one door
- up on the keys bar goes back into the page and esc to the menu
- the focused key is drawn on button focused its blank cells too

### `tests/console/test_lists.py`

Design 0001 §5: the console's lists change without flicker (`clank/console/lists.py`): the same lines again change nothing, a changed line is updated in its item, colors count as a change, the items are made again only when their number changes, and the highlight follows its row's key.

- a list changes only what changed and keeps its highlight
- a label is updated only when its text or colors change

### `tests/console/test_machine_column.py`

Design 0004 §9 (the owner's request, 2026-10-14): the console shows the machine's CPU, GPU, memory, disks and network all the time, on the right, and is more dynamic. The gauges draw a share as a bar in eighths of a cell, green, then yellow when busy, red when nearly full; the last values as a sparkline; sizes and rates briefly; and a spinner. The column's parts give each meter's lines from the readings, and what Clank is doing with a spinner while it runs; the header has a pill for each part that runs, its dot in its state's color. A reading the service sends live is kept with the last five minutes'. The column shows at 130 columns and more, and F5 shows or hides it at any width.

- a bar fills in eighths of a cell and is colored by how full
- a sparkline shows the last values padded on the left and sizes and rates are brief
- the panel shows each meter from the last reading
- what clank does turns a spinner while it runs and says how the last request went
- the panel shows each workspaces container and the busiest and largest processes
- the meters are parted by a blank line when they fit so else the blank lines go then the last meters
- the header has a pill for each part that runs and a spinner while something runs
- a reading sent live is kept with the last five minutes
- the machine column shows at 130 columns and more and f5 shows or hides it
- the machine columns lines fit inside its two panels
- the machine panel has the column less clank now and the blank lines between meters go first
- a turn of the spinner draws the machine panel again when clank now changes its number of lines
- a machine column too short for every meter hides the last ones whole and says how many
- the menu shows each pages badge the questions waiting and dockers state

### `tests/console/test_monitor_biggest.py`

Design 0012 §2, §4.2, §4.6 and §11 step 12: the Session view's Biggest in the context panel lists the largest items of the followed session's context, by the estimate: each with its tokens, its part's color, the step it came with (the flow's, by its call id) and what it is, marked when it is loop text, when a newer call made it one that could be freed, when OpenCode cleared it and when a compaction would keep it; narrower, a line cuts what the item is first, then its marks, then its step, never its tokens; Enter shows the item highlighted whole, with why it is marked. When rows are short, Biggest gives up rows with Tool calls (§2's room). Frames: the Session view with board A's parts at 128, 88 and 60 columns. The session and its request are synthetic.

- the biggest items are listed with their tokens their step and what they are
- an item the guide cleared says by you and what it freed
- a biggest line cuts what it is first then its marks then its step never its tokens
- enter on an item shows it whole with why it is marked
- enter on biggest opens the item highlighted
- the session view with the parts at 128 88 and 60 columns

### `tests/console/test_monitor_calls.py`

Design 0012 §2, §7 and §11 step 7: the Monitor's Tool calls panel lists the followed session's calls newest first, each with when it started, its tool, what it does and its result's size in characters; a failed one in the failed color, marked ✗; one running in the now color. Enter on a call reads it whole through the bridge, a `docker exec`, so only on Enter while the page is on screen, and shows it in a window: its input, its output (its end, past the bridge's cap) and its error; when the bridge cannot give it whole (an older sandbox image), the window shows what the flow keeps of it and why. Nothing reaches the bridge while the page is not on screen. A session followed with no call yet says so. When rows are short, Tool calls gives up rows first (design 0012 §2's room), so at 160 by 40 no panel is cut.

- tool calls are listed newest first with their time tool what they do and their size a failed one in red
- enter on a call reads it whole through the bridge once and only while the page is on screen
- a call the bridge cannot give whole shows what the flow keeps and why
- a session followed with no call yet says so in tool calls
- when rows are short tool calls give up rows so no panel is cut

### `tests/console/test_monitor_cloud.py`

Design 0016 §11 and §13: the Monitor with a cloud model, in the service's words. The heading line says what the followed session cost so far (its subagents' with it); the Context window's "On the GPU exactly" says it does not apply (the model runs at OpenRouter's provider); the Flow view says the flow's cost so far in its heading and in Models in this flow, and each box's cost when it is chosen.

- the heading says what the followed session cost so far
- on the gpu exactly does not apply to a cloud model
- the flow view says the flows cost and each boxs

### `tests/console/test_monitor_context.py`

Design 0012 §2, §4.1, §4.4 and §11 step 3, decision 0067: the Monitor's Context window panel shows the followed session's totals from the loaded slot's facts only: how much of the window its last finished step used, how much is left before OpenCode compacts, the share read from the cache (or "not reported by this engine"), the bar to the compaction line and the room kept for the answer, what sits on the GPU (under KVMem its exact window and the rest in system memory; under llama.cpp its whole window attended), whose conversation the engine holds and when another session took it, what a compaction keeps, where the figures come from (a model without calibration labeled so), and the slot's preset after a switch; no line wider than the panel. Design 0012 §4.5 (§11 step 7): under the GPU's line, a spark of the session's size at each step, as OpenCode counts it, marked where the loop guard stopped an answer (its cell in the failed color) and where it was compacted (in the attention color), each said after the spark. When the engine stopped, started again or was switched, the panel says it holds no conversation and that this session's tokens are read again; when the last step's answer did not report its size, it says so; when the totals cannot be read, it says why. Totals that did not change are not drawn again (design 0001 §5.7). Frames: the Session view at 128 columns with a session on KVMem. Design 0012 §4.2 (§11 step 12): with the parts estimated, the bar is drawn by part, each in its color, and a line says what each part holds, "estimate" first; narrower, it gives up its smallest parts first, saying how many.

- the panel says used left and the cache share from the totals
- the gpu line under kvmem and under llamacpp
- a figure not reported by this engine is said so and a model without calibration too
- the panel says whose conversation the engine holds and when another took it
- the panel says the engine holds no conversation once it stopped started again or was switched
- the panel says when the last steps answer did not report its size
- the panel says why the totals could not be read
- the panel says when the slot switched to another preset
- no line is wider than the panel
- the monitor shows the followed sessions totals in the context window panel
- totals that did not change are not drawn again
- the growth spark marks compactions and loop guard stops
- the growth spark fits the panel and says nothing before a step ended
- the bar is drawn by part and the legend says what each part holds
- the legend gives up its smallest parts first and no line is wider than the panel

### `tests/console/test_monitor_context_drop.py`

Design 0012 §4.6, §4.7, §9.7 and §11 step 12: `x` drops the highlighted item through the Guide's Drop (step 10), by the same path as the Session view's `x`: the Guide is asked about that call first (what it frees, what it costs, or why it cannot run), then its window asks, and only Enter in that window clears the result. In the Context view, Enter or `x` on a part of What is in it lists the part's items, and Enter on a row of Could be freed that drops (the loop text, the earlier results of calls made again) lists its items, each in a window where ↑↓ highlight one and `x` drops it; an item that is not a call's result says why and opens nothing. In the Session view, `x` with Biggest in the context focused drops the item it highlights. Neither the key that opens a window nor Enter in the window of items acts in it. The session and its request are synthetic.

- x on a part lists its items and x there drops the highlighted result once confirmed
- enter on a drop row of could be freed lists its items and x drops one
- x on an item that is not a calls result says why and opens nothing
- x with biggest focused drops the item it highlights
- esc in the window of items closes it quietly
- keys in the window of items never reach the page under it

### `tests/console/test_monitor_context_view.py`

Design 0012 §2, §4.2, §4.4, §4.5, §4.6, §4.7 and §11 step 12: the Monitor's Context view (board C), opened with Enter on the Session view's Context window panel. Its Context window: the totals, the bar by part and what each part holds, whose conversation the engine holds. What is in it: each part of the loaded model's (a template with no thinking has no thinking part) with its tokens, its share, its items, the biggest and its last ten steps, from the estimate; narrower, the biggest's words are cut first, then the last ten steps go, then the items, then the biggest, never a part's name, tokens or share; ↑↓ move through the parts and Enter lists a part's items, biggest first, and says how many more it holds. Where the room went: the biggest groups in words, with their tokens and share, the words cut first. Where these figures come from: the engine adapter's context facts (the window, the output limit, the exact window or none, whether it counts tokens), the model's catalog entry (its template's parts) and its calibration, or that it has none, and how the estimate is made. On the GPU exactly: under KVMem, its first block, the newest tokens (from where the cache stopped matching, or the last user message), what it picked and the rest in system memory, about, and that its picks are not reported; under an engine without an exact window, that it attends its whole window. Over this session: a chart of the session's size at each step against the window, each column split by part where the step's parts are known, the steps the loop guard stopped and the compactions marked under it and said. Could be freed: the loop text, the earlier results of calls made again and compacting now, each with about what it frees; Enter on Compact now asks first, as `k` does on the view, and Enter on the others lists their items and how they are dropped. Frames: the Context view with board A's parts at 128, 88 and 60 columns. Synthetic sessions.

- what is in it lists each part with its tokens share items biggest and last ten steps
- what is in it gives up the biggests words first then its trend then its items
- enter on a part lists its items biggest first
- enter on a part of what is in it opens its items
- where the room went names the biggest groups
- where these figures come from names the engines facts the template and the calibration
- the context view at 128 88 and 60 columns
- on the gpu exactly says the first block the newest the picked and the rest under kvmem
- on the gpu under an engine without an exact window says it attends its whole window
- the chart draws each steps size split by part and marks compactions and loop guard stops
- no chart line is wider than its panel
- could be freed says what each frees
- enter on compact now in could be freed asks first as k does
- could be freed leaves out a result already cleared

### `tests/console/test_monitor_flow.py`

Design 0012 §3, §6.1, §2 and §11 step 6: the Monitor follows the newest active root session of the console's folder (else the newest root session), reads its flow and its subagents' from the service, only what changed since the version it holds, and draws it in the Flow panel; the heading line names the session (OpenCode, the workspace, its title, its agent, what it does, its steps and calls, since when, that it is the newest), or says why none is followed. A flow that did not change is neither read again nor drawn (design 0001 §5.7); what changed keeps the flow whole after the service forgets old removals; one reading at a time; nothing is read while the page is not on screen (design 0012 §2: what asks Docker is read only while on screen). Frames: the Session view with board A's flow at 128, 88 and 60 columns.

- the newest active root session is followed else the newest root
- what changed since a version brings a flow up to date
- a flow kept up to date stays whole after the service forgets old removals
- one reading at a time a second one meanwhile asks nothing
- the monitor draws the followed sessions flow and names it in the heading
- the session view with board as flow at 128 88 and 60 columns
- a flow that did not change is not read again or drawn
- nothing is read while the page is not on screen
- with no session the heading says why

Design 0013 §9, §10, §12 and §14 step 6 (boards D2, D3 and D4): the Flow view's boards beside board D1, each shown in place of D1's panels, Esc going back to it. Board D4, proposals (`Enter` on a DECOMPOSE while its proposals wait, revised 2026-10-18 at the owner's comment: cards, not a list): the flow so far with the proposals dashed; the proposals as cards side by side in the order they will run, each with its state, its settings and its own answers; the highlighted card's whole prompt below them; how the model proposed them. Its decisions (accept, edit, reject and accept all) reach the service, and the cards say what became of each; at any width, no card touches another. Against a service served in the test, on runs written as the runner writes them.

- d4 shows each proposal as a card in the order they will run
- d4s decisions accept edit reject and accept all reach the service
- d4s cards never touch at any width
- d3 shows the gate what it shows the verifys report and the brief against the plan
- d3s answers approve send back with a note and stop answer its question

### `tests/console/test_monitor_flow_built.py`

Design 0013 §12 and §15 (decisions 0098, 0099 and 0100): a flow built with the model, in the Monitor's Flow view. The view follows it once built; Waiting for you says it was built with the model, to look, then press `r`, and lists the check's warnings of its last draft whole, wrapped, never cut; frames at 128 and 88 columns and below 70. `r` on a flow that has not run starts it at its first box, once confirmed. `n` opens a planning beside the console, never in its terminal, even in a workspace with no flow yet, and the view becomes the flow once the model's draft is written; `N` opens it in Clank's VS Code window, but over SSH nothing opens on the PC's own screen, the message saying how to attach. `clank console --page monitor --view flow` opens on the Flow view, for a terminal that keeps F10 for itself, and `n` and `r` act there. Everything runs against a service served in the test; nothing runs OpenCode.

- a built flow shows with its boxes its models and its warnings whole
- r starts a flow that has not run once confirmed

- the console opens on the flow view where n and r act

Design 0013 §12 and §14 step 6 (board D1), with design 0012 §2 (the page, its keys, its room, its redraws): the Monitor's Flow view shows the flow its folder follows, read from the service (`GET /flows/view`) once a second while the view is on screen: the heading names the flow, its workspace, its run and its start; Flow draws its boxes and lines, its note how many boxes are done, running and waiting; Selected says the box chosen (the running one until the arrows choose another) with a Live panel while it runs; Models in this flow, Waiting for you, The brief, checked, Deliverables and Ahead say theirs. Frames: board D1's moment at 128 and 88 columns and below 70 (the list). Without a flow, the view says why. Nothing redraws while nothing changes; a running box redraws at most four times a second, and only its lines. Enter on a running box opens the Session view on its session, and Esc comes back to the box; Enter on a finished box shows its deliverable, `h` the run log; `p` pauses the flow, `r` runs it again from the box once confirmed. Everything runs against a service served in the test, on runs written as the runner writes them.

Design 0012 §2, §9.1, §9.2, §9.3 and §9.6 (§11 step 9): the Monitor's Guide panel lists Stop now, a note and Compact now with their keys, what each does briefly and what it costs, and under an action that ran its last state, when and by whom; an action that cannot run now is drawn quiet with its reason. ### `tests/console/test_monitor_live.py`

Design 0012 §2 and §7 (§11 step 7): `l` on the Session view shows the live text, a panel under the Flow with the followed session's request as it is written (its thinking dimmed, its answer, its calls' arguments in the calls' color), from the gateway's live view of that session (`GET /gateway/live?session=`); `l` again hides it. The text is read from the offset already held, so each reading brings only what is new (one at a time, the next once the last one's answer is in, however slowly it comes), and a new request is read from its start; it is read only while it is shown: on the Session view, with the page on screen. Frames: the Session view at 128 columns with the live text.

### `tests/console/test_monitor_undo_and_drop.py`

Design 0012 §2, §9.1, §9.5 and §9.7 (§11 step 10): the Monitor's Guide panel lists Undo (Undo turn, or Redo while a revert is pending) and Drop with their keys, what each does briefly, and under one that ran its state (undone, the turn undone, final, redone; clearing, cleared). On the Session view `u` asks first, its window the preview (the steps, the files and the owner's messages it takes back, and why the step alone is refused when the turn is offered) and the exact call; Enter undoes, sending the revert the window showed, and after the turn is undone the note window opens with its text; `u` while a revert is pending redoes. `x` drops the call under the flow's cursor, or the call highlighted in Tool calls when that panel has the focus: its window asks first, says what it frees and costs and that the drop is final; one that cannot run says why on the message line. They are the Session view's keys: on the Flow view they open nothing of the Guide's. An undo is sent with the revert and what it takes back as its window showed them, the words it gives back open whole in a window of several lines (Esc keeps them for `m`), and a warning that OpenCode took back more is shown in a window; `?` says an action's last state whole.

- the panel lists undo and drop with their keys and states
- u asks first then undoes and the turns text opens in the note window
- the guides question mark says the reason of an actions last state

- u and x do nothing of the guides on the flow view

### `tests/console/test_monitor_watch_for.py`

Design 0012 §2 and §8 (decision 0053): the Monitor's Watch for panel lists the newest marks the rules left, each with its rule, its session and why, a mark not seen yet in the attention color; Enter on the panel opens the marks it lists (§2's keys: Enter opens a mark), each whole, and so they are seen. Beside "Monitor" in the menu, the count of marks not seen yet, gone once they are seen, from the console or elsewhere (the event stream says so). Until the page follows a session's marks (design 0012 §3), the panel lists the marks of every session; with none, it says what it said before ("no session yet").

- the watch for panel lists the newest marks each with why
- the menu shows how many marks are not seen beside monitor

### `tests/console/test_one_door.py`

Design 0011 §12.3 and §12.7 (pressing a button; a button that cannot act now): a key typed and its button, pressed by Enter, Space or a click, reach the same `press` of the page, through the console's one door; a key that acts anywhere runs its binding's action; a key the page says cannot act now says why on the message line, typed or pressed, and the page's `press` is not called; a key of several says the keys it stands for; every group that shows a key shows it pressed when it is pressed, by its key too. A digit opens its page while the focus is not in the page, and is the page's only once the focus is in it (design 0001 §5.1).

- a key typed and its button reach the same press of the page
- a key that acts anywhere runs its bindings action
- a key that cannot act now says why typed or pressed and the page is not pressed
- a key of several says the keys it stands for
- every group that shows a key shows it pressed when its key is typed
- a digit opens its page until the focus is in the page that takes it

### `tests/console/test_orchestrator_panel.py`

Design 0013 §16: the Flow view's Orchestrator panel and its key. The panel says the orchestrator's role (steering or watching) and the model it runs on, and its last word for the owner; `O` turns it on steering, then watching, then off, each press saying what it does now. Design 0013 §12 (decision 0131): the Thinking panel says what the running box does now, a tool at work with its command and output, and the model's text as it writes.

- the panel says its role and the model it runs on
- the panel shows its last word for the owner
- o turns it on steering then watching then off
- thinking says what the box does now a tool running with its command output and the models last words
- while the model writes the live text is shown and while idle it says so
- a command cut short by the watch is still shown line by line

### `tests/console/test_pages.py`

Design 0001 §4.8, §5.6, design 0002 §2.1, §2.4 and §3.2: the console's pages beyond home. History shows every event the console holds, newest last, and filters by what is typed after `/`. Docker's page (the workspaces component's) shows Docker's state and versions and each container with whose it is; `s` asks Docker to stop. The machine's page (the machine component's) shows the profile and the doctor's checks with what to do; `d` runs the doctor. The operations page lists the newest operations; the highlighted one shows its steps and its log, and `c` cancels it. Components declare their pages, which join the menu with their keys, whatever status tag a page draws beside its name and whenever it comes. Design 0016 §15: a cloud preset says its provider and its prices on the Presets page and in the picker, and its model on the Models page (no GPU memory of this computer's); a switch to it from the picker is the switch route, as for any preset.

- components pages join the menu with their keys
- history shows every event newest last and filters by what is typed
- the history filter shows on slash stays while it filters and hides when left empty
- dockers page shows each container with whose it is and s asks to stop
- the machines page shows the profile and the doctors checks once d ran it
- operations show their steps and log and c cancels the highlighted one
- the picker says a cloud presets provider and prices
- a cloud preset says its provider and prices on presets and its model on models
- a switch to a cloud preset from the picker is the switch route
- a cloud presets explanation says its provider

### `tests/console/test_pages_fit.py`

Design 0011 §4, §7 and §11 (the owner's request of 2026-10-29: visual tests that catch what a person sees as wrong, after a window opened in the console's top left corner): every page of the console, opened at 80, 120 and 170 columns, fits the screen across: each of its panels starts at or after the screen's left edge and ends at or before its right edge, and no two of its panels cover each other (one inside the other is a panel in a panel, and allowed). A page that scrolls may run below the screen; across, nothing may.

- every page fits the screen across and its panels never cover each other

### `tests/console/test_panel.py`

Design 0011 §4 (the panel): every section is a panel, a box with its title set in its top border at the left, bold, and its note at the right of that border, drawn again when a page changes it, and dropped whole, never cut, before the title goes when the panel is narrow; a panel is never narrower than its title; the panel that holds the focus, and only it, draws its border in `border-focused`, and Tab goes from panel to panel in reading order; panels in a row share the row's height and stack when the row is narrow, or at once when what they hold asks for more room than the row has (design 0011 §9), standing side by side again when it asks for less; panels in a grid stand in columns of set widths, one beside the several it spans (design 0012 §2: the Monitor's Flow beside Now, Watch for and the Guide), their borders lined up with the row below, and stack in the order given when the grid is narrow; a style's `round` border draws round corners; moving the focus costs the console no redraw of its own (design 0001 §5.7).

- a panel has four borders its title in the top one and its note at its right
- a narrow panel drops its note and keeps its title whole
- a note set or changed after the panel is drawn shows in its border
- a panel is never narrower than its title
- only the panel that holds the focus draws its border in border focused
- tab goes from panel to panel in reading order
- panels in a row share its height and stack when it is narrow
- a row whose content asks for more room stacks at once and stands side by side again after
- a style with round borders draws round corners
- the title takes the styles title color and the border its own
- a tall panel of a grid stands beside the panels it spans its borders lined up with the row below
- a narrow grid stacks its panels in the order given

### `tests/console/test_panels_everywhere.py`

Design 0011 §2, §4, §7, §8, §9 and §11 (R2): every section of every page is a panel, and nothing of a page sits outside one (the hint lines that ended some pages are the keys bar's now, design 0011 §7); every page of the console, the shell's, each component's and the planned ones, draws the panels it declares (`PANELS`), in that order, each a box with four borders and its title whole in its own top border, at 80, 120 and 170 columns, Requests' live view too (shown by `v`), each view of a page with views (the Monitor, design 0012 §2), which keeps its row of tabs and one heading line outside its panels (decision 0043), and each screen of a page shown one screen at a time (Add a model, design 0016 §14); with the machine column in a narrow window (F5 at 80 and 100 columns), a page narrower than one of its panels scrolls sideways to all of it rather than cut its title, in each of its views; a list in a panel is drawn on the panel's background, not on `surface` (a button's or a choice's), whether it has the focus or not; the phrases the console's tests look for are still found at 80 columns.

- each page draws its declared panels in order each a box with its title whole and nothing outside them
- with the machine column in a narrow window each page scrolls sideways to the whole of its panels
- a panel wider than its page by no more than the vertical scroll bar is reached sideways too
- a list in a panel is drawn on the panels background whether it has the focus or not
- the phrases the tests look for are found at 80 columns

### `tests/console/test_preset_change.py`

Design 0016 §7 and §15, and design 0003 §8.2 (revised 2026-10-29, the owner's request): `e` on the Presets page changes the highlighted cloud preset's settings in a form filled with them, the model's limits in its labels as the service words them; only what was changed is sent, and the service's answer (what changed, where it applies, and, while the main slot runs it, that OpenCode windows open now keep the limits they started with) shows in a window, a refusal on the message line in the service's words, a number that is not one in the console's. On a local preset, `e` says where its options change instead. The form leaves Esc and Backspace to the window: Esc leaves it, sending nothing, and Backspace erases in its fields. The keys bar and the page's explanation list the key.

- e opens a form of its settings with the models limits and sends only what changed
- a refusal is said in the services words and a number that is not one in the consoles
- esc leaves the form sending nothing and backspace erases in its fields
- on a local preset e says where its options change
- the keys bar and the pages explanation list e

### `tests/console/test_providers_cloud.py`

Design 0016 §15: the Providers and models page's Cloud panel. With a provider: its title, its agreement, its key (its last four and its label, never the rest), its data policy, OpenRouter's own count for the key, Clank's spending today and this month against the limits, its last check and its presets, in the service's words; without one, how to add one. Each key calls its route: `a` Add a model at Cloud, `k` the key again in a hidden field, `c` the check (its items in a window), `o` the data policy (asked first), `L` the limits (a form, sent as typed), `r` remove (asked first; refused while in use, in the service's words).

- the cloud panel with a provider
- the cloud panel without a provider says how to add one
- k sets the key again in a hidden field
- c checks and shows each item
- o changes the data policy asked first
- capital l sets the limits in a form
- r removes asked first and while in use says why not

### `tests/console/test_questions.py`

Design 0002 §2.5 and design 0011 §12.1: the questions page lists every question waiting for the user, each with its detail, its options and who asked it when; the highlighted one is answered with an option's key, as you (console). Each question's options are buttons, clicked: a click on another question's option highlights it and answers it, the list keeping the focus.

- the questions page lists every waiting question and answers the highlighted one
- a click on another questions option highlights it and answers it the list keeping the focus

### `tests/console/test_recording.py`

Design 0011 §13 (decision 0126): `clank console --record FOLDER` keeps what the console draws, as the terminal gets it, in asciicast files: a header with the terminal's size, then each write with its time, a resize kept as one; each part begins on a clear screen with the whole screen drawn, and a new part begins once one has run its time. A view the console does not have (`--view flow.`) is refused, never opened as another.

- the console keeps each write to the terminal and each resize
- a recording is kept in parts each beginning on a clear screen
- a view the console does not have is refused

### `tests/console/test_records.py`

Design 0002 §2.2 and design 0001 §5.4: the records page lists every kind of record (settings and state), the records of the chosen kind and the chosen record's values, each with its field's title. `?` explains what has the focus: a value (what it is, its value, where it comes from, the file that holds it or would, when a change applies, where its kind is defined), a planned menu entry (its milestone and design section), or the page itself. The command palette (Ctrl+P) finds every page and action by name and runs it.

- the records page lists the kinds then a kinds records then a records values
- question mark on a value explains it and where it is kept
- question mark on a planned menu entry names its milestone and design
- the command palette finds pages and actions by name and runs them

### `tests/console/test_requests_page.py`

Design 0001 §6 (the Requests page, 2026-10-15): F7 shows the model requests the gateway kept, newest first, a line each (when, who, status, tokens in and how much was cached, out, first token, total, speed), and the highlighted one whole: where it went, what was asked and what was answered. `v` shows the newest request live as the model writes it: what it does now, what was asked, and its text as it comes (asking only for what is new), following the next request once it ended. Design 0004 §2: a request the loop guard stopped is marked so. Design 0012 §4.1 (decision 0090): so is one whose engine refused it because the context was full, which OpenCode was told so that it compacts. Design 0011 §4 and §8 (board E16): how many requests are listed is the note in the top border of the list's panel; "No request kept yet." shows in that panel only while there is none; the live view is one panel, which `v` shows in place of the other two, and `v` again brings them back. Design 0016 §11: each request's cost ends its line, in the service's words, an estimate marked ≈; the highlighted one's details say the cost, how it was known, and its tokens in, cached, out and thinking. Design 0003 §4.1: the highlighted request says the sampling it sent of its own beside its preset's (the values the preset sets, else the engine's own defaults in words), none sent with the preset's in effect, or that it is not known when the gateway kept no copy of the request.

- f7 lists the requests newest first and shows the highlighted one whole
- with no request kept the lists panel says so and has no note
- one request kept is counted in the singular
- a request resent after its engine broke off says so
- a request the loop guard stopped says so
- a request refused because the context was full says so
- v follows the newest request live as the model writes it
- each requests cost shows in the services words an estimate marked and its details say how
- the sampling a request sent is shown beside its presets
- f7 shows the sampling a request sent beside its presets
- n shows the agents internet exchanges through the proxy and the hosts allowed

### `tests/console/test_secret_field.py`

Design 0016 §4 and §14: the hidden field a key is typed or pasted into. It draws dots as the key comes, then `…` and its last four characters; the frame's text (and so a snapshot) and the console's log hold no other part of it; nothing copies it to the clipboard; Esc clears it, and taking it to send clears it too. A key's field in a form gives the key to the page that asked, and only once. Textual's own log (TEXTUAL_LOG) never holds it either: what is typed or pasted into the field never reaches a logger, and the worker that sends it is described, so its repr is not the call holding it; and `clank console` turns Textual's log file and devtools off.

- a key draws as dots then its last four
- a pasted key shows as dots and its last four and nothing else of it
- typed keys stay in the field and never reach the consoles own keys
- esc clears the field and gives nothing
- textuals own log holds no key typed pasted or sent
- clank console turns textuals log file and devtools off

### `tests/console/test_setup_page.py`

Design 0028 §4 and decision 0148: the console's Setup page shows `clank setup`'s checklist, the service's: each item with its mark and what it is now, and under an item not done what to do, with the key that does it here (`m` Add a model, `f` This folder). Under the items, "Next:" and the key of the first required item a key can do; once everything required is done, "Ready:", what was set up and what each thing is for, and `f`. `r` reads the checklist again. With no page named, the console opens on Setup while anything required is missing, and as it did before once everything is done. The page has no key of its own: it is the menu's last page. It fits at 80 columns.

- each item shows what it is now and what to do with the key that does it
- the setup page fits at 80 columns
- with no page named the console opens on setup while something required is missing
- once everything required is done it opens on this folder and setup says ready
- m opens add a model and f opens this folder
- r reads the checklist again
- the page has no key of its own and is the menus last page

### `tests/console/test_setup_page_fixes.py`

Design 0028 §4 and §5: the Setup page does the next step on one key. `s` starts Docker Desktop through its own command, the service's start; `t` runs the command shown, one that needs administrator rights, in this terminal, asked first in a window that names it: the console steps aside for it (headless, it runs at once), sudo asks for the password itself, and the console says how it ended and what the person does next; Esc in the window runs nothing. `b` builds the sandbox image in the background, asked first (S4). A key that has nothing to do says why: `s` with Docker Engine stopped, that Clank never starts it, and the command `t` runs. `t` runs only a command the checklist builds, whoever gave it: another is refused, its words naming it.

- s starts docker desktop through its own start
- t asks first naming the command then runs it in this terminal
- esc in the window runs nothing
- after joining the docker group the console says to log out and in once
- a key with nothing to do says why
- s with docker engine stopped says clank never starts it and names ts command
- b builds the sandbox image in the background asked first
- t never runs a command the checklist does not build

### `tests/console/test_shell.py`

Design 0001 §5.1, §5.5, §5.7, design 0002 §2.15, §3.2 and design 0011 §7: the console's shell. Its menu lists every page it has, each with its key (the F-keys by their number, F10 after F9: design 0012 §2), then every page a later milestone brings, in red, each with its milestone; a planned entry opens a red page that says which milestone builds it and what it will do (the owner's request, 2026-10-11); a highlight overtaken by a newer one before it was heard never takes the menu back. The header shows the service's uptime, Docker's state and the questions waiting, or that the service is not running. The style sheet in CLANK_HOME is loaded after Clank's; animations are off; a burst of events is drawn in a few redraws, at most four a second, and nothing is lost. In the style's look (design 0011 §7): the header is one row on `bars`, the folder's name in `name` at normal weight on every page ("◆ CLANK" alone bold), each pill whole in its state's color and the clock in `quiet`; the menu's keys are in `key`, its highlighted entry on `selection` in `selected-text`, bold, whether the menu has the focus or not, and "coming" in `quiet`; the message line stands above the keys bar, in `quiet`, an error in `attention`, bold. Design 0028 §4 and decision 0148: while anything the setup needs is missing, a bare `clank` and `clank console` open on Setup in place of This folder or Home, the header saying "no model: Add a model" when no model is usable; design 0016 §14 and decision 0105: with the setup done and no model usable, they open on Add a model; a page named on the command line opens as asked, the header still saying so; with a model ready and the setup done, the console opens as it always did. A page without a key of its own (Setup) is listed after those with one.

- the menu lists every page with its key then every planned page in red with its milestone
- the menu sorts its f keys by their number so f10 follows f9
- a planned entry opens a red page naming its milestone and what it will do
- a menu highlight overtaken by a newer one never takes the menu back
- the header shows the services uptime dockers state and the questions waiting
- the header says when the service is not running
- a style sheet in clank home is loaded after clanks
- animations are off
- home at 120 and at 80 columns
- a burst of events is drawn in a few redraws and nothing is lost
- the page on screen is found without searching the whole console
- the header is one row on bars naming the folder in name not bold on every page
- each pill is drawn whole in its states color and the clock in quiet
- the menus keys are in key and its highlighted entry on selection in selected text bold
- the message line stands above the keys bar in quiet and an error in attention bold
- with something the setup needs missing clank opens on setup the header saying no model
- with the setup done and no model usable clank opens on add a model
- with a model ready clank opens on this folder
- a page named on the command line opens as asked the header saying no model
- a bare clank and clank console open the guide and a page named does not
- with docker desktop only stopped after a restart clank opens on this folder which starts it

### `tests/console/test_side_work_panel.py`

Design 0013 §17 (decision 0132): the Flow view shows a box's job and the run's side work. A box waiting on its job says so on its tag line (`job 1`); Selected says the job (its title, how long it has run of its minutes, or how it ended), its command and its log; the Side work panel lists each side task with its state, its time and tokens, its note and why it failed or was dropped; and the session the view follows (Thinking, Tool calls, the context window) is a side task's while one works, else the box's.

- a box waiting on its job says so on its tag line and selected says the job
- the side work panel lists each task with its state time tokens note and why
- the view follows a side tasks session while one works else the boxs

### `tests/console/test_terminal.py`

Design 0004 §1: handing the console's terminal to a program. The console steps aside, the program runs attached to the terminal, the keys it left unread are dropped, and the console comes back; a program that cannot start, or one interrupted, still gives the console back, saying so; headless (tests, snapshots) the program runs without stepping aside; a terminal resized meanwhile tells the console its new size; and Ctrl+C belongs to the program while it runs.

- the console steps aside runs the program drops its unread keys and comes back
- a program that cannot start or is interrupted still gives the console back
- headless the program runs without stepping aside
- a terminal resized while the program ran tells the console its size
- a program runs attached with ctrl c its own and the console s handler back after

### `tests/console/test_text.py`

Design 0002 §3.2: the console shows a state in color: green when it is as wanted, yellow while it changes, red when it is not; text is shown as it is, never read as markup; a state that appears more than once is colored each time, in order. Design 0016 §11: a cost is shown in US dollars, cents from a dollar up, two significant digits below, an estimate marked ≈; a model's window in thousands of tokens.

- a state is green when as wanted yellow while it changes and red when not
- each mark is colored where it next appears and the text is never read as markup
- money is cents from a dollar up and two significant digits below an estimate marked
- a window of tokens is said in thousands

### `tests/console/test_windows.py`

Design 0011 §4, §7, §11 and §12.1 (R2; every key a button): the windows over the console, the details window (`?`, `v`), the picker, the form, an MCP server's tools and a device's traffic, are each one panel (the traffic window two: its list and the highlighted exchange), their title in the top border, no longer a bold first line; a window's border is `border-focused`, since it has the focus (in the traffic window, the border of the panel that holds it), on the page's `background`; its keys ("Esc closes this") stay its last line, in `quiet`, the details window's a button, `esc close`, which has the focus when it opens. A window is never wider than the screen allows: a title too long for it is cut in the border with "…" and shown whole as the window's first line (design 0011 §4 and §5, revised after approval with decision 0081: nothing cut is lost).

- a window is one panel its title in its border its border focused its keys its last line
- the traffic window is two panels its list and the highlighted exchange
- a window title too long for the screen is cut in its border and shown whole inside

### `tests/console/test_windows_placed.py`

Design 0011 §7 (the owner's report of 2026-10-29: the workspace's cloud question opened in the console's top left corner, not over its middle): every window over the console opens centered and whole on the screen. Every kind of window Clank has is found by walking its modules for ModalScreen's subclasses, so a new kind cannot be left out of this test: each is opened over the console at 60, 100 and 160 columns, and its box (the panels it shows) must lie inside the screen, with as much room on its left as on its right and above as below, within a cell. The workspace's cloud question, as This folder asks it before the agent starts, is a frame too.

- every kind of window clank has is opened here
- a window opens centered and whole on the screen
- the workspaces cloud question opens over the middle of the console

### `tests/console/test_workspaces_page.py`

Design 0004 §3, §10 (the Workspaces page, 2026-10-15): F6 lists every workspace with its container's state, its software and its folder; the highlighted one shows its container and limits, its use now (from the meters), its software and the MCP servers its agent gets; `x` stops its container. Design 0011 §4 and §8 (board E15): how many workspaces there are and run is the note in the top border of the list's panel, drawn again when it changes, and the highlighted workspace's name the note of its; "No workspace yet" shows in the list's panel only while there is none. Design 0004 §3 (revised 2026-10-26): a workspace whose running container is cut off from the model (the edge not running on its network) says `cut off` in place of its state, in the failed color, and the highlighted one says what is wrong and what Clank does about it, in the service's words.

- f6 lists the workspaces and shows the highlighted one whole
- with no workspace the lists panel says so and has no note
- a workspaces detail says its use and which servers its agent gets
- a workspace cut off from the model says so in its line and its detail

### `tests/contract/test_agent_program.py`

Design 0004 §4 and decision 0011: every agent program adapter meets the same contract. It says the command its program runs in a workspace's container and the environment it runs with (plain names), and writes, for a model reached at a URL, a configuration that names that URL, that model and its limits, kept in the container's home (never in the user's folder); and what it supports is declared.

- its command and environment are well formed
- its configuration names the model and lives in the containers home

### `tests/contract/test_container_platform.py`

Design 0002 §2.10 and design 0001 §8.2-8.3: every container platform adapter meets the same contract, run against what its program answered at each recorded version, as it ran where it was recorded (that platform, its command where it was installed): running, it reports its version, its engine's and its containers, with Clank's labels read exactly and every label's name; stopped, no containers, read within a few seconds although the stopped program leaves some questions unanswered; a start and a stop are asked of the program and change its state, even when the program's own start does nothing; without its command it is not installed and refuses to start; what it supports is read from the program. Design 0004 §3 (revised 2026-10-26): each container's networks are read by name from the same listing, a stopped one's too, so the edge's place on every workspace's network costs no other question. Design 0014 §2: an adapter that asks for a command's whole answer gets it as recorded, with its errors; and a platform Clank does not start (a system service, whose recording has no transitions: Docker Engine) refuses a start and a stop with the command its owner runs, having run nothing, and its capabilities say so. Design 0028 §5: how it is set up is read from the program, and only a platform Clank does not start names the commands its owner runs, each with administrator rights; one Clank starts asks nothing for it.

- running it reports its version its engine and its containers
- clanks labels are read exactly and every labels name is listed
- each containers networks are read by name a stopped ones too
- stopped it reports no containers within a few seconds
- a start and a stop are asked of the program and change its state
- without its command it is not installed and refuses to start
- what it supports is read from the program
- a platform clank does not start refuses with the command to run
- how it is set up is read from the program and only one clank does not start names its owners commands

### `tests/contract/test_endpoint.py`

Design 0016 §18 (design 0015 §14): every endpoint adapter meets the same contract, run against every HTTP recording of its provider (`tests/fixtures/<adapter>/<date>/http.json`), the documented shapes first and the owner's recordings after: it reads the recorded list of models, the key's account, a whole answer's and a stream's cost, and each refusal into words, without an error; and the fake that stands in for the provider in every other test answers each recorded request as recorded.

- every endpoint adapter has a recording
- it reads the recorded list of models and the keys account
- it reads a whole answers and a streams cost from their usage
- it reads each recorded refusal into words
- the fake answers each recorded request as recorded

### `tests/contract/test_engine.py`

Design 0001 §8.2-8.3, design 0003 §4 and decision 0010: every engine adapter meets the same contract, run against what its engine printed at each recorded version: its options are well formed (each key once, each default valid, each choice listed), every flag they map to is in the engine's own `--help`, its startup log says when it is ready and what memory it took, and what its version supports is declared.

- its options are well formed
- every flag its options map to is in its recorded help
- its recorded startup says when it is ready and what memory it took
- what its version supports is declared

### `tests/contract/test_host.py`

Design 0003 §2 and decision 0030: every host meets the same contract: a process it starts is alive by its identity until it is stopped, stops once, and is gone after; it reads a file's facts; and it runs a step (of an install) to its end in its folder, with its environment, each line of its output given as it comes, and its exit status. A host whose program is the running system itself runs the contract live; any other runs it against what its program answered at each recorded version, with the inputs the recording was made with.

- a started process is alive by its identity until it stops once
- it reads a files facts
- a step runs to its end in its folder with its environment each line as it comes

### `tests/docker/test_agent_files_live.py`

Design 0018 §7.5, on this machine's Docker when asked (`-m docker`): starting an agent in a real workspace's container writes Clank's current MCP server and instructions into the agent's home there, and that server, run as OpenCode runs it, lists `show_files` and `find_figures`, whatever image the container was made from.

- starting an agent gives it this clanks tools

### `tests/docker/test_board_layer.py`

Design 0022 §6 and §7, run in the TeX layer's image on this machine's Docker (`-m docker`; skipped when the image is not built): a pgfplots drawing on a board is compiled by pdfLaTeX and made SVG by `pdftocairo`, its glyphs flattened and its size its own; and a frame exports as a vector PDF made by librsvg, the figure's lines and the LaTeX as paths, not pixels.

- a drawing is compiled and a frame exports as a vector pdf

### `tests/docker/test_cloud_live.py`

Design 0014 §3, §10 and design 0016 §8, on Docker Engine when asked (`-m docker`): the owner's first test on Linux, from the agent's side. OpenCode, as Clank configures it in a workspace's container, asks its model through the edge with its workspace's key; the edge forwards to the gateway's socket, in the folder it mounts read-only; the gateway, made and served as its process makes and serves it on Linux (its TCP socket and its Unix socket, the routes the service gives it followed as it follows them, the provider's key held for the launch), forwards the request to OpenRouter (the fake, playing its recording) with the owner's key and the listed model, and OpenCode prints the answer that came back. Nothing in it is a stand-in but OpenRouter: the image, the edge, the gateway's application and OpenCode are Clank's.

- an agent in its container gets its answer from a cloud model through the edge and the gateway

### `tests/docker/test_escape.py`

Design 0004 §3 and §8, design 0002 §3.3 (M4's escape tests), on this machine's Docker when asked (`-m docker`): a workspace's container sees its folder and nothing else of the machine (its mounts, a subprocess, a symlink that points out of the folder); from inside it nothing answers but the edge: not the internet (by address or by name, IPv4 or IPv6), not the machine, not another workspace; the edge answers, forwarding to the gateway's port; and the container runs as the image's user, with no capability, within its limits. Design 0014 §3 and §4, on Linux: the gateway's stand-in listens where the gateway does on each platform (on Docker Engine on a socket, in a short folder of its own that the test's `config/gateway.json` names; elsewhere on 127.0.0.1); on Docker Engine the edge reaches it through that socket, mounting nothing but its folder (read-only) and Clank's programs, as the owner; and the host's own address on a workspace's network answers nothing (the probe listens on that address only, never on every address).

- a container sees its folder and nothing else of the machine
- from inside nothing answers but the edge
- a container runs within its limits
- the edge reaches the gateway on linux
- on linux the hosts own address on a workspaces network answers nothing

### `tests/docker/test_latex_layer.py`

Design 0020 §8-§10, run in the TeX layer's image on this machine's Docker (`-m docker`; skipped when the image is not built): a build of a changed document answers its log and changes nothing in the folder, though the folder holds the owner's own `.aux` and PDF of that document; latexdiff's marked-up PDF is kept under `.clank/`; a PDF's pages become images with their words in boxes; and SyncTeX gives back the line a point of a page comes from.

- a build of a change answers its log and writes nothing in the folder
- a changes marked up pdf is kept under clank
- a pdfs pages and words and synctex give back the line of a point
- latex workshops build keeps the pdf log and synctex beside the document with this machines paths

### `tests/docker/test_latex_live.py`

Design 0020 §3, §5, §6, §7, §8, §9 and §10, end to end, on this machine's Docker when asked (`-m docker`): a workspace opened with the TeX layer (its image built when missing), then, through the service's own parts and the workspace's real container: the checks on real changes to a document (a clean change passes and its build is as clean as before; an invented citation, a value without a source, an undefined reference and a change that breaks the build are refused, each with its reason; a new bibliography entry is checked against Crossref's record, a wrong year refused), the change's PDF marked by latexdiff, the document's pages rendered with their words and a mark said with its source line, and LaTeX Workshop's build keeping the PDF beside the document.

- the latex checks marked pdf pages and build run for real

### `tests/docker/test_setup_live.py`

Design 0028 §4 and §5, on this machine's Docker when asked (`-m docker`): `clank setup --check` reads this computer as it is, through the platform the settings choose and without the service, as on a new computer right after the installer: Docker installed, running and usable by this account are done, Clank's service is to start, the model and the folder are not known yet; on Docker Engine, whether systemd starts it with the computer is said, and every command it offers its owner is one a client runs. Reading it changes nothing: no container, network, volume or image is made or removed.

- clank setup check reads this computers docker as it is and changes nothing

### `tests/fakes/test_recorded.py`

Design 0001 §8.3: adapters are tested against what the real program answered at its pinned version. The recorded program answers each command as recorded for its current state, moves between states on the recorded commands, and fails on any command that was never recorded, so an adapter that starts asking something new is caught. It keeps time as the real program did: an answer that never came, or came later than the caller waits, is lost, the caller having waited its whole time limit; an application it launches answers by its name; and it says on which platform it was recorded and where its command was. Design 0014 §2: a recorded answer keeps its errors too, which a caller that asks for the whole answer (as `run_answer` gives it) receives; the shorter forms of an answer read as before.

- the recorded program answers by state and moves on its transitions
- it can start in another state
- a command never recorded fails the test
- an answer that never came keeps the caller waiting its whole time limit
- an answer later than the time limit is lost and changes nothing
- an application it launches answers by its name and moves it on
- a recorded answer gives its errors to a caller that asks for the whole answer

### `tests/kernel/test_accepting.py`

Design 0002 §1: the service keeps answering.
